affine image of half-plane SLE$_6$ at its smooth starting boundary point.
It does not construct a source-dependent domain Markov family. In particular,
it does not establish that the metric can be recovered from the unlabelled
boundary germ at a later random exploration tip. Those distinctions are
important when assessing whether target consistency alone could repair the
classification conjecture.

\subsection{State space and the meaning of a trace germ}

We retain the chronological order of a path but forget its original clock.
For the half-plane curves used here, the usual half-plane-capacity clock
provides a canonical representative: the filled hull at time $t$ has
hydrodynamic map $g_t(z)=z+2t/z+o(1/z)$.
This clock is determined by the ordered unparameterized trace. Section~\ref{sec:loewner-admissible} justifies continuous Loewner driving of affine images; hence they admit this clock.
The clock has infinite total lifetime almost surely: its total half-plane
capacity $T$ is positive, while spatial scale invariance makes its law equal
to that of $r^2T$ for every $r>0$; the only such probability laws on
$[0,\infty]$ are supported on $\{0,\infty\}$. Thus $T=\infty$.
All functions below are functions of the ordered unparameterized trace.

Use the standard Borel space of these capacity-parameterized continuous
curves, with the sigma-field generated by restrictions to finite time
intervals. Equivalently, this can be regarded as a Borel realization of the
usual ordered unparameterized path space. For $t>0$, let
\[
 \mathcal F_t=\sigma\{\gamma(s):0\leq s\leq t\},\qquad
 \mathcal F_{0+}=\bigcap_{t>0}\mathcal F_t.
\]
The latter is the initial trace-germ sigma-field. It is unaffected by changing
any part of the curve after a positive capacity time. The canonical time is
used only to give an explicit measurable coding of this germ.

For $r>0$, let $S_r\gamma$ be the spatially dilated trace, again put in the
canonical clock. Capacity scaling gives the exact identity
\[
 (S_r\gamma)(t)=r\gamma(t/r^2).
 \tag{SG1}
\]
In particular $S_r$ is invertible with inverse $S_{1/r}$.

\subsection{Dilation ergodicity}

\begin{lemma}[Brownian scaling is mixing]
\label{lem:sg-brownianmix}
For a one-sided Brownian motion $B=(B_t)_{t\geq0}$ and any fixed $r>1$, the
measure-preserving transformation
\[
 (\mathcal S_rB)_t=rB_{t/r^2}
\]
is mixing and hence ergodic.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set $Z_s=e^{-s/2}B_{e^s}$ for $s\in\mathbb R$.
This is a stationary centered Gaussian process with
\[
 \mathbb E[Z_sZ_u]=e^{-|s-u|/2}.
\]
It is the stationary Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process. Under $\mathcal S_r$, it
becomes $Z_{s-2\log r}$. Covariances between finite collections of coordinates
separated by $n(2\log r)$ tend to zero, so the corresponding jointly Gaussian
vectors become independent in the limit. Approximation by finite-coordinate
cylinder functions gives mixing on the full path sigma-field.
The correspondence is invertible almost surely:
$B_t=\sqrt t\,Z_{\log t}$ for $t>0$, with $B_0=0$. Thus mixing transfers back
to Brownian scaling. The two-sided log-time process here is obtained from a
one-sided Brownian path at times $t>0$; no independent Brownian past is being
added.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Affine SLE$_6$ is dilation mixing]
\label{lem:sg-affinemix}
Let $A$ be any invertible linear map carrying $\mathbb H$ onto itself and
fixing its positive boundary orientation. Define
\[
 \mu_A=\operatorname{Law}\bigl(A^{-1}\eta\bigr),\qquad
 \eta\sim\operatorname{SLE}_6(\mathbb H,0,\infty),
\]
with the physical half-plane-capacity representative understood.
For every fixed $r>1$, $S_r$ preserves $\mu_A$ and is mixing under $\mu_A$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The SLE trace is a measurable, scaling-equivariant function of its Brownian
Loewner driver $\sqrt6B$. The trace exists almost surely. Therefore its
dilation action is a factor of the Brownian scaling action and is mixing
by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-brownianmix}. The map on ordered traces
$\eta\mapsto A^{-1}\eta$ commutes with scalar spatial dilations, and is an
invertible measurable map after recoding by capacity. Mixing and invariance
therefore transfer to $\mu_A$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Separating distinct metrics from arbitrarily short initial segments}

Normalize the maps preserving $\mathbb H$ by
\[
 A_{b,c}=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\0&c\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad b\in\mathbb R,\ c>0.
 \tag{SG2}
\]
This parametrizes all constant positive-definite conformal metrics, up to a
positive scalar, in a fixed half-plane frame: the tensor is
$A_{b,c}^{\mathsf T}A_{b,c}$ with its $(1,1)$ entry normalized to $1$.
The laws $\mu_{A_{b,c}}$ are distinct. Indeed, the strictly increasing SLE$_6$
left-passage function $F$ gives
\[
 \mu_{A_{b,c}}[L(x+iy)]=F\left(\frac{x+by}{cy}\right).
 \tag{SG3}
\]
Equality of the probabilities at $z=i$ and $z=1+i$ first identifies $b/c$
and then $1/c$, hence both parameters.

\begin{lemma}[Unit-time restrictions distinguish the metrics]
\label{lem:sg-unitrestriction}
If $A\ne A'$ are two normalized maps in (SG2), the laws of their traces
restricted to capacity time $[0,1]$ are distinct.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If the restrictions agreed, dilation invariance and (SG1) would make the
restrictions to $[0,T]$ agree for every $T>0$. Those restrictions generate the
full path sigma-field, so the entire laws would agree, contrary to (SG3).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Metric decoding from an initial trace germ]
\label{thm:sg-decoder}
Let $\{A_j:j\in J\}$ be any finite or countable collection of distinct
normalized maps (SG2). There exists an $\mathcal F_{0+}$-measurable function
$D$ with values in $J\cup\{\dagger\}$ such that
\[
 \mu_{A_j}[D(\gamma)=j]=1\qquad\hbox{for every }j\in J.
 \tag{SG4}
\]
In particular, restrictions of these affine SLE$_6$ measures to the initial
trace-germ sigma-field are mutually singular, even though each separately
has its usual almost-sure germ zero-one property.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For each pair $j\ne k$, Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-unitrestriction} supplies a Borel
set of unit-time traces whose indicator $f_{jk}$ has distinct expectations
under $\mu_{A_j}$ and $\mu_{A_k}$. There are only countably many such pairs;
list all the resulting bounded unit-time observables as $(f_m)_{m\geq1}$.
Write $v_{j,m}=\int f_m\,d\mu_{A_j}$. The vectors
$v_j=(v_{j,m})_m$ are pairwise distinct.

Fix $r>1$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-affinemix} and Birkhoff's ergodic theorem,
for each $m$ and each $j$,
\[
 \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_m(S_{r^n}\gamma)
 \longrightarrow v_{j,m}\qquad\mu_{A_j}\hbox{-almost surely}.
 \tag{SG5}
\]
The intersection of these events over $m$ still has full $\mu_{A_j}$ measure.
Every term $f_m(S_{r^n}\gamma)$ depends only on the trace up to time
$r^{-2n}$. For any $t>0$, all sufficiently late terms are consequently
$\mathcal F_t$-measurable. Omitting finitely many initial terms does not
change a Ces\`aro limit. Thus every limit in (SG5), and the event that the
limit exists, is $\mathcal F_{0+}$-measurable.

Define $D=j$ if all limits exist and their vector equals $v_j$; use
$D=\dagger$ otherwise. Countability of $J$ and pairwise distinctness of the
$v_j$ make this a measurable unambiguous definition. Formula (SG5) proves
(SG4).
\end{proof}


\subsection{A common decoder for the continuum of normalized metrics}

The countable proof extends to all parameters $(b,c)\in\Theta
=\mathbb R\times(0,\infty)$, provided the standard path coding is used
jointly in the affine parameter. We state this coding point explicitly.

\paragraph{Joint Borel capacity coding.}
Let $\eta$ be an ordinary capacity-parameterized SLE$_6$ trace and $K_s$ its
filled hull. Before recoding, its affine image is the continuous path
$A_{b,c}^{-1}\eta(s)$. Its physical capacity clock is
\[
 u_{b,c,\eta}(s)=\tfrac12\operatorname{hcap}(A_{b,c}^{-1}K_s).
 \tag{SG6}
\]
Use the standard measurable coding of compact half-plane hulls, in which
filling a continuous path prefix and taking its half-plane capacity are
Borel operations. Linear transformation is jointly continuous on compact
sets. Consequently $(b,c,\eta,s)\mapsto u_{b,c,\eta}(s)$ is Borel.
The inverse clock is measurable because, on the set where the clock is
continuous and strictly increasing,
\[
 u_{b,c,\eta}^{-1}(t)=\inf\{s:u_{b,c,\eta}(s)>t\}
\]
can be computed using rational $s$ (with equality interpreted by continuity).
Thus evaluation of the recoded image on $[0,1]$ is jointly Borel in
$(b,c,\eta)$. The good-clock conditions are Borel using rational tests;
on their complement use a cemetery path. For each fixed $(b,c)$ this
complement has SLE probability zero by the affine local-growth and lifetime
arguments above. No single full-probability set valid simultaneously for
all affine parameters is required.

This paragraph uses the usual deterministic measurability of hull capacity
as part of the standard Borel curve coding. If that coding fact is not
taken as established, the next corollary should be read conditionally on
joint Borel recoding. The preceding countable theorem needs only measurable
recoding for each of its countably many fixed maps.

\begin{corollary}[Continuum metric decoder]
\label{cor:sg-continuum}
With the joint Borel coding just specified, there exists one
$\mathcal F_{0+}$-measurable map
\[
 D_{\mathrm{all}}:\mathcal C\longrightarrow\Theta\cup\{\dagger\}
\]
such that for every $(b,c)\in\Theta$,
\[
 \mu_{A_{b,c}}[D_{\mathrm{all}}(\gamma)=(b,c)]=1.
 \tag{SG7}
\]
The same single measurable rule therefore recovers every normalized affine
metric, with almost-sure correctness under each metric law separately.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Choose a countable generating $\pi$-system for the standard Borel space of
unit-capacity path segments, including the cemetery state. For example, use
finite intersections of rational-time evaluation cylinders in rational
rectangles, together with the cemetery singleton. Let $(f_m)_{m\geq1}$ be
their indicator functions. They determine probability laws on unit segments.
Joint Borel recoding and integration against the fixed ordinary SLE law imply
that
\[
 v:\Theta\to[0,1]^{\mathbb N},\qquad
 v_m(b,c)=\int f_m\,d\mu_{A_{b,c}},
 \tag{SG8}
\]
is Borel. Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-unitrestriction} and the determining property
of $(f_m)$ imply that $v$ is injective.
Both parameter and sequence spaces are standard Borel, so the Lusin--Souslin
theorem makes $v(\Theta)$ Borel and $v^{-1}:v(\Theta)\to\Theta$ Borel.

Fix $r>1$. Form the vector $L(\gamma)$ of Ces\`aro limits
\[
 L_m(\gamma)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N
 f_m(S_{r^n}\gamma),
\]
using a cemetery value if any limit fails to exist. The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-decoder} shows that $L$ is
$\mathcal F_{0+}$-measurable. Define
$D_{\mathrm{all}}=v^{-1}(L)$ when $L\in v(\Theta)$ and set it to
$\dagger$ otherwise. This is a measurable definition; no measurable
selection theorem is needed.
For each fixed $(b,c)$, ergodicity and Birkhoff applied to the countable
family $(f_m)$ give $L=v(b,c)$ almost surely under $\mu_{A_{b,c}}$.
This proves (SG7).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Quantifiers and limitations.}
The statement is one common decoder with correctness under each law:
$\forall(b,c)\ \mu_{A_{b,c}}(D_{\mathrm{all}}=(b,c))=1$.
It does not assert one common full-probability set of Brownian seeds on
which correctness holds simultaneously after every uncountable collection
of affine transformations. More importantly, the decoded object is still
an ordered initial trace germ at a smooth start, not the bare boundary germ
of a random slit-domain tip.

\paragraph{What has and has not been proved.}
The theorem demonstrates an exact mechanism by which a microscopic ordered
path germ can store a constant affine metric. It is more than identifiability
of the law: a single arbitrarily short trace germ distinguishes any prescribed
countable collection of metrics almost surely, and the continuum corollary
extends this under the stated joint Borel coding. The result does not contradict
a germ zero-one law, since that law concerns one probability measure, while
the distinguishing full-measure germ sets differ between measures.

A hypothetical source-germ decoration could choose a metric from the smooth
initial source geometry, then attempt to recover the same metric from the
slit boundary at all later tips. That recovery does \emph{not} follow from Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-decoder}.
Three obstacles remain:
\begin{enumerate}
\item A later tip is not a smooth half-plane starting boundary point; the
nearby past trace can have a different two-sided or conditioned local law.
\item The remaining marked domain gives an embedded boundary germ, which may
lose chronological information when the trace has touched itself. The theorem
uses an oriented initial trace germ and does not prove that the needed data
are recoverable from that boundary alone.
\item A domain Markov construction would need one measurable rule valid at
all relevant stopping times and compatible with locality deletions, not merely
a decoder at a deterministic initial point under each fixed metric law.
\end{enumerate}
Extending local metric decoding to random interior or tip germs, or proving
that such decoding is impossible from the domain state, is a concrete next
research question. No full counterexample satisfying target consistency is
claimed from this mechanism.

\section{A fixed metric follows inside the affine-SLE ansatz with target consistency and reversal}
\label{sec:affine-ansatz-classification}

\paragraph{Status.}
This theorem assumes that every marginal is already an affine image of
SLE$_6$. It does not derive that ansatz from coarse percolation properties.
It shows that quite weak physical coherence conditions then eliminate all
variation of the affine conformal structure between domains and marked points.
Geometric reversal is an explicit additional hypothesis; it is not asserted
here to follow from RSW or from the original three axioms.

Let $\mathcal D$ be the class of bounded Jordan domains whose boundaries
consist of finitely many regular real-analytic arcs meeting at corners.
Each analytic arc extends to a neighborhood of its closed parameter interval.
Restrict marks to regular boundary points, meaning interior points of these
arcs. Suppose
\[
 P_{D,p,q}=(A_{D,p,q}^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6
 (A_{D,p,q}D,A_{D,p,q}p,A_{D,p,q}q),
 \tag{AA1}
\]
where $A_{D,p,q}$ is an invertible orientation-preserving linear map. Write
$[Q_{D,p,q}]$ for the projective positive-definite tensor
$[A_{D,p,q}^{\mathsf T}A_{D,p,q}]$; brackets identify positive scalar multiples.

The following elementary local identifiability principle will be used:
if two laws of the form (AA1), on domains agreeing near the same regular
start, have identical initial paths stopped in every sufficiently small ball,
then their projective tensors are equal. Indeed blow up at the regular start.
Conformal kernel convergence for ordinary SLE gives half-plane limits
$A_i^{-1}\operatorname{SLE}_6(A_iH,0,\infty)$. Equality of local prefixes
gives equality of these half-plane laws; the strictly monotone SLE$_6$
left-passage function makes $A_2A_1^{-1}$ a similarity. This argument uses
only local SLE convergence and the affine ansatz.

\begin{theorem}[Conditional global metric rigidity]
\label{thm:aa-global}
Assume (AA1), together with the following properties on $\mathcal D$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Translation covariance.}
\item \emph{Locality for nested domains}: if $D'\subset D$ agrees with $D$
near regular marks $p,q$, the two initial laws agree until they encounter the
removed set.
\item \emph{Local target consistency}: for fixed $D,p$ and any two regular
targets $q_1,q_2$, the two initial laws agree when stopped on every
sufficiently small circle centered at $p$.
\item \emph{Geometric reversibility}: reversing an ordered path with law
$P_{D,p,q}$ gives law $P_{D,q,p}$.
\end{enumerate}
Then there is one fixed positive-definite tensor $Q_0$, up to scalar, with
$[Q_{D,p,q}]=[Q_0]$ for every admissible triple. Equivalently, one fixed
linear map transforms the entire family into ordinary chordal SLE$_6$.
No domain Markov hypothesis is needed for this conditional conclusion.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{First, remove dependence on marks inside one domain.}
Fix $D$ and a regular start $p$. Local target consistency and local
identifiability make $[Q_{D,p,q}]$ independent of $q$.
Ordinary SLE$_6$ is geometrically reversible by \cite{ms}. Therefore reversal of (AA1)
retains its same tensor, and the assumed reversal of $P$ implies
\[
 [Q_{D,p,q}]=[Q_{D,q,p}].
\]
Given regular $p,p'$, choose a third regular point $q$. Changing the target
with fixed start $q$ and reversing at the two ends shows
\[
 [Q_{D,p,q}]=[Q_{D,q,p}]=[Q_{D,q,p'}]=[Q_{D,p',q}].
\]
Thus there is a tensor $[Q(D)]$ independent of both marks.

\emph{Second, compare nested domains with retained regular boundary arcs.}
If $D'\subset D$ agrees with $D$ near two distinct regular boundary points,
choose them as $p,q$. Locality makes the initial laws agree in some ball
about $p$, and local identifiability gives $[Q(D')]=[Q(D)]$.
The removed set may meet other parts of the boundary; it stays a positive
distance from the selected marks.

\emph{Third, create a horizontal boundary segment without changing the metric.}
For any $D\in\mathcal D$, choose a horizontal line $\{y=c\}$ with $\min_{\partial D}\operatorname{Im}z<c<\max_{\partial D}\operatorname{Im}z$, at a regular value of the boundary height and not through a corner, and intersect $D$ with
$\{y>c\}$. Such a regular value with nonempty intersection exists: on each nonhorizontal
analytic boundary arc the critical points are isolated, and horizontal arcs
contribute only finitely many excluded height values. Choose the level also
away from all corner heights. The analytic arcs are understood to extend
regularly to neighborhoods of their closed parameter intervals, so there
are only finitely many critical points unless an arc is horizontal.
There are finitely many transverse crossings, and a component $U$ of the
intersection is again a piecewise real-analytic Jordan domain. It contains an
original analytic boundary arc above the cutting line and also an open
horizontal segment of the cutting line on its boundary.
Choose two regular marks on the retained original arc. The second step gives
$[Q(U)]=[Q(D)]$.

Choose a sufficiently short subsegment of the horizontal boundary segment.
An upper half-disk $V$ on that diameter lies in $U$, agrees with $U$ near
two distinct interior diameter points, and belongs to $\mathcal D$.
The second step again gives $[Q(V)]=[Q(U)]$.

\emph{Finally, compare all horizontal upper half-disks.}
Translation covariance identifies their tensors for equal radii and arbitrary centers. Two upper half-disks with the same center and radii
$r<R$ are nested and agree near any two distinct points on the smaller open
diameter. The second step makes their tensors equal. Thus all such half-disks
have one tensor $[Q_0]$, and the third step shows $[Q(D)]=[Q_0]$ for every $D$.

Choose a fixed $A_0$ with $A_0^{\mathsf T}A_0=Q_0$. Equality of projective
tensors gives $A_{D,p,q}=c_{D,p,q}R_{D,p,q}A_0$ with $c_{D,p,q}>0$ and
$R_{D,p,q}$ orthogonal. Similarity covariance of SLE$_6$ in (AA1) removes
those extra factors, leaving exactly
$(A_0^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(A_0D,A_0p,A_0q)$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Boundary regularity in the cutting step.}
A transverse horizontal cut of a piecewise real-analytic Jordan domain has finitely
many intersections with its boundary. Each component is simply connected:
a Jordan loop in the intersection has its interior in both the original
Jordan domain and the upper half-plane. Its boundary consists of finitely
many original arcs and cutting-line segments meeting at transverse corners,
so the component is Jordan with piecewise real-analytic boundary. At least one
retained original arc and one cutting segment occur in every nontrivial
component chosen in the proof. This also makes all marked-point comparisons
legitimate within the stated class.

\paragraph{Use for the percolation program.}
Local target consistency can be transferred from common-color discrete
couplings to simultaneous marginal curve limits without random-domain kernel
convergence, as established separately. Reversibility is a further assumption.
For a general lattice model with critical density different from $1/2$,
reversing the color-oriented exploration is not automatically the same as
swapping its start and target in a fixed convention: it also changes which
boundary arc has each color. No exact discrete color symmetry, nor limiting
reversal theorem, is supplied by the present argument.
The main unsolved analytic step remains establishing (AA1), or an alternative
local structure theorem, from the original axioms plus appropriate coherence.

% Research fragment; intended for inclusion in progress.tex.
\section{Continuous affine labels: deterministic support and source independence}
\label{sec:support-source}

\paragraph{Status.}
The result here is conditional on the deterministic affine-SLE ansatz and an
explicit continuity condition on its metric labels.  It does not apply to a
general exploration ensemble.  It gives a further restriction inside that
ansatz: a continuous conserved label cannot encode information in the source
prime end.  Source independence alone gives no target or domain independence; the final corollary adds explicit target-consistency and locality hypotheses to obtain a global metric.

\subsection{The precise continuity premise}

Let $T(D,p,q)$ be the determinant-one conformal metric tensor of the affine
label, as in (RA6), on all bounded simply connected domains with two distinct
prime ends.  We use the following sufficient premise, stated in a form that
is stable under the fixed affine change of coordinates needed for each
starting law.

\paragraph{Affine-stable marked-chart continuity.}
For every fixed invertible real-linear map $L$, suppose
$f_n:\mathbb H\to E_n$ are conformal maps onto bounded simply connected
domains, with a nondegenerate conformal limit
$f_n\to f:\mathbb H\to E$ locally uniformly in $\mathbb H$.  The marked
prime ends are $f_n(0),f_n(\infty)$ and $f(0),f(\infty)$, respectively;
these are prime-end extensions, whether or not they have Euclidean point
limits.  Require
\[
 T(LE_n,Lf_n(0),Lf_n(\infty))
   \longrightarrow T(LE,Lf(0),Lf(\infty)).
 \tag{SS1}
\]
Thus the topology is locally uniform convergence of inverse conformal
charts with the two designated boundary preimages $0,\infty$, and the
premise also applies after each fixed affine map.  This is an explicit
additional hypothesis; it is not inferred merely from DMP, locality, or
physical translation invariance.  An equivalent affine-stable marked kernel
notion may be used instead, provided it supplies (SS1).


\paragraph{Why fixed affine maps preserve this marked topology (proved using
standard Beltrami stability).}
In fact the displayed premise for all $L$ follows from its case $L=I$.
Here is the needed topological fact.  Write an orientation-preserving
real-linear map as $L(z)=\alpha z+\beta\bar z$, with
$|\beta/\alpha|<1$.  The Beltrami coefficients of $L\circ f_n$ are
\[
 \mu_n(z)=\frac{\beta}{\alpha}
             \frac{\overline{f_n'(z)}}{f_n'(z)}.
\]
Since the nonvanishing derivatives $f_n'$ converge locally uniformly to
$f'$, these coefficients converge pointwise in $\mathbb H$ to the analogous
$\mu$, all with the same bound $k<1$.  Extend them to the lower half-plane
by reflection, $\mu_n(\bar z)=\overline{\mu_n(z)}$.  Let $h_n$ be the
normalized quasiconformal sphere solutions fixing $0,1,\infty$.
The standard stability theorem for the measurable Riemann mapping theorem
(uniform bound $k<1$ and almost-everywhere convergence of coefficients)
gives locally uniform convergence of $h_n$ and their inverses to $h$ and
$h^{-1}$.  Reflection symmetry and normalized uniqueness imply that every
$h_n$ maps $\mathbb H$ onto itself and fixes the two marked boundary points
$0,\infty$.

The maps
\[
 \chi_n=L\circ f_n\circ h_n^{-1}:\mathbb H\longrightarrow LE_n
\]
are conformal by Beltrami factorization.  They converge locally uniformly
to the nondegenerate conformal map
$\chi=L\circ f\circ h^{-1}$, and their marked endpoints are precisely
$Lf_n(0),Lf_n(\infty)$.  This proves continuity of a fixed orientation-
preserving affine change of the physical domains in the marked conformal-
chart topology.  A reflection handles the other orientation.
Consequently ordinary marked-chart continuity of $T$ implies (SS1).
Keeping (SS1) explicit in the proof below makes clear that its charts are
holomorphic in the affine SLE coordinates, even when their physical pullbacks
are only quasiconformal.  The additional analytical input in this paragraph
is the standard normalized Beltrami stability theorem; no claim about
Euclidean uniform boundary convergence is needed.

\subsection{Full support transfers conservation to deterministic Loewner controls}

Fix a starting label $A=A(D,p,q)$ and a conformal map
$f:\mathbb H\to AD$ with $f(0)=Ap$, $f(\infty)=Aq$.  For any continuous
real function $w$ on $[0,t]$ with $w(0)=0$, let $g_t^w$ denote the associated
hydrodynamic Loewner map on its surviving half-plane domain.  Define the
marked target state by the inverse conformal chart
\[
 f_t^w(z)=f\bigl((g_t^w)^{-1}(z+w(t))\bigr),\qquad z\in\mathbb H.
 \tag{SS2}
\]
This state is a simply connected domain with two designated prime ends for
\emph{every} continuous $w$.  The assertion does not say that every such
$w$ generates a trace.  No trace is needed to define the domain state in
(SS2).  For the Brownian driving paths of SLE the state is its actual
remaining target domain.

The map $w\mapsto f_t^w$ is continuous from the uniform norm on $C[0,t]$
to locally uniform convergence of conformal maps on $\mathbb H$.
For example, the inverse map can be represented by the reverse ODE
\[
 \dot v_s=-\frac{2}{v_s-w(t-s)},\quad v_0=z,\quad
 (g_t^w)^{-1}(z)=v_t.
 \tag{SS3}
\]
Its imaginary part is nondecreasing.  On a compact set with
$\operatorname{Im}z\geq\delta>0$, denominators stay away from zero and the
usual ODE stability estimate gives locally uniform continuity in $w$.
Translations by $w(t)$ and composition with $f$ preserve this continuity.
The corresponding metric functional
\[
 \mathcal T_t(w)=
 T\bigl(A^{-1}f_t^w(\mathbb H),
         A^{-1}f_t^w(0),A^{-1}f_t^w(\infty)\bigr)
 \tag{SS4}
\]
is therefore continuous by (SS1), with $L=A^{-1}$.

\paragraph{Deterministic-support lemma (proved).}
If the affine-label ensemble has strong DMP and satisfies (SS1), then for
every $t>0$ and every continuous control $w\in C[0,t]$ with $w(0)=0$,
\[
 \mathcal T_t(w)=T(D,p,q).
 \tag{SS5}
\]
For the source-independence conclusion below it suffices to apply this to
smooth linear ramps.

\begin{proof}
Under the original affine SLE law the driving process in $AD$ is
$\sqrt6 B$.  The DMP conservation criterion (RA7) gives
$\mathcal T_t(\sqrt6 B)=T(D,p,q)$ almost surely.  Brownian measure has full
support on the space of continuous functions starting at zero in the
uniform topology.  If (SS5) failed at one control $w$, continuity of
$\mathcal T_t$ would give an open uniform neighborhood on which it differs
from the fixed tensor.  Brownian full support gives that neighborhood
positive probability, contradicting almost-sure conservation.
\end{proof}

One could avoid defining the functional at non-trace-generating controls
by restricting the statement to controls at which the state functional has
a continuous extension, and applying full support there.  In particular
smooth ramps suffice.  The all-prime-end ansatz already makes (SS4) defined
on every state, so the formulation above is simpler.

\subsection{Rapid driving ramps move the source at vanishing capacity}

\paragraph{Theorem (proved, within the stated ansatz).}
Under strong DMP and (SS1), the tensor $T(D,p,q)$ is independent of the
source prime end: for every bounded simply connected $D$ and every three
prime ends with $p,p'\ne q$,
\[
 T(D,p,q)=T(D,p',q).
 \tag{SS6}
\]
It may still depend on the target or on the domain; these are unresolved
by this argument.

\begin{proof}
Use the notation above and write $Ap'=f(a)$ for the unique finite
$a\in\mathbb R$ corresponding to the new source.  Choose $t_n\downarrow0$
and use the smooth control
\[
 w_n(s)=\frac{as}{t_n},\qquad 0\leq s\leq t_n.
\]
The deterministic-support lemma says that every resulting metric equals
$T(D,p,q)$.  From the reverse equation (SS3),
\[
 |(g_{t_n}^{w_n})^{-1}(z)-z|
 \leq\frac{2t_n}{\operatorname{Im}z}.
 \tag{SS7}
\]
Consequently, locally uniformly in $\mathbb H$,
\[
 f_{t_n}^{w_n}(z)
   = f\bigl((g_{t_n}^{w_n})^{-1}(z+a)\bigr)
   \longrightarrow f(z+a).
\]
The limiting chart has image $AD$, source $f(a)=Ap'$, and the same target
$f(\infty)=Aq$.  Applying (SS1) with $L=A^{-1}$ gives (SS6).
\end{proof}

The controls may have very large slopes as $t_n\to0$; this is harmless.
Each fixed ramp lies in the support of Brownian driving on its own finite
interval.  The argument uses the possibility of rare events, not their
uniform probability as $n\to\infty$.  The limiting source change is a
statement about marked prime-end charts, not Euclidean convergence of a
curve of vanishing diameter: the capacity vanishes while the tip may
travel far along a thin boundary region.

\subsection{A fixed metric in the affine ansatz without a reversal hypothesis}

\paragraph{Corollary (proved, conditional on the global affine ansatz).}
Suppose the ensemble has a deterministic affine-SLE label on \emph{all}
bounded simply connected domains and distinct prime ends, has strong DMP,
and its normalized tensor $T$ is continuous for marked inverse-conformal-
chart convergence.  Suppose in addition that its piecewise real-analytic
Jordan subfamily has translation covariance, locality for nested domains
retaining neighborhoods of regular marks, and local target consistency in
the sense used by the conditional global metric-rigidity theorem.  Then
there is one fixed determinant-one tensor $Q_0$ such that
\[
 T(D,p,q)=Q_0
\]
for every bounded simply connected marked domain.  No geometric reversal
hypothesis is needed for this conditional conclusion.

\begin{proof}
The source-independence theorem removes dependence on $p$.  On a regular
piecewise real-analytic Jordan domain, local target consistency together
with the local half-plane identifiability argument removes dependence on
$q$ as well.  Thus these domains have a tensor $Q(D)$ independent of both
marks.  The nested-cut argument in the preceding conditional global
metric-rigidity theorem now applies verbatim, without its reversal step:
nested locality identifies tensors of domains agreeing near two retained
regular marks; a transverse horizontal cut creates a horizontal segment;
a small upper half-disk on that segment retains the metric; translation
and nesting identify all such half-disks.  Hence all piecewise real-analytic
Jordan domains have the same tensor $Q_0$.

To extend to an arbitrary bounded simply connected $D$, choose a conformal
$f:\mathbb D\to D$ and distinct circle points $\zeta_p,\zeta_q$ representing
$p,q$.  For $r_n\uparrow1$, set
\[
 D_n=f(r_n\mathbb D),\qquad
 p_n=f(r_n\zeta_p),\quad q_n=f(r_n\zeta_q).
\]
Each $D_n$ is an analytic Jordan domain: $f$ extends holomorphically and
univalently across the compact circle $|z|=r_n$, with nonzero derivative.
Choose a conformal $m:\mathbb H\to\mathbb D$ with
$m(0)=\zeta_p$, $m(\infty)=\zeta_q$.  The marked inverse charts
\[
 f_n(z)=f(r_n m(z))
\]
converge locally uniformly to $f(m(z))$, giving exactly the desired marked
prime-end limit, even if $f$ has no Euclidean limit at one of the selected
circle points.  Tensor continuity and $T(D_n,p_n,q_n)=Q_0$ give
$T(D,p,q)=Q_0$.  The fixed-affine normalizer follows from SLE similarity
covariance as in the preceding theorem.
\end{proof}

This corollary removes a reversal assumption \emph{inside the global affine
ansatz}, at the price of strong DMP and a precise continuity premise.  It
still supplies no argument that the original three axioms, RSW, or local
target consistency force a marginal exploration law to belong to that ansatz.


\clearpage
\section{Continuation under the corrected source-neighborhood locality}
\label{sec:corrected-continuation}

The specification at commit \texttt{0e47018} requires equality of initial
curve laws whenever the physical domains and their boundary colors agree
near the source; the remote target and remote domain may differ. We call
this strong source locality below. We use one fixed boundary-color orientation,
so two distinct color-orientation sectors are not an additional hidden label.
No counterexample to the corrected three axioms in that convention is
established in this continuation. Neither is the full classification proved.

There are three main positive advances: a quantitative driving-drift
rigidity theorem needing no assumed regularity of quadratic variation;
a common-metric theorem inside the affine-SLE ansatz requiring stability
only at one straight slit-tip germ; and target-patch stability from a precise
conditional unforced-crossing estimate. A coarse nonconcentration estimate also makes affine anisotropies compact. A common-color RSW shield additionally proves quantitative source-patch stability in flat and straight-slit geometries; it is not asserted for arbitrary curve laws or arbitrary domain approximations. Every analytical or coupling
premise added to the project axioms is identified explicitly. The stronger
wrong-coordinate obstruction concerns the known affine-SLE family, not an
unknown general scaling limit.

Independent audits checked the signed-measure characteristic identities,
the mirrored-slit coefficients, analytic-germ covariance, the narrow
straight-tip support argument, the rough domain-control extension, and
the target-patch and rank-collapse estimates. In particular, Brownian full
support was not used to extend almost-sure identities to null deterministic
histories without a stated stability premise.
% Research fragment for the corrected source-neighborhood locality assumption.
% Requires amsmath/amssymb/amsthm; all additional hypotheses are explicit.
\section{A near-source drift-boundedness criterion}
\label{sec:strong-local-loewner}

\paragraph{Status.}
This is a new conditional rigidity result.  It does not assume conformal
invariance, Brownian driving, or local-martingale driving.  Instead it shows
that a locally bounded \emph{initial driving drift}, together with an
absolutely continuous drift part and locality, already forces zero drift and
variance six.  No regularity of quadratic variation is assumed.  Strong DMP then identifies the whole driving law.  The
boundedness and characteristic hypotheses are not derived from RSW or from
the three project axioms.  A finite-dimensional bulk-jet closure is given as
one explicit sufficient hypothesis.

Only a particular deletion consequence of the corrected source-neighborhood
locality is needed.  Arbitrary target changes and the full force of corrected
locality are not used in this theorem.  Thus the result is compatible with,
but does not exhaust, the corrected research objective.

\subsection{Chart-dependent initial characteristics and their covariance}

Let $f:\mathbb H\to D$ be an inverse chordal conformal chart, with source
$f(0)=p$ and target $f(\infty)=q$, both interpreted as prime ends.
For the physical ensemble in $D$, let $W^f$ be its driving process in this
chart, with half-plane capacity normalization
\[
 \partial_t g_t(z)=\frac{2}{g_t(z)-W^f_t},\qquad W^f_0=0.
\]
Assume that it is a continuous semimartingale in its natural trace filtration,
with decomposition
\[
 W^f_t=M^f_t+\int_0^t b^f_s\,ds,\qquad
 C^f_t=\langle M^f\rangle_t.
 \tag{DR1}
\]
The drift-density paths $b^f$ are assumed finite and right-continuous,
after local stopping if necessary.  No absolute continuity or regularity
of $C^f$ is assumed.  At the initial time suppose
\[
 b^f_0=B(f)\in\mathbb R
 \tag{DR2}
\]
is deterministic.  It may depend on the entire physical marked domain and
its conformal chart.  In particular no Markov property of the single real
driver is assumed.  A finite deterministic initial variance rate $V(f)$
will be \emph{derived} below from locality and (DR1)--(DR2).

Let $A\subset\overline{\mathbb H}$ be a half-plane hull disjoint from $0$,
let $h:\mathbb H\setminus A\to\mathbb H$ be hydrodynamically normalized,
and set
\[
 \bar h(z)=h(z)-h(0),\qquad f_A=f\circ\bar h^{-1}.
 \tag{DR3}
\]
The source is still the same physical prime end, since $\bar h(0)=0$;
the target is also unchanged.  Put $d=h'(0)>0$, $e=h''(0)$.
Source locality, together with the usual deletion-chart calculation, will
give a finite deterministic initial variance rate $V(f)$ and the covariance
\[
 \boxed{\quad V(f_A)=V(f),\qquad
 B(f_A)=\frac{B(f)}{d}
       +\left(\frac{V(f)}2-3\right)\frac{e}{d^2}.\quad}
 \tag{DR4}
\]
These are identities for \emph{initial} characteristics and do not identify
the two complete driving laws.

Here are the details without assuming a quadratic-variation density.
If $h_t$ is the evolving deletion map, then $du=h_t'(W_t)^2dt$, and
It\^o's formula gives
\[
 d\widehat W_{u(t)}
  =h_t'(W_t)\,dM_t+h_t'(W_t)b_t\,dt
       +\frac12 h_t''(W_t)\,dC_t-3h_t''(W_t)\,dt.
 \tag{DR5}
\]
The $-3$ comes from
\[
 \partial_t h_t(z)
 =\frac{2h_t'(W_t)^2}{h_t(z)-h_t(W_t)}
       -\frac{2h_t'(z)}{z-W_t},\qquad
 (\partial_t h_t)(W_t)=-3h_t''(W_t).
\]
The comparison of filtrations is justified by geometric invertibility:
from a deleted-domain initial trace one reconstructs its original trace and
capacity, so the inverse capacity clock is adapted and its inverse is a
stopping-time clock.  Locality therefore compares the same trace filtration,
not an unrelated enlargement.  Adaptedness of an arbitrary increasing clock
would not suffice for this step.

\paragraph{Local bracket-regularization lemma (proved).}
Suppose $h''(0)\ne0$.  Then on a positive initial interval, $C_t$ is absolutely
continuous and has a finite right-continuous density.  In particular its
initial right derivative $V(f)$ exists and is deterministic.

\begin{proof}
The deleted-domain driver also has an absolutely continuous drift,
$\int\widehat b_u\,du$, by (DR1).  Uniqueness of the continuous
semimartingale decomposition applied to (DR5), after the justified capacity
time change, gives the signed-measure identity
\[
 h_t''(W_t)\bigl(dC_t-6\,dt\bigr)
   =2\bigl[h_t'(W_t)^2\widehat b_{u(t)}
                      -h_t'(W_t)b_t\bigr]dt.
 \tag{DR5a}
\]
The second derivative starts nonzero and remains so on a positive initial
interval.  On that interval the bracket is absolutely continuous, with
\[
 v_t=6+2\frac{h_t'(W_t)^2\widehat b_{u(t)}
                         -h_t'(W_t)b_t}{h_t''(W_t)}.
 \tag{DR5b}
\]
The derivative paths of $h_t$ along the continuous driving point are
continuous, while the two drift-density paths are right-continuous and
finite.  Thus $v$ is right-continuous and finite there.  In particular
\[
 V(f)=v_0
   =6+2\frac{d^2 B(f_A)-d B(f)}e
 \tag{DR5c}
\]
is deterministic.  This is an intrinsic initial rate of $C$, so it does
not depend on which nonzero-$e$ deletion was used to derive it.
Applying the same time change to bracket gives
$V(f_A)=V(f)$.  Rearranging (DR5c) gives the drift identity (DR4).
\end{proof}

There is always an available nonzero-$e$ test deletion, for example one
vertical slit at any fixed real point different from $0$.  Right-continuity
makes the initial drift and variance rates intrinsic, rather than arbitrary
versions of densities at a single time.  The bracket density has only been
proved locally at the start; its global regularity is not being presumed.

\paragraph{Source-neighborhood convention.}
In half-plane coordinates these slit deletions preserve a full neighborhood
of the source end.  Under $f$ this is a prime-end neighborhood.  Thus (DR4)
is justified on arbitrary rough domains if the corrected locality assumption
is formulated for such neighborhoods, or if these particular deletions are
explicitly admissible.  For regular analytic source boundaries it is ordinary
Euclidean neighborhood locality.  If locality is instead stated only using
Euclidean balls at non-singleton source impressions, the all-rough-domain
extension must remain an explicit additional hypothesis.

\subsection{Two vanishing slits force both characteristics}

For $\epsilon>0$ take the reflected vertical slits
\[
 A_\epsilon^\pm
  =\{\,\pm\epsilon^2+iy:0\leq y\leq2\epsilon\,\}.
 \tag{DR6}
\]
Their hydrodynamic maps are
\[
 h_\epsilon^\pm(z)
 =\pm\epsilon^2+
   \sqrt{(z\mp\epsilon^2)^2+4\epsilon^2},
 \tag{DR7}
\]
with the square-root branch asymptotic to $z\mp\epsilon^2$ at infinity.
Let $f_\epsilon^\pm=f\circ
(h_\epsilon^\pm-h_\epsilon^\pm(0))^{-1}$.
Both sequences are conformal charts of bounded simply connected domains and
\[
 f_\epsilon^\pm\longrightarrow f
 \quad\text{locally uniformly in }\mathbb H.
 \tag{DR8}
\]
Indeed the slits have diameter $2\epsilon$ and approach $0$; directly from
(DR7), their maps and inverse maps converge to the identity on compact
subsets of $\mathbb H$, while $h_\epsilon^\pm(0)\to0$.
Crucially their boundary derivatives do \emph{not} converge to the identity
jet:
\[
 d_\epsilon=(h_\epsilon^\pm)'(0)
     =\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{\epsilon^2+4}}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 e_\epsilon^\pm=(h_\epsilon^\pm)''(0)
     =\mp\frac{4}{\epsilon(\epsilon^2+4)^{3/2}}.
 \tag{DR9}
\]
In particular
\[
 \frac{e_\epsilon^\pm}{d_\epsilon^2}
 =\mp\frac{4}{\epsilon^3\sqrt{\epsilon^2+4}}.
 \tag{DR10}
\]
Thus compact convergence of inverse charts permits very large second-jet
changes precisely at the source boundary.

\paragraph{Initial rigidity theorem (proved).}
Fix $f$ and assume (DR1)--(DR4) for $f$ and the charts $f_\epsilon^\pm$.
If
\[
 \sup_{0<\epsilon<\epsilon_0}
       \max\{|B(f_\epsilon^+)|,|B(f_\epsilon^-)|\}<\infty
 \tag{DR11}
\]
for some $\epsilon_0>0$, then
\[
 B(f)=0,\qquad V(f)=6.
 \tag{DR12}
\]
No continuity of $B$ is required.

\begin{proof}
Write $b=B(f)$, $v=V(f)$ and $b_\epsilon^\pm=B(f_\epsilon^\pm)$.
Equation (DR4) gives
\[
 b_\epsilon^\pm
 =\frac b{d_\epsilon}
    +\left(\frac v2-3\right)
                       \frac{e_\epsilon^\pm}{d_\epsilon^2}.
\]
Since $e_\epsilon^-=-e_\epsilon^+$,
\[
 b_\epsilon^++b_\epsilon^-=\frac{2b}{d_\epsilon},\qquad
 b_\epsilon^+-b_\epsilon^-=
       (v-6)\frac{e_\epsilon^+}{d_\epsilon^2}.
 \tag{DR13}
\]
The first right side is bounded only if $b=0$, since
$d_\epsilon\to0$.  The second is bounded only if $v=6$, since its multiplier
has magnitude asymptotic to $2\epsilon^{-3}$.  This proves (DR12).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Exact instability and weaker rate conditions.}
For real numbers $U,C$ one has
$\max\{|U+C|,|U-C|\}=|U|+|C|$.  Hence the two drift covariances give the
exact identity
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \max\{|b_\epsilon^+|,|b_\epsilon^-|\}
 =\frac{|b|\sqrt{\epsilon^2+4}}{\epsilon}
  +\frac{4|v/2-3|}{\epsilon^3\sqrt{\epsilon^2+4}}.
 \quad}
 \tag{DR14}
\]
There is no possible cancellation between the two reflected tests.
In particular, if the displayed maximum is $o(\epsilon^{-3})$, then $v=6$.
If it is $o(\epsilon^{-1})$, then $b=0$ and $v=6$.
Boundedness is more than sufficient.  Conversely, if $v\ne6$, its leading
blow-up is $|v-6|\epsilon^{-3}$; if $v=6$ and $b\ne0$, its leading blow-up
is $2|b|\epsilon^{-1}$.  This is a concrete, quantitative obstruction to any
alternative semimartingale driving law with tame initial drift under source
perturbations.

\subsection{Local boundedness in marked-chart topology suffices}

Equip inverse marked charts with locally uniform convergence on
$\mathbb H$, retaining the fixed preimages $0,\infty$ of source and target.
If $B$ is locally bounded at every $f$ in this topology, then (DR8) implies
(DR11).  In particular continuity of $B$ is sufficient, but unnecessarily
strong.  This is a hypothesis about the driving characteristic itself;
weak convergence of curve laws alone does not imply it.

\paragraph{Conditional classification theorem (proved).}
Suppose a Borel family of continuous Loewner-generating exploration laws on
all bounded simply connected marked prime-end domains has strong DMP and
source-neighborhood locality (including the test deletions (DR6) in each
chart).  Suppose its drivers satisfy (DR1)--(DR2), with finite right-continuous
drift-density paths and the usual restart compatibility of characteristic
kernels, with no initial assumption on quadratic variation.  If the initial drift map $B$ is locally bounded in marked inverse-
chart topology, then every driving process is
\[
 W^f_t=\sqrt6\,B_t,
\]
and the family is chordal SLE$_6$ in these conformal coordinates.

\begin{proof}
The initial rigidity theorem gives $B(f)=0$ and the derived $V(f)=6$ for
every marked inverse chart.  At any deterministic rational time $r$,
condition on the initial exploration and use strong DMP in its remaining
marked domain and current conformal chart.  The centered future driver is
exactly the restarted driver; its initial drift is $b^f_r$, by
right-continuity.  The restarted law has initial drift zero, so
$b^f_r=0$ almost surely.  Countably many rational times and right-continuity
give $b^f_t=0$ throughout each localized lifetime.  Therefore all domain
chart drivers are continuous local martingales.

For completeness the no-density local-martingale locality argument then
identifies their brackets.  With both drifts zero, (DR5a) becomes
$h_t''(W_t)(dC_t-6\,dt)=0$.  A nonzero-$e$ vertical deletion yields
$C_t=6t$ on a positive initial interval.  Let
\[
 T=\inf\{t\geq0:C_t-6t\ne0\}.
\]
This is an entrance stopping time of a continuous adapted process into an
open set, in the usual right-continuous filtration.  The initial zero
interval gives $T>0$.  If $T<\infty$, continuity gives $C_T=6T$ and the
first-deviation definition gives nonzero values arbitrarily soon after $T$.
Strong DMP restarted at $T$ gives another positive zero interval, a
contradiction.  Thus $C_t=6t$ throughout.  L\'evy's characterization gives
Brownian driving, and Loewner generation identifies SLE$_6$.
\end{proof}

The Borel-kernel condition means that the family admits the standard strong
conditional-kernel interpretation on random slit domains, and that the
characteristics can be chosen consistently on that family.  It is not a
claim that deterministic-domain convergence of discrete explorations proves
this property.  Translation invariance is not needed in this conditional
theorem.  If its hypotheses hold only after one fixed physical affine
normalization, the conclusion is the corresponding fixed-affine SLE$_6$
family; an affine change of physical coordinates is not presumed to preserve
semimartingale driving in an arbitrary preselected complex structure.

\subsection{A finite-dimensional closure that is completely classified}

For example, fix finitely many interior points $z_j\in\mathbb H$ and
finitely many nonnegative derivative orders $m_j$.  Let
\[
 J(f)=\bigl(f^{(k)}(z_j):1\leq j\leq N,
                              0\leq k\leq m_j\bigr).
\]
Suppose the initial drift has a representation
\[
 B(f)=\mathcal B(J(f)),
 \tag{DR15}
\]
where $\mathcal B$ is locally bounded at every realized jet vector.
The bracket has no assumed density or finite-dimensional representation;
its initial rate is supplied by the local regularization lemma.
Locally uniform convergence of conformal maps implies convergence of all
these interior jets by Cauchy's formula, so (DR15) implies local boundedness
of $B$ in marked-chart topology.  The conditional classification theorem
therefore applies: within (DR1)--(DR2), every source-local strong-DMP family
with such a locally bounded finite-dimensional \emph{bulk-jet drift closure}
is SLE$_6$.

This statement does not cover a closure depending on unbounded source
boundary derivatives such as $f'(0)$ or a boundary pre-Schwarzian.  Those
quantities can change without control under (DR8), and can carry exactly the
singular terms in (DR13).  Nor does it show that true percolation limits have
semimartingale driving in a fixed physical complex structure.  The new
rigorous reduction is that any non-SLE candidate with regular characteristic
rates must violate the displayed near-source drift bound, in the explicit
$\epsilon^{-1}$ or $\epsilon^{-3}$ manner above.


\subsection{The criterion also detects a wrong affine coordinate system}

\paragraph{Corollary (proved).}
Let $A$ be an orientation-preserving real-linear map which is not a Euclidean
similarity, and let the physical ensemble be the fixed-affine pullback
\[
 P_{D,p,q}=(A^{-1})_*
       \operatorname{Law}(\mathrm{SLE}_6(AD;Ap,Aq)).
\]
Although this family satisfies corrected source-neighborhood locality and
strong DMP, in the original Euclidean complex structure it cannot satisfy
both the regular semimartingale characteristic assumptions (DR1)--(DR2) in
all marked domains and the locally bounded initial-drift condition above.

\begin{proof}
If both properties held, the conditional classification theorem would make
this ensemble ordinary SLE$_6$ in the original complex structure.  The
single-domain affine identifiability theorem would then make $A$ a
similarity, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

Thus the drift criterion selects a conformal structure.  It is not a
coordinate-free consequence of coarse crossing bounds: bounded affine
changes preserve such bounds, whereas a wrong affine structure necessarily
violates at least one of the criterion's additional analytic hypotheses.
The corollary does not decide whether the failure is semimartingality,
right-continuity of the drift density, deterministic initial drift,
or boundedness of the initial drift; distinguishing these failures is a
separate question.

\subsection{What corrected locality says at a regular analytic source germ}

Here is a separate necessary local structure statement that uses the full
corrected locality, including changes of the distant target.  It concerns
initial characteristics only, not existence of a process from arbitrary
coefficient choices.

Suppose the physical boundary is analytic near $p$ and the source colors
are fixed there.  Let $G$ denote this embedded colored source germ.  For a
chart $f$ extending conformally across $0$, define
\[
 a(f)=|f'(0)|>0,\qquad
 P(f)=\operatorname{Re}\frac{f''(0)}{f'(0)}.
 \tag{DR16}
\]
If another physical marked domain has the same source germ but any distant
target, let $k$ be its inverse chordal chart with $k(0)=p$.  The local map
$h=k^{-1}\circ f$ is analytic across $0$, takes the real boundary interval
to a real interval, and has $h(0)=0$, $d=h'(0)>0$, $e=h''(0)\in\mathbb R$.
The two initial physical traces agree by corrected locality.  The local
Loewner coordinate comparison is exactly (DR4), with the same derivation
from pole cancellation and It\^o's formula; a globally defined deletion map
is unnecessary.  Consequently $V(f)=V(k)$ depends only on $G$.

\paragraph{Proposition (proved).}
Under the semimartingale assumptions above, corrected locality at analytic
source germs is equivalent at the level of initial characteristic covariance
to the existence of two source-germ quantities $V(G)$ and $\beta(G)$ such that
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 V(f)=V(G),\qquad
 B(f)=a(f)\,\beta(G)
                 +\left(3-\frac{V(G)}2\right)P(f).
 \quad}
 \tag{DR17}
\]
Here ``equivalent at the level of covariance'' means precisely that every
covariant initial-characteristic assignment has this form, and every
assignment of this form obeys the local chart covariance identity.  It does
not mean that arbitrary $V,\beta$ yield a domain Markov exploration family.

\begin{proof}
Since $k=f\circ h^{-1}$, the chain rule gives
\[
 a(k)=\frac{a(f)}d,\qquad
 P(k)=\frac{P(f)}d-\frac e{d^2}.
\]
Write $c=V(G)/2-3$.  Combining these transformations with
$B(k)=B(f)/d+c e/d^2$ yields
\[
 \frac{B(k)+cP(k)}{a(k)}
       =\frac{B(f)+cP(f)}{a(f)}.
\]
This common value is the source-germ invariant $\beta(G)$, proving (DR17).
Conversely direct substitution gives the required covariance.
\end{proof}

Physical translation invariance makes $V(G)$ and $\beta(G)$ invariant under
translations of the colored germ.  If dilation covariance is separately
assumed, then $V(\lambda G)=V(G)$ and
$\beta(\lambda G)=\lambda^{-1}\beta(G)$.  For a straight source-boundary germ,
which is unchanged up to translation by dilation, this would force
$\beta(G)=0$; the dimensionless variance can still depend on the boundary
orientation before an affine metric has been identified.

The boundary pre-Schwarzian term in (DR17) explains the quantitative anomaly
in the vanishing-slit test.  Even when the physical source germ is unchanged,
$P(f_\epsilon)$ may grow as $\epsilon^{-3}$ while $f_\epsilon\to f$ on every
interior compact set.  Thus source locality by itself gives a connection-type
transformation law, rather than vanishing drift.  The locally bounded bulk
closure theorem removes exactly this uncontrolled boundary dependence.
The missing global step is to understand how DMP constrains these local
assignments at rough, dynamically created source germs, where the analytic
boundary jet in (DR16) no longer exists.

% Investigation under the revised source-neighborhood locality axiom.
% Research fragment; intended for progress.tex after independent checking.
\section{Rigidity from an averaged initial finite-variation characteristic}
\label{sec:fv-tangent-rigidity}

\paragraph{Scope.}
This conditional theorem weakens the preceding driving-regularity premise.
The canonical finite-variation characteristic need not be absolutely
continuous and need not possess a right-continuous density. We assume only
a deterministic first-order tangent in probability, uniformly on shrinking
initial intervals. No such property is derived from RSW or the three axioms.

Let the driver in inverse chart $f$ have canonical decomposition
\[
 W^f=M^f+A^f,\qquad W^f_0=M^f_0=A^f_0=0,
 \tag{FV1}
\]
where $M^f$ is a continuous local martingale and $A^f$ is continuous,
adapted, and locally of finite variation. Write $C^f=\langle M^f\rangle$.
Assume for every deterministic state and inherited chordal chart that a
finite deterministic number $B(f)$ satisfies
\[
 \frac{1}{t}\sup_{0\le s\le t}|A^f_s-B(f)s|
       \xrightarrow[t\downarrow0]{\mathbb P}0.
 \tag{FV2}
\]
An almost-sure derivative $A^f_t/t\to B(f)$ implies (FV2), but (FV2)
is weaker. No bound on $\operatorname{TV}(A^f;[0,t])/t$ is assumed.

\begin{lemma}[A small finite-variation displacement in an analytic field]
\label{lem:fv-analytic-displacement}
Let $W=M+A$ as in (FV1), let $C=\langle M\rangle$, and suppose
$\sup_{s\le t}|A_s|=O_{\mathbb P}(t)$ and $A_t/t\to B$ in probability.
Let $H_t(x)$ be a random field analytic in $x$ on a fixed neighborhood of
zero for a positive initial time interval. Suppose it has absolutely
continuous finite variation in $t$, and, after localization, its first three
spatial derivatives and the first spatial derivative of $\partial_tH_t$
are bounded there. Assume $H_t'(x)\to d$ as $(t,x)\to(0,0)$.
Then
\[
 \frac1t\int_0^t H_s'(W_s)\,dA_s\longrightarrow dB
       \quad\hbox{in probability}.
 \tag{FV3}
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Localize so $M,A,W$ stay in the spatial neighborhood. Apply the
finite-variation It\^o--Wentzell formula to
$F_t(x)=H_t(x+A_t)-H_t(x)$ at $x=M_t$. It gives
\begin{align*}
 \int_0^tH_s'(W_s)\,dA_s
 ={}&H_t(M_t+A_t)-H_t(M_t)\\
 &-\int_0^t[H_s'(M_s+A_s)-H_s'(M_s)]\,dM_s\\
 &-\frac12\int_0^t[H_s''(M_s+A_s)-H_s''(M_s)]\,dC_s\\
 &-\int_0^t[(\partial_sH_s)(M_s+A_s)
                  -(\partial_sH_s)(M_s)]\,ds.
\end{align*}
The first term divided by $t$ converges to $dB$ by the mean-value formula.
The second term is a continuous local martingale $N_t$ with bracket at
most $K^2\sup_{s\le t}|A_s|^2 C_t$. Thus $N_t/t\to0$ in probability:
stop when $|A_s|>Lt$ or $C_s>\eta$, apply the martingale maximal
inequality, then let $t\downarrow0$, $\eta\downarrow0$, and $L\uparrow\infty$.
Here $C_t\to0$ almost surely and $\sup|A_s|/t$ is tight. The third term
divided by $t$ is bounded by
$K\sup|A_s|C_t/t=o_{\mathbb P}(1)$; the fourth is bounded by
$K\sup|A_s|=o_{\mathbb P}(1)$. No total-variation estimate was used.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Initial covariance without a drift density]
\label{prop:fv-initial-covariance}
Suppose source locality allows one test deletion with hydrodynamic map $h$,
$d=h'(0)>0$, $e=h''(0)\ne0$, and both original and deleted drivers obey
(FV1)--(FV2), with their canonical characteristics in the same trace
filtration. Then
\[
 C^f_t/t\longrightarrow V(f)\in[0,\infty)
       \quad\hbox{in probability},
\]
where $V(f)$ is deterministic, and
\[
 V(f_A)=V(f),\qquad
 B(f_A)=\frac{B(f)}d+
            \left(\frac{V(f)}2-3\right)\frac e{d^2}.
 \tag{FV4}
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the same evolving deletion maps and stopping-time capacity clocks as
in (DR5), now without assuming a drift density. Put
$p_t=h_t'(W_t)$, $q_t=h_t''(W_t)$ and $u(t)=\int_0^t p_s^2ds$.
Uniqueness of the canonical semimartingale decomposition gives
\[
 \widehat A_{u(t)}
  =\int_0^t p_s\,dA_s+\frac12\int_0^tq_s\,dC_s
                                    -3\int_0^tq_s\,ds.
 \tag{FV5}
\]
The deletion field satisfies the analytic-field lemma after localization;
its Loewner field derivative and spatial derivatives are bounded near the
uncollided source. Therefore the first integral divided by $t$ tends to
$dB(f)$, without assuming $C_t/t$ is tight. Also $u(t)/t\to d^2$.
Condition (FV2) for the deleted state implies
$\widehat A_{u(t)}/t\to d^2B(f_A)$: on the event $u(t)\le Kt$, bound
the error by $t^{-1}\sup_{s\le Kt}|\widehat A_s-B(f_A)s|$, and choose
fixed $K>d^2$.

Since $q_t\to e\ne0$, (FV5) first implies $C_t/t$ is tight: with
probability tending to one $q_s$ has fixed sign and magnitude at least
$|e|/2$ on $[0,t]$, so $|\int q_s dC_s|\ge |e|C_t/2$.
Consequently
\[
 \frac1t\int_0^tq_s\,dC_s-e\frac{C_t}t
      \longrightarrow0
 \quad\hbox{in probability}.
\]
Taking (FV5) divided by $t$ now gives
\[
 V(f)=6+\frac{2\{d^2B(f_A)-dB(f)\}}e.
\]
This is intrinsic, nonnegative, and deterministic. The bracket relation
$\widehat C_{u(t)}=\int_0^t p_s^2dC_s$ gives $V(f_A)=V(f)$.
For this last step the tangents of $C,\widehat C$ may be evaluated at
asymptotically deterministic clocks by monotonic squeezing; convergence in
probability of an arbitrary signed process would not justify that step.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A finite-variation process with zero right tangent at every stopping time]
\label{lem:fv-zero-stopping-tangents}
Let $A_0=0$ be a continuous adapted locally finite-variation process. If
for every bounded stopping time $\tau$ before lifetime
\[
 \frac{A_{\tau+h}-A_\tau}{h}\longrightarrow0
       \quad\hbox{in probability as }h\downarrow0,
 \tag{FV6}
\]
then $A$ is indistinguishably zero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Work on a compact localized interval. Pathwise put
$V_t=\operatorname{TV}(A;[0,t])$, and use its generalized inverse
$\theta(u)=\inf\{t:V_t\ge u\}$ for $0<u<V_T$. The usual arclength
parametrization of a continuous finite-variation path is
$G(u)=A_{\theta(u)}$: it is $1$-Lipschitz, satisfies
$A_t=G(V_t)$, and has $|G'(u)|=1$ for Lebesgue almost every $u$.
Indeed the variation of $G$ on every interval is its interval length.
The monotone function $\theta$ has a finite derivative
$\theta'(u)\in[0,\infty)$ for almost every $u$. Exclude also the
countably many levels corresponding to nontrivial flat intervals of $V$.

At a level where these properties hold, let $s=\theta(u)$ and
$v(h)=V_{s+h}-u$. Then $v(h)>0$ and $v(h)\to0$ as $h\downarrow0$.
The generalized-inverse bounds
$\theta((u+v(h))- )\le s+h\le\theta((u+v(h))+ )$
and differentiability of $\theta$ at $u$ give
$h/v(h)\to\theta'(u)$. Combining this with differentiability of $G$ gives
\[
 \lim_{h\downarrow0}\frac{A_{s+h}-A_s}{h}
 =\begin{cases}
 G'(u)/\theta'(u),&\theta'(u)>0,\\
 \operatorname{sgn}(G'(u))\,\infty,&\theta'(u)=0.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{FV7}
\]
Thus the extended right derivative is nonzero at almost every variation
level, including levels whose inverse belongs to a singular part of $dV$.

For deterministic $u>0$ use the stopping times
$\tau_u=\inf\{t:V_t\ge u\}$, truncated at a deterministic endpoint $T$.
Fubini and the preceding pathwise statement show that for Lebesgue
almost every deterministic $u$, on $\{\tau_u<T\}$ the quotient in
(FV6) has a nonzero or infinite pathwise limit almost surely.
But (FV6) gives convergence in probability to zero at this stopping time,
which forbids such an event of positive probability. Thus
$\mathbb P(\tau_u<T)=0$ for Lebesgue almost every $u$, and
$\mathbb E[V_T]=\int_0^\infty\mathbb P(\tau_u<T)du=0$ after localizing
$V_T$ to be integrable. Hence $V_T=0$ almost surely. Localization and a
countable exhaustion of the lifetime prove the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional classification from a finite-variation initial tangent]
\label{thm:fv-tangent-rigidity}
Suppose a Borel family on all relevant marked slit states has strong DMP
and source locality, including the same mirrored test deletions (DR6).
Suppose canonical decompositions (FV1), compatible with conditional restart
in the inherited chordal charts, obey (FV2). If $B(f)$ is locally bounded
in inverse-chart topology, or just satisfies the paired slit bound (DR11)
at every state, the family is chordal SLE$_6$ in these coordinates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:fv-initial-covariance} gives exactly (DR4), hence
the reflected-slit sum and difference argument gives $B(f)=0$, $V(f)=6$
at every deterministic state.

At a stopping time $\tau$, the centered future driver is the driver of
the remaining physical state in the inherited conformal chart. Its
capacity parameter is exactly $s=t-\tau$ in the original half-plane
coordinate: recentering by $W_\tau$ introduces no additional scale clock.
Strong DMP and conditional-characteristic compatibility identify its
canonical finite-variation part with $A_{\tau+s}-A_\tau$ under the
regular conditional law. Applying (FV2) with $B=0$, and integrating these
conditional probabilities over the random state, gives (FV6).
Lemma~\ref{lem:fv-zero-stopping-tangents} therefore makes $A=0$.

The same conditional restart argument gives
$(C_{\tau+h}-C_\tau)/h\to6$ in probability at every stopping time.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:fv-zero-stopping-tangents} to the continuous
finite-variation process $C_t-6t$: it is identically zero.
Thus $W=M$ and $\langle M\rangle_t=6t$ globally.
L\'evy's characterization and continuous Loewner generation identify SLE$_6$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Strict weakening and remaining limitation.}
Even an absolutely continuous drift with density
$b_s=B+\sin(1/s)$, $s>0$, has no right limit at zero, whereas
$\int_0^t b_sds=Bt+O(t^2)$ and therefore satisfies (FV2).
The theorem also allows an a priori singular finite-variation drift;
its exclusion is a conclusion, not an assumption. A claim that an actual
exploration family has these initial characteristic tangents remains
unproved. The theorem still depends on choosing one correct physical
conformal structure and on tame behavior under the source slit tests.

\subsection{The wrong-coordinate obstruction also only needs finite-variation tangents}

\paragraph{Scaling lemma with probability tangents.}
The exact-scaling lemma (SM1)--(SM4) remains true if its drift assumption
is replaced by continuous finite variation and (FV2), and its bracket
assumption by $C_t/t\to v$ in probability for deterministic $v$.
Indeed (FV2) makes
$\sup_{s\le T}|c^{-1/2}A_{cs}|=O_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt c)\to0$.
Since $C$ is increasing, a finite deterministic mesh and monotonic
squeezing show
\[
 \sup_{s\le T}|C_{cs}/c-vs|\longrightarrow0
           \quad\hbox{in probability}.
\]
The continuous-martingale functional central limit argument in the earlier
scaling lemma then applies unchanged. Exact scaling makes the whole law
Brownian with variance $v$.

\paragraph{Local transfer of the tangent.}
In Proposition~\ref{prop:fv-initial-covariance}, the analytic-field
argument actually gives the uniform version
\[
 \frac1t\sup_{s\le t}
 \left|\int_0^s h_r'(W_r)\,dA_r-dB(f)s\right|
          \longrightarrow0
          \quad\hbox{in probability}.
\]
Use the martingale maximal inequality in its proof, and the same uniform
initial bound for $A$. Monotonic squeezing similarly makes
$C_{ts}/t\to V(f)s$ uniformly on compact $s$ intervals in probability.
Equation (FV5) therefore transfers (FV2) through a local analytic chart
change, once the initial bracket tangent has been obtained; it also
transfers its inverse, since the first chart derivative is positive.
No total-variation rate bound is introduced by this transfer.

\begin{corollary}[Wrong-affine obstruction under finite-variation tangents]
\label{cor:fv-wrong-affine}
Consider the canonical fixed-affine SLE$_6$ family with matrix $A$ on all
bounded marked domains. If every Euclidean chart driver admits (FV1)--(FV2),
then $A$ is a Euclidean similarity. No local boundedness of $B(f)$ is needed.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Choose the bounded flat-source domain and one test deletion used in the
bounded-domain obstruction theorem. Proposition~\ref{prop:fv-initial-covariance}
gives its deterministic bracket tangent in probability. Couple its initial
physical trace by known affine-SLE locality to the canonical half-plane
affine-SLE trace. The local analytic chart transfer just proved gives a
finite-variation decomposition and uniform deterministic drift tangent for
the half-plane driver, and the same deterministic bracket tangent.
Its exact Brownian scaling and the extended scaling lemma make its law
ordinary Brownian driving. The boundary-swallowing-race and affine
identification arguments in the half-plane obstruction theorem then give
variance six and force $A$ to be a similarity.
\end{proof}


% Independently checked extension of the finite-variation theorem.
\subsection{A tightness-in-law variant without deterministic initial tangents}

\paragraph{Proposition (proved).}
There is a useful alternative formulation of the preceding theorem.
Allow (FV2) to have a finite random tangent $\beta^f$ rather than a
deterministic $B(f)$. Assume that for every fixed chart $f$ the family of
initial tangents in its two shrinking slit domains is uniformly tight:
\[
 \lim_{K\to\infty}\sup_{0<\epsilon<\epsilon_0}
 \max_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}}
 \mathbb P\{|\beta^{f_\epsilon^\sigma}|>K\}=0.
 \tag{FV8}
\]
Then the same classification follows, with all other family and restart
hypotheses unchanged.

The analytic-field lemma and initial covariance proof apply verbatim to
finite random tangents, producing an intrinsic finite random bracket
tangent $V^f$. For each fixed $\epsilon$, source locality and the
invertible local coordinate changes put the original and both deleted
initial traces on one probability space. Only their initial germs are
needed, so no simultaneous extension of this coupling past their exit times
is required. On that space the covariances give
\[
 \beta^{f_\epsilon^+}+\beta^{f_\epsilon^-}
       =2\beta^f/d_\epsilon,
 \qquad
 \beta^{f_\epsilon^+}-\beta^{f_\epsilon^-}
       =(V^f-6)e_\epsilon^+/d_\epsilon^2.
\]
Uniform tightness of the two marginals makes their sum and difference
uniformly tight under any coupling. Since $d_\epsilon\to0$ and
$|e_\epsilon^+|/d_\epsilon^2\to\infty$, these identities force
$\beta^f=0$ and $V^f=6$ almost surely. Formally, for $a>0$,
\[
 \mathbb P\{|\beta^f|>a\}
 \le \sum_{\sigma=\pm}
       \mathbb P\{|\beta^{f_\epsilon^\sigma}|>a/d_\epsilon\}
       \longrightarrow0,
\]
and the identical argument with threshold
$a|e_\epsilon^+|/(2d_\epsilon^2)$ gives $V^f=6$.
The stopping-time lemma then kills the finite-variation drift and identifies
the bracket as before. Local boundedness of a deterministic drift tangent
is one special case of (FV8); no deterministic initial-tangent premise is
otherwise necessary for this version.


% Candidate addendum: canonical characteristics do not need a separate
% restart assumption once strong DMP is supplied in the natural filtration.
\section{Canonical characteristics under a genuine strong restart law}
\label{sec:canonical-restart-automatic}

The strong DMP required above is substantive. Once it is an identity of
regular conditional laws in the usual natural driver filtration, however,
compatibility of the continuous canonical characteristics follows from
uniqueness. It is useful to separate these two issues.

\begin{proposition}[Strong DMP transports continuous characteristics]
\label{prop:automatic-characteristics}
Let $\{P_f\}$ be a Borel family of laws on
$C_0([0,\infty),\mathbb R)$. In each state the coordinate process has a
continuous canonical decomposition $W=M^f+A^f$ in its usual natural
filtration, where $A^f$ has finite variation on compact intervals. Write
$C^f=\langle M^f\rangle$. Fix an initial state $f$ and a bounded stopping
time $\tau$. Suppose $f_\tau$ is measurable at $\tau$ and the strong restart
identity is literally
\[
 \mathcal L_{P_f}\bigl((W_{\tau+t}-W_\tau)_{t\ge0}
                  \mid\mathcal F^f_\tau\bigr)=P_{f_\tau}.
 \tag{KR1}
\]
Then, for almost every conditioned history, the shifted finite-variation
process $A^f_{\tau+t}-A^f_\tau$ and bracket
$C^f_{\tau+t}-C^f_\tau$ are the canonical characteristics of the restarted
law. This is equality up to indistinguishability under that conditional
law. No continuity of $f\mapsto P_f$ is required.

The same conclusion applies to an inherited Loewner chart when the future
coordinate in (KR1) is exactly that inherited driving process. Changing to
a different chart or clock still requires the appropriate invertible
adapted coordinate transformation.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write $P=P_f$, $M=M^f$, $A=A^f$, and $C=C^f$.
The continuous adapted processes $M,A,C$ have raw Borel adapted versions,
up to one $P$-evanescent set. Indeed, at a positive time approximate from
strictly earlier rational times; a variable measurable in the usual
filtration at $r<t$ has a version measurable in the raw filtration at
some time strictly between $r$ and $t$. Make these choices on the
countable rational approximation grid and take the continuous limits.
Their values at zero are fixed. On exceptional paths retain the raw
adapted pathwise-limit definitions, rather than zeroing a nonadapted
exceptional set. After conditioning, freeze the stopped past, $\tau$,
and any additional information in $\mathcal F_\tau$. Thus the
shifted processes are adapted Borel functionals of the future coordinate
$U_t=W_{\tau+t}-W_\tau$ alone.

Choose stopping times $\sigma_n\uparrow\infty$ such that
$M^{\sigma_n}$ and $(M^2-C)^{\sigma_n}$ are bounded martingales; for
example stop at time $n$ or at the first time $|M|+C$ reaches $n$.
For rational $0\le r<s$ and a bounded cylinder test $H$ of $U$ at
rational times at most $r$, optional sampling and the tower property give
\[
 \mathbb E_P\!\left[
   \bigl(M_{(\tau+s)\wedge\sigma_n}
        -M_{(\tau+r)\wedge\sigma_n}\bigr)H
       \mid\mathcal F^f_\tau\right]=0.
 \tag{KR2}
\]
Use a countable cylinder class, for example indicators of finite
intersections of rational half-lines. Disintegrating all these identities
at once gives, outside one null set of histories, their martingale
identities under the conditional future law. Monotone classes and
continuity extend them to every time and bounded past test. The bounded
martingales remain martingales after usual augmentation by conditioning
at times decreasing to the desired time.

Under any such conditional law put
$T_n=(\sigma_n-\tau)^+$. The stopped shifted martingale
$M_{\tau+t\wedge T_n}-M_\tau$ is the process in (KR2) with its initial
value subtracted: if $\sigma_n\le\tau$ both are identically zero; otherwise
they coincide. The $T_n$ are stopping times in the conditional future
natural filtration, increase to infinity, and hence
$M_{\tau+t}-M_\tau$ is a continuous local martingale there. The same
argument applied to $M^2-C$, subtracting the frozen cross term
$2M_\tau(M_{\tau+t}-M_\tau)$, identifies its bracket as
$C_{\tau+t}-C_\tau$.

The shifted $A$ is adapted, continuous, and of finite variation, so it is
predictable. Consequently
\[
 U_t=(M_{\tau+t}-M_\tau)+(A_{\tau+t}-A_\tau)
\]
is a canonical decomposition in the conditional future natural
filtration. By (KR1) this is the usual natural filtration of the
coordinate process under $P_{f_\tau}$. Uniqueness of the continuous
canonical decomposition and bracket proves the claim. A separate
jointly measurable choice of characteristics over every deterministic
state is unnecessary for this almost-every-history conclusion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Consequence for the driving criteria.}
Under the genuine strong restart identity (KR1), the explicit
canonical-restart-compatibility premises in the earlier conditional
driving theorems are redundant. The locality identification of natural
trace filtrations and the existence of the strong conditional kernels
remain necessary premises of those arguments. This reduction does not
deduce strong DMP from deterministic-domain convergence.

\subsection{Pointwise convergence, even of continuous kernels, is insufficient}

Here is a small counterexample to an otherwise tempting kernel-transfer
argument. Let $V$ take the values $-1,1$ with equal probability and put
$I_n=V$, $D_n=V/n$, and $Y_n=V$. Choose continuous functions
$g_n:\mathbb R\to[-1,1]$ supported in the union of intervals of radius
$1/(4n^2)$ around $-1/n$ and $1/n$, with
$g_n(1/n)=1$ and $g_n(-1/n)=-1$. Define
$K_n(d)=\delta_{g_n(d)}$. These are weakly continuous probability kernels,
and for every fixed deterministic $d$,
\[
 K_n(d)\longrightarrow\delta_0=:K(d).
 \tag{KR3}
\]
The limiting kernel $K$ is continuous. Moreover the exact conditional
identity $\mathcal L(Y_n\mid I_n,D_n)=K_n(D_n)$ holds. Nevertheless
$(I_n,D_n,Y_n)\to(V,0,V)$ almost surely, and
$\mathcal L(V\mid V,0)=\delta_V\ne K(0)$. Thus pointwise convergence
at every deterministic state, and continuity of every kernel including
the limit, do not justify a conditional limit at random states. Uniform
equicontinuity, sequential convergence along the actually visited
states, or another quantitative substitute is essential.

\begin{lemma}[An annealed substitute for sequential kernel convergence]
\label{lem:annealed-kernel-transfer}
Let the past, state, and future variables take values in fixed Polish
spaces. Suppose $(I_n,D_n,Y_n)\Rightarrow(I,D,Y)$ and
$\mathcal L(Y_n\mid I_n,D_n)=K_n(D_n)$. Suppose $K$ is a Borel kernel
such that $d\mapsto K\varphi(d)$ is continuous at $D$ almost surely,
for each member $\varphi$ of a countable bounded continuous
convergence-determining class on the future space. It suffices that
\[
 \mathbb E\bigl|K_n\varphi(D_n)-K\varphi(D_n)\bigr|
       \longrightarrow0
 \tag{KR4}
\]
for each such $\varphi$. Then
$\mathcal L(Y\mid I,D)=K(D)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For any bounded continuous test $h$ of $(I_n,D_n)$, replace
$K_n\varphi(D_n)$ in the exact finite-$n$ conditional identity by
$K\varphi(D_n)$. The error is bounded by $\|h\|_\infty$ times (KR4).
The continuous mapping theorem, with its almost-sure-continuity version,
then passes the resulting identity to the limit. A monotone class in
$h$, followed by the countable measure-determining class in $\varphi$,
identifies the conditional law.
\end{proof}

Condition (KR4) only asks for a stability estimate on domains sampled by
the exploration. It can therefore be more economical than a theorem
uniform over every rough slit domain. It has not been proved here for
percolation explorations. For strong DMP it must hold for the relevant
stopping times, or be combined with an independent strong-Markov
extension theorem.


\section{Rigidity from conditional first-moment bounds}
\label{sec:conditional-bias}

\paragraph{Scope.}
The next criterion replaces the a priori semimartingale and initial-tangent
premises by conditional first-moment bounds. It does not derive those bounds
from RSW. They must hold in one fixed physical conformal structure, with the stated control
along the specified shrinking slit tests. No quadratic-moment bound or
initial drift derivative is assumed.

\begin{lemma}[Conditional bias produces a bounded drift density]
\label{lem:cbias-doobmeyer}
Let $X$ be a bounded continuous adapted real process, with $X_0=0$, in a
usual filtration. Suppose a finite deterministic $K$ satisfies
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[X_t-X_s\mid\mathcal F_s]\right|
       \le K(t-s) \quad\hbox{almost surely},\qquad 0\le s\le t.
 \tag{BI1}
\]
Then $X=M+\int_0^\cdot b_rdr$, where $M$ is a continuous local martingale
and $b$ is predictable with $|b|\le K$ almost everywhere.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
On a fixed finite time interval, $X_t+Kt$ and $-X_t+Kt$ are class-D
submartingales. Their Doob--Meyer decompositions have increasing predictable
parts $A^+$ and $A^-$. Adding the decompositions and using uniqueness gives
$A^++A^-=2Kt$. Thus $A^+$ and $A^-$ are absolutely continuous, with
densities in $[0,2K]$. The decomposition of $X$ has drift density
$b=dA^+/dt-K\in[-K,K]$. Since $X$ and its drift are continuous, its
martingale part is continuous. Uniqueness patches the decompositions over
finite time intervals.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Controlled rate bounds force the tangents to exist]
\label{prop:cbias-derived-tangents}
Use the mirrored shrinking slits $A_\epsilon^\pm$ in (DR6), with initial
derivatives $d_\epsilon$ and $e_\epsilon^\pm$ in (DR9). Assume the driver
in a state $f$, and the drivers in all these deleted states, have canonical
continuous-semimartingale decompositions with absolutely continuous drift.
In each state the drift density is bounded on some strictly positive
initial stopping interval. The interval may depend on the state and sample.
Let $K_\epsilon<\infty$ be a deterministic bound common to the two
deleted states. If $d_\epsilon K_\epsilon\to0$ along a sequence tending
to zero, then in the original state
\[
 \frac1t\sup_{s\le t}|A_s|\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \frac{C_t}{t}\longrightarrow6
 \quad\hbox{almost surely as }t\downarrow0.
 \tag{BI2}
\]
Here $A$ is canonical drift and $C$ is the martingale bracket. No right
continuity or initial tangent of the drift densities is presumed.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By locality, compare the original and each deleted driver using the same
trace filtration and the stopping-time capacity clocks. With
$p_t^\sigma=(h_t^\sigma)'(W_t)$ and
$q_t^\sigma=(h_t^\sigma)''(W_t)$, uniqueness of canonical
characteristics gives
\[
 \frac12q_t^\sigma(dC_t-6dt)
   =\bigl[(p_t^\sigma)^2\widehat b^\sigma_{u_\sigma(t)}
                    -p_t^\sigma b_t\bigr]dt.
 \tag{BI3}
\]
One fixed nonzero-$q_0$ test makes $C$ absolutely continuous on a positive
initial interval; write $dC_t=v_tdt$ there. For each fixed $\epsilon$, both
mirrored identities hold almost everywhere on a positive initial interval,
as a two-by-two system in $b_t$ and $(v_t-6)/2$. Its coefficients converge
as $t\downarrow0$ to
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}d_\epsilon&e_\epsilon^+\\
                 d_\epsilon&-e_\epsilon^+\end{pmatrix},
\]
whose determinant is nonzero. The right sides are
$(p_t^\sigma)^2\widehat b^\sigma_{u_\sigma(t)}$, with each transformed
density bounded by $K_\epsilon$. Continuity of the coefficients and matrix
inversion therefore give the pathwise bounds
\[
 \mathop{\rm ess\,limsup}_{t\downarrow0}|b_t|\le d_\epsilon K_\epsilon,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\rm ess\,limsup}_{t\downarrow0}|v_t-6|
       \le \frac{2d_\epsilon^2K_\epsilon}{|e_\epsilon^+|}.
 \tag{BI4}
\]
These assertions concern the same original densities. Intersect the
probability-one sets for a countable sequence $\epsilon\downarrow0$.
By assumption $d_\epsilon K_\epsilon\to0$; also
$d_\epsilon/|e_\epsilon^+|\to0$, so both right sides tend to zero. Integrating the resulting essential right limits
proves (BI2). The positive comparison intervals may shrink with
