$\epsilon$: the essential limsup is taken at fixed $\epsilon$ first.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[SLE from controlled conditional driving bias]
\label{thm:cbias-rigidity}
Let a Borel exploration family have continuous Loewner drivers on all
$t\ge0$, source locality including the test deletions, and strong DMP.
Require locality to identify the completed natural trace filtrations via
the invertible capacity clocks. Require strong DMP to be the regular-conditional-law identity (KR1)
for the inherited driver in its usual natural filtration.
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-characteristics} then supplies canonical
characteristic compatibility; it is not a separate premise. Suppose that, in every
deterministic state $f$, there is an increasing sequence of stopping times
$\rho_{f,m}$ increasing almost surely to infinity, with $\rho_{f,1}>0$ almost surely,
such that each $X^{f,m}_t=W^f_{t\wedge\rho_{f,m}}$ is bounded and
satisfies (BI1), with a finite deterministic bound $K(f,m)$. Require
\[
 \lim_{n\to\infty}\epsilon_n
          \max_{\sigma=\pm}K(f_{\epsilon_n}^\sigma,1)=0
 \tag{BI5}
\]
at every state, for some sequence $\epsilon_n\downarrow0$. In particular uniformly
bounded constants suffice; growth $o(\epsilon_n^{-1})$ is allowed.
Then the family is chordal SLE$_6$ in these coordinates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:cbias-doobmeyer}, applied to the localizing sequence, gives
global continuous-semimartingale decompositions with locally bounded
absolutely continuous drift. Uniqueness patches the decompositions on
overlapping stopped intervals. Proposition~\ref{prop:cbias-derived-tangents}
then gives the zero drift tangent and bracket tangent six in every state.

Use the conditional-kernel compatibility of canonical decompositions in
the inherited natural trace filtration, as in the earlier conditional
characteristic theorems. At every
bounded stopping time $\tau$, conditional DMP and (BI2) give
\[
 \frac{A_{\tau+h}-A_\tau}{h}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \frac{C_{\tau+h}-C_\tau}{h}\longrightarrow6
 \quad\hbox{in probability}.
\]
The zero-stopping-tangent lemma
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fv-zero-stopping-tangents}) applied to $A$ and $C-6t$
gives $A=0$ and $C_t=6t$. L\'evy's characterization identifies the
driver as $\sqrt6 B$, hence the trace as SLE$_6$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Rates in the slit test.}
Since $d_\epsilon\sim\epsilon/2$ and
$|e_\epsilon^+|\sim1/(2\epsilon)$, the two bounds in (BI4)
are of orders $\epsilon K_\epsilon$ and
$\epsilon^3 K_\epsilon$. The weaker condition
$\epsilon^3K_\epsilon\to0$ alone forces the initial bracket tangent
to be six. The full classification above also needs the stronger drift
condition in (BI5).

\paragraph{What this reduction gains and what it leaves open.}
Conditional bias bounds concern first moments of observable driver
increments, rather than unknown canonical decompositions. They produce
semimartingality, bounded drift densities, and the missing initial tangents
without assuming quadratic variation estimates. They are nevertheless
substantial cancellation estimates: a bound on
$\mathbb E|W_{t+h}-W_t|$ of Brownian order $\sqrt h$, or tightness from
RSW, does not imply (BI1). The rate control in (BI5) is coordinate sensitive
and is not justified by convergence of curve laws in the shrinking slit
domains. Thus this is a conditional route toward the desired regularity,
not a proof of percolation universality.

% Research addendum; intended for inclusion after the conditional-bias theorem.
\subsection{An observable discrete criterion that passes to curve limits}
\label{sec:discrete-bias-transfer}

There is no need to identify the discrete or limiting canonical
characteristics in order to test the preceding criterion. Signed
conditional first moments on a discrete exploration clock suffice.
The following elementary transfer also avoids convergence of filtrations.
It does not establish the separate strong-DMP premise of the rigidity
theorem.

\begin{proposition}[Discrete conditional bias passes to the natural limit filtration]
\label{prop:discrete-bias-transfer}
Fix $T>0$. For each $n$, let
$0=t^n_0<t^n_1<\cdots<t^n_{N_n}=T$ be a deterministic partition
with maximum spacing tending to zero. Let $X^n$ be its continuous
linear interpolation of grid values, all bounded by one deterministic
constant $L$. Suppose the grid values are adapted to filtrations
$(\mathcal G^n_j)_j$ and
\[
 \left|\mathbb E\left[X^n_{t^n_{j+1}}-X^n_{t^n_j}
                         \mid\mathcal G^n_j\right]\right|
 \le (K+\delta_n)(t^n_{j+1}-t^n_j),\qquad\delta_n\downarrow0.
 \tag{DB1}
\]
Suppose $X^n$ converges in distribution in $C([0,T])$ to $X$.
Then in the usual augmentation of the natural filtration of $X$,
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[X_t-X_s\mid\mathcal F_s]\right|
       \le K(t-s),\qquad 0\le s\le t\le T.
 \tag{DB2}
\]
Consequently $X$ has a continuous local-martingale part and an
absolutely continuous canonical drift with density bounded by $K$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Summing the conditional inequalities and using the tower property
shows that the grid processes
$X^n_{t^n_j}\pm(K+\delta_n)t^n_j$ are respectively a submartingale
and a supermartingale. In particular, for grid points $s_n\le t_n$
and every bounded nonnegative $\mathcal G^n_{j(s_n)}$-measurable $H_n$,
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[(X^n_{t_n}-X^n_{s_n})H_n]\right|
       \le (K+\delta_n)(t_n-s_n)\mathbb E H_n.
 \tag{DB3}
\]
Choose grid points $s_n\to s$ and $t_n\to t$, with $s_n\le t_n$,
and set $H_n=H(X^n_{\cdot\wedge s_n})$, where $H$ is any bounded
nonnegative continuous function on $C([0,T])$.
The stopped interpolated path is measurable at the grid time $s_n$.
Convergence in $C([0,T])$ and uniform continuity of each limiting
path give joint convergence of the evaluations and stopped paths.
The deterministic bound $L$ permits passage of their expectations to
the limit in (DB3).

Bounded continuous tests determine finite measures on the stopped-path
space. Thus the resulting two inequalities extend to all bounded
nonnegative tests measurable in the raw natural filtration at $s$.
This proves (DB2) there. Equivalently $X_t+Kt$ and $-X_t+Kt$ are
continuous class-D submartingales. These remain submartingales under
the usual augmentation: for $u\downarrow s$, apply the inequality
at $u<t$, then use reverse-martingale convergence and continuity of
$X$ to condition on $\bigcap_{u>s}\mathcal F_u^{\rm raw}$.
Completion changes only null sets. Lemma~\ref{lem:cbias-doobmeyer}
now supplies the asserted canonical decomposition.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Exploration-time variant.}
An equivalent usable sufficient hypothesis allows a random discrete
clock. Let the successive capacity times $t^n_j$ and grid values
$X^n_j$ be adapted to the discrete revealed-color filtration, with
uniform deterministic bounds on the drivers and clock horizons. Assume
the times are nondecreasing, interpolation is well defined, and
$J_n(s)=\inf\{j:t^n_j\ge s\}<\infty$ almost surely for each $s<T$.
Suppose their largest spacing tends to zero in probability and
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[X^n_{j+1}-X^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j]\right|
 \le (K+\delta_n)
       \mathbb E[t^n_{j+1}-t^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j].
 \tag{DB4}
\]
After continuous interpolation, assume the same uniform-path convergence.
Then (DB2) again follows. Indeed $X^n_j\pm(K+\delta_n)t^n_j$
are bounded discrete sub/supermartingales. Use optional sampling at
$J_n(s)=\inf\{j:t^n_j\ge s\}$ and $J_n(t)$, with a test
of the path stopped at $t^n_{J_n(s)}$. The indices are stopping times
because $\{J_n(s)\le j\}=\{t^n_j\ge s\}$.
The clock overshoots tend to zero in probability; tightness in the
continuous-path topology makes the stopped paths and sampled values
converge to their deterministic-time counterparts. Boundedness then
passes the inequalities to the limit as above. At $T$, use continuity
from times below $T$.

\paragraph{Fixed-interval block estimates also suffice.}
The per-reveal condition (DB4) is sufficient, not necessary for this
transfer argument. It is enough that for each fixed $s<t$ and every
bounded nonnegative continuous stopped-path test $H$, the tested
inequalities (DB3), using approximations of $s,t$, hold up to an error
$o(1)\|H\|_\infty$. The same weak-convergence proof then gives
(DB2). Thus one may instead seek signed cancellation over mesoscopic
blocks, allowing microscopic biases to cancel before a fixed capacity
interval. No per-reveal estimate is asserted for lattice percolation.

Thus a possible lattice-level research target is the signed cancellation
estimate (DB4), after suitable bounded stopping, in an unknown but fixed
affine normalization. The constants must satisfy the shrinking-slit
control in Theorem~\ref{thm:cbias-rigidity}, and the limit must separately
have its stated strong-DMP and conditional-characteristic compatibility.
Neither a Brownian-size absolute increment estimate nor RSW alone is
being substituted for this signed conditional estimate.


\section{Microscopic correctors for signed driving cancellation}
\label{sec:corrected-bias}

The per-reveal condition (DB4) is sensitive to bounded microscopic
oscillations which disappear in the continuum limit. The following
criterion permits exactly this kind of cancellation. It supplies neither
a corrector for a percolation exploration nor the required slit-uniform
bounds; those remain mathematical tasks.

\begin{proposition}[Vanishing-corrector transfer on an exploration clock]
\label{prop:corrector-bias-transfer}
Fix a deterministic horizon $T$. In the random-clock setting of (DB4),
assume $|X^n_j|\le L$, $t^n_0=0$, $0\le t^n_j\le T_*$, every level $s<T$ is
reached almost surely, and the maximum clock spacing tends to zero in
probability. Require zero clock increments to have zero driver increments,
so continuous interpolation is well defined. Suppose the interpolated
$X^n$ converge in law in $C([0,T])$ to $X$.
Let $R^n_j$ be adapted real correctors with
$\mathbb E\sup_j|R^n_j|<\infty$ for every $n$, such that
\[
 \mathbb E\sup_j|R^n_j|\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{MC1}
\]
Assume also zero clock increments have zero corrector increments.
Set $Y^n_j=X^n_j+R^n_j$ and suppose for $K<\infty$ and
$\delta_n\downarrow0$ that
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[Y^n_{j+1}-Y^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j]\right|
 \le (K+\delta_n)
       \mathbb E[t^n_{j+1}-t^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j].
 \tag{MC2}
\]
Then $X$ satisfies (DB2) in its usual natural filtration. In particular its
canonical drift is absolutely continuous with density bounded by $K$.
If $K=0$, $X$ is a continuous martingale.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The grid processes $Y^n_j+(K+\delta_n)t^n_j$ and
$-Y^n_j+(K+\delta_n)t^n_j$ are submartingales. For each fixed $n$,
their absolute values at all stopping indices are dominated by the
integrable variable $L+\sup_j|R^n_j|+(K+\delta_n)T_*$.
Consequently optional sampling applies to the almost surely finite
indices, for $0\le s<t<T$, $J_n(s)=\inf\{j:t^n_j\ge s\}$ and $J_n(t)$, even when
they have no deterministic upper bound. For every bounded nonnegative
$\mathcal G^n_{J_n(s)}$-measurable test $H_n$, it gives
\[
 \left|\mathbb E[(Y^n_{J_n(t)}-Y^n_{J_n(s)})H_n]\right|
 \le(K+\delta_n)
       \mathbb E[(t^n_{J_n(t)}-t^n_{J_n(s)})H_n].
\]
Replace $Y^n$ by $X^n$. The resulting error is at most
$2\|H_n\|_\infty\mathbb E\sup_j|R^n_j|=o(1)\|H_n\|_\infty$.
Take $H_n=H(X^n_{\cdot\wedge t^n_{J_n(s)}})$ for a bounded
nonnegative continuous stopped-path test $H$. Clock overshoots vanish
in probability, and tightness in $C([0,T])$ identifies the limiting
sampled values and stopped paths. The bound on $X^n$ and on the clock
then passes their expectations to the limit and yields
\[
 |\mathbb E[(X_t-X_s)H(X_{\cdot\wedge s})]|
 \le K(t-s)\mathbb E H(X_{\cdot\wedge s}).
\]
As in Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete-bias-transfer}, bounded continuous
tests determine the corresponding finite signed measures, giving (DB2)
in the raw natural filtration. Continuity and reverse-martingale
convergence extend it to the usual augmentation. The endpoint $T$
follows by continuity, and Lemma~\ref{lem:cbias-doobmeyer} identifies the
canonical drift.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A finite-state cell problem.}
There is a useful sufficient construction of such a corrector. For
simplicity let $t^n_j=jh_n^2$ and let $E^n_j$ be an adapted process in
a fixed finite set $\mathcal E$ whose conditional transition matrix given
$\mathcal G^n_j$ is a fixed irreducible stochastic matrix $P$.
Suppose
\[
 \mathbb E[X^n_{j+1}-X^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j]
       =h_n b(E^n_j)+h_n^2r^n_j,\qquad |r^n_j|\le K.
 \tag{MC3}
\]
Let $\pi$ be the unique invariant probability of $P$. If
$\sum_e\pi(e)b(e)=0$, the finite linear system
\[
 (I-P)\chi=b
 \tag{MC4}
\]
has a solution $\chi$, unique up to an additive constant. This follows
because the kernel of $I-P$ is the constants and the image is the
$\pi$-mean-zero hyperplane; no aperiodicity assumption is needed.
The adapted corrector $R^n_j=h_n\chi(E^n_j)$ then obeys (MC1), and
\[
 \mathbb E[\Delta(X^n+R^n)_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j]
 =h_n\{b+P\chi-\chi\}(E^n_j)+h_n^2r^n_j
 =h_n^2r^n_j.
\]
Thus (MC2) holds. The same computation remains valid before an adapted
stopping index, provided both the driver and corrector are frozen there. The capacity
clock may continue after this stopping index, with zero corrected
driver increments.
For a random clock the right side of (MC3) can instead be a residual
bounded by $K\mathbb E[\Delta t^n_j\mid\mathcal G^n_j]$.

This is only a finite-state sufficient model. The local conformal
geometry of an explored percolation boundary is not represented by the
tip orientation alone. No closure to a fixed finite $P$, and no solution
of a percolation cell problem, is asserted.

\begin{remark}[Raw one-step bias may diverge for Brownian approximants]
\label{ex:oscillatory-bias}
Let $Z_t=\sqrt6 B_{t\wedge\tau}$, where
$\tau=\inf\{t:|\sqrt6 B_t|=L\}$, and let $h_n\downarrow0$.
At the deterministic grid times $t_j^n=jh_n^2$, put
\[
 X^n_j=Z_{t^n_j}+h_n\{(-1)^j-1\}.
 \tag{MC5}
\]
With the Brownian grid filtration, $X^n_0=0$ and the conditional signed
increment is exactly $-2h_n(-1)^j$. Hence any (DB4) constant must satisfy
$K_n\ge2/h_n\to\infty$. Nevertheless $|X^n_j|\le L+2h_n$ and
continuous interpolation converges uniformly on compact time intervals,
almost surely, to $Z$. The corrector
$R^n_j=h_n\{1-(-1)^j\}$ has supremum at most $2h_n$ and makes
$X^n+R^n$ an exact grid martingale. On a fixed positive horizon the sum
of the absolute one-step predictable biases diverges like $h_n^{-1}$,
while their cumulative signed sum stays bounded by $2h_n$.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Implication for the universality reduction.}
A percolation proof need not bound the raw per-reveal conditional bias.
It would suffice to construct an adapted corrector satisfying (MC1)
and the corrected estimate (MC2), in one fixed affine normalization.
The resulting constants must still satisfy the shrinking-slit condition
(BI5), and strong continuation and characteristic compatibility remain
separate requirements of Theorem~\ref{thm:cbias-rigidity}.
The example proves only a logical obstruction to treating (DB4) as a
necessary property of approximating drivers; it is not a counterexample
to (DB4) for a particular percolation lattice.


\section{Strong source locality inside the affine-SLE ansatz}
\label{sec:strong-local-affine}

\paragraph{Scope and revised axiom.}
Here locality means agreement of exploration laws up to exiting any common
source neighborhood, including agreement of the boundary colors there; the
targets and the domains outside the neighborhood may differ. This is stronger
than the earlier target-preserving deletion axiom. All statements below use
the revised version. We investigate the deterministic affine-SLE marginal
ansatz, not arbitrary exploration laws.

For each marked domain $S=(D,p,q)$ suppose
\[
 P_S=(A_S^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(A_SD,A_Sp,A_Sq),
 \qquad A_S\in\mathrm{GL}^+(2,\mathbb R).
 \tag{AF1}
\]
Let $T(S)$ be the determinant-one representative of
$[A_S^{\mathsf T}A_S]$. The metric-identification results elsewhere in the
record make this independent of the choice of representing matrix.
When invoking the domain Markov property we assume (AF1) on the resulting
rough slit states as well, not merely on initially regular domains.

\subsection{Exact freedom remaining without the domain Markov property}

An embedded colored source germ is the germ of $(D,p)$ at the source,
including the white and black boundary sides. At a regular source it may
be represented by the physical boundary curve and its interior side in
an arbitrarily small disk. Denote it by $G_s(D,p)$.

\begin{proposition}[Strong locality leaves arbitrary regular-source germ metrics]
\label{prop:sl-germfreedom}
On bounded piecewise real-analytic Jordan domains with regular source,
strong locality forces $T(D,p,q)$ to depend only on the colored embedded
source germ. Translation covariance makes this germ function invariant
under translation. Conversely, every measurable translation-invariant
choice of a positive-definite projective tensor from the source germ
produces, through (AF1), a translation-covariant affine-SLE family with
strong locality. No domain Markov assertion is part of this converse.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
If two triples have the same regular source germ, strong locality identifies
the initial laws stopped inside a common source disk. Blow up at the regular
source. The limiting laws are affine images of half-plane SLE$_6$ from
$0$ to $\infty$. Strict monotonicity of the left-passage probability makes
the two affine tensors proportional, hence their determinant-one forms
equal. In particular the target and the remote domain cannot change $T$.
Translation covariance gives the corresponding equality for translated
germs, by the same local identifiability.

Conversely, choose a canonical matrix from the tensor, for example its
positive-definite square root after determinant normalization. Equal source
germs use the same matrix. Applying it to two domains that agree near their
source gives transformed domains with the same colored source neighborhood.
The usual strong locality/target-independence property of SLE$_6$ couples
its initial paths there. Pull back the coupling. Translation covariance
follows from the assumed invariance of the tensor assignment.
\end{proof}

Thus strong locality and translations alone do \emph{not} give a fixed metric,
even inside the affine-SLE ansatz. This is an exact obstruction, rather than
a gap in a proposed proof.

\subsection{An explicit source-germ example, and a direct proof that DMP excludes it}

Fix $a>0$, $a\ne1$. At a source germ locally equal to a half-plane with
boundary line $\ell$, assign
\[
 A_s=\Pi_\ell+a\Pi_{\ell^\perp};
 \tag{AF2}
\]
at every other source germ assign $A_s=I$. Here locally equal means a genuine
full embedded end neighborhood with straight boundary, not an artificial
crosscut drawn through the interior. This rule is translation covariant and
is even covariant under Euclidean similarities. It yields strong locality
by the preceding proposition and the identical-germ argument on arbitrary
admissible source ends.
It has no fixed affine normalizer: sources on horizontal and vertical
flat arcs have tensors $\operatorname{diag}(1,a^2)$ and
$\operatorname{diag}(a^2,1)$, which are not proportional. Regular-source
half-plane identifiability already proves this on two sources of a rectangle.

The failure of DMP can be proved without any unestablished claim about the
fractal regularity of a later hull boundary.

\begin{lemma}[A fresh horizontal-level tip is not a flat source germ]
\label{lem:sl-freshlevel}
Let a continuous curve in a rectangular domain start below a horizontal
line $L=\{y=c\}$ and stop at its first hit $z$ of $L$, with $z$ in the
interior of the rectangle. Suppose the target is above $L$. Then the target
component of the slit domain is not locally equal to a half-plane at $z$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The pre-stopping trace lies below $L$ and meets $L$ only at $z$. The entire
upper part of the rectangle is connected to the target and avoids the trace,
so it survives in the target component. Every point of $L$ other than $z$
also survives: the compact trace stays a positive distance from each such
point, which can be joined vertically to the upper part. Thus the removed
filled hull meets $L$ only at $z$.

In a sufficiently small ball about $z$, the full open upper half-ball belongs
to the target component. Any putative straight boundary line through $z$
would therefore have to be horizontal, since every other line enters this
upper half-ball. But a horizontal boundary segment would contain other
points of $L$, all of which are interior surviving points. Contradiction.
The same argument rules out a flat full prime-end germ at this tip.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The source-flat/default-identity rule violates DMP]
\label{prop:sl-flatfailsdmp}
The family (AF1)--(AF2) does not satisfy the domain Markov property.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Take a rectangle, source at the midpoint of its bottom side, and target at
the midpoint of its top side. The initial matrix is
$A_h=\operatorname{diag}(1,a)$, a nonsimilarity. Stop at first hit of an
intermediate horizontal line. The hit is almost surely interior: the two
possible boundary endpoints of the line are fixed boundary points, and
SLE$_6$ hits each fixed boundary point other than its marks with probability
zero; an affine map preserves this statement. The path must reach the line
before reaching its target.

By Lemma~\ref{lem:sl-freshlevel}, the prescribed matrix at the restarted
source is $I$. But ordinary SLE$_6$ DMP in the fixed $A_h$ coordinates says
that the true conditional continuation has matrix $A_h$. Identifiability
of the affine metric for one marked bounded prime-end domain makes these
continuation laws unequal, since $A_h$ is not a similarity. Thus DMP fails.
\end{proof}

This example distinguishes two separate conclusions. Revised strong locality
eliminates the earlier \emph{target}-germ construction. It still permits
arbitrary \emph{source}-germ metrics before DMP is imposed; the simple flat
source rule above is then genuinely excluded by DMP.

\subsection{The exact DMP restriction and why full support is not enough by itself}

\begin{proposition}[Conservation of the affine metric]
\label{prop:sl-conservation}
Within the all-state affine-SLE ansatz, DMP is equivalent to
\[
 T(D_t,p_t,q)=T(D,p,q)\quad\hbox{almost surely}
 \tag{AF3}
\]
at every relevant stopping time. This is in addition to the ordinary SLE
conditional kernel in the current fixed affine coordinates.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
In the original affine coordinates $A=A_{D,p,q}$, ordinary SLE$_6$ DMP gives
an affine-SLE continuation with the same matrix $A$. The family prescribes
matrix $A_{D_t,p_t,q}$. The two laws coincide exactly when their tensors
are proportional, by the one-pair metric-identification theorem. This proves
both implications, including the stopping-time version when the kernels are
measurable.
\end{proof}

Strong locality on regular starts and conservation do not immediately erase
information stored in rough restart germs. Full support of Brownian driving
alone cannot extend an almost-sure identity to deterministic analytic curves.
For example, bounded-variation controls are dense in $C_0[0,t]$, have Brownian
probability zero, and form a Borel set (total variation at most $N$ is closed).
The Borel function equal to $0$ on bounded-variation controls and $1$ elsewhere
is almost surely constant under Brownian measure, yet differs on every smooth
control. Some stability at the deterministic restart states is essential to
that particular support argument. It is not silently implied by full support.

The initial-trace-germ metric decoder proved elsewhere gives a concrete reason
to take this obstruction seriously: different affine metrics can already be
distinguished from arbitrarily short ordered trace germs. It does not yet
provide a decoder from the unlabelled boundary germ of the current target
component, and therefore does not construct a DMP counterexample here.


\paragraph{A probabilistic alternative to topological stability.}
Within the more specific class of source-germ decorations, suppose a measurable
metric label $t(G)$ is defined on every reachable colored source germ and
DMP gives $t(G_\tau)=t(G_0)$ almost surely. If the laws $\nu_1,\nu_2$ of two
restart germs are not mutually singular, their initial metrics must coincide:
otherwise $\nu_i$ is supported on the disjoint measurable fiber
$t^{-1}(t(G_0^i))$, which makes the laws mutually singular.
Consequently a connected graph of such nonsingular restart-law comparisons
would force a common tensor without any continuity assumption.
This is a conditional communication criterion, not a proof that the required
overlap occurs. Brownian full support supplies topological support only;
mutually singular measures can have identical full topological support.
Initial-germ metric decoding gives an explicit instance of that distinction.
For the unrestricted affine ansatz, even expressing $T$ as a function of a
rough source germ rather than the full marked state needs local metric
identifiability at that rough germ; only regular and straight-tip germs have
been proved sufficient in this fragment.

\subsection{Straight slit-tip germs identify the metric locally}

Let $\mathcal S=\mathbb C\setminus(-\infty,0]$, with source the slit tip $0$
and target the end at infinity. The two sides of the slit are distinct colored
boundary arcs. A canonical straight-tip germ in physical space agrees, up to
translation, with $\mathcal S$ near $0$, with fixed colors on its two sides.

\begin{lemma}[Local metric identification at a straight slit tip]
\label{lem:sl-slitidentify}
If two affine-SLE laws have identical initial prefixes in a common canonical
straight-tip neighborhood, their projective affine tensors are equal.
Consequently strong locality and translations assign one tensor $T_*$ to
all states having this canonical colored source germ.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Blow up at the tip. The straight-boundary corner of opening $2\pi$ has a
square-root uniformizing coordinate, so ordinary conformal kernel convergence
identifies the two local limits as affine-SLE laws in $\mathcal S$ from $0$
to $\infty$. Equality of prefixes in every fixed disk determines equality
of these complete limiting laws. Stopping continuity may first be used at
almost every radius and then extended to all smaller radii by composition.

It remains to identify the metric in $\mathcal S$. If a linear map $M$ carries
ordinary SLE$_6$ in $\mathcal S$ to SLE$_6$ in $M\mathcal S$, rotate the image
slit to the negative real axis. The resulting map $N$ preserves $\mathcal S$
and its slit orientation. The conformal map $\psi(z)=i\sqrt z$ takes
$\mathcal S$ to $\mathbb H$ with marks $0,\infty$. For
$z=\rho e^{i\theta}$, $-\pi<\theta<\pi$, the left-passage probability is
\[
 F\bigl(-\tan(\theta/2)\bigr),
\]
where $F$ is strictly increasing. Topological side preservation forces
$\arg Nz=\arg z$ for every $z\in\mathcal S$. In particular, writing
$N(x,y)=(\alpha x+\beta y,\delta y)$ with $\alpha,\delta>0$, tests at
$z=i$ and $z=1+i$ give $\beta=0$ and $\alpha=\delta$. Thus $M$ is a similarity.
Apply this to the two limiting affine laws. Translations and strong locality
then identify all canonical straight-tip states.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A narrower sufficient stability premise than full domain continuity}

The preceding lemma permits a support argument requiring continuity only at
one kind of source germ, rather than at every marked domain.

\paragraph{Straight-tip stability (additional hypothesis).}
Suppose $f_n:\mathbb H\to E_n$ and $f:\mathbb H\to E$ are conformal maps onto
bounded simply connected domains with $f_n\to f$ locally uniformly and
nondegenerate limit. Mark the ends by $0,\infty$. For each fixed invertible
linear $L$, require
\[
 T(LE_n,Lf_n(0),Lf_n(\infty))\longrightarrow
 T(LE,Lf(0),Lf(\infty))
 \tag{AF4}
\]
\emph{only when the limiting physical state} has a canonical horizontal
straight-tip source germ, up to translation and with the prescribed colors.
The approximating states may have rough boundaries. No continuity is assumed
at other limiting states. This is a condition at one local germ type across
its different global representatives, not a derived consequence of strong
locality or DMP.

\begin{theorem}[One stable slit-germ type forces a common regular-domain metric]
\label{thm:sl-onegerm}
Assume the all-state affine-SLE ansatz, strong DMP, revised strong locality,
translation covariance, and straight-tip stability (AF4). Then one fixed
matrix normalizes every marginal on bounded piecewise real-analytic Jordan
domains with regular source and target. Neither geometric reversal nor full
marked-domain continuity is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:sl-slitidentify}, every state with the canonical straight-tip
germ has the same determinant-one tensor $T_*$.
Fix a regular initial triple and its matrix $A$. Choose a simple deterministic
arc from its source into its interior that ends with a horizontal straight
segment traversed from left to right. The arc stays away from the target and
can be chosen piecewise analytic, smooth at its finitely many joins. Its final
point lies in an interior disk and no earlier part of the arc enters a smaller
disk about that point. The resulting slit source therefore has exactly the
canonical germ, with the same fixed boundary colors.

In $A$ coordinates, uniformize the initial domain by
$f:\mathbb H\to AD$, source $0$, target $\infty$. The chosen simple arc has
a continuous Loewner control $w_*$ on a finite capacity interval $[0,t]$.
For every continuous control $w$ on this interval define the inverse chart of
the remaining state by
\[
 f_t^w(z)=f\bigl((g_t^w)^{-1}(z+w(t))\bigr).
 \tag{AF5}
\]
The inverse Loewner ODE shows $w\mapsto f_t^w$ continuous from the uniform
norm to locally uniform convergence on $\mathbb H$. No trace-continuity
theorem is needed for general approximating controls. At $w=w_*$ the physical
state $A^{-1}f_t^{w_*}(\mathbb H)$ has the canonical germ.
Thus (AF4), with $L=A^{-1}$, makes the metric functional of (AF5) continuous
at $w_*$.

Under the original affine-SLE law, the driving control is $\sqrt6B$.
Conservation (AF3) says its restarted metric is the original tensor almost
surely. Brownian measure has full uniform support on $C_0[0,t]$.
If the metric at $w_*$ differed from the original tensor, continuity at $w_*$
would give a positive-Brownian-probability neighborhood with different
metrics, contradicting conservation. Hence the original tensor equals the
metric at the deterministic slit state, which is $T_*$. The argument applies
to every regular initial triple. Similarity covariance removes the residual
scalar and orthogonal factors from the representing matrices.
\end{proof}



\paragraph{Extension at the marked-domain-control level.}
If the all-state ansatz and (AF4) are formulated in inverse conformal charts,
the same proof gives a common tensor on \emph{all bounded simply connected
prime-end states}, without assuming an initially regular Euclidean boundary.
Here are the extra deterministic details. Given
$f:\mathbb H\to AD$ with source $f(0)$, first use the zero driving control
for a positive time. Its vertical chart slit has a positive-time endpoint
in $AD$. The physical image of the slit is analytic near that endpoint,
because $f$ is conformal at its interior preimage. Earlier parts of the
slit cannot accumulate at this endpoint: that would contradict continuity
of $f^{-1}$ at an interior point. Its local source domain is therefore a
straightenable analytic slit of opening $2\pi$.

Continue from this tip along a simple physical arc which initially avoids
the old slit, stays in the remaining domain, and ends with an isolated
horizontal straight segment traversed from left to right. Such an arc
exists by polygonal connectivity of an open planar domain, with smoothing
away from the final straight segment. In the current normalized half-plane
chart it is a simple curve starting at the accessible tip and otherwise
interior, hence has a continuous Loewner driver starting at zero.
Concatenating the initial zero driver and this continuation driver is a
continuous control on a finite interval. Its final physical state has the
canonical source germ, exactly as in the regular proof. Conservation,
continuity at this one limiting germ type, and Brownian full support again
force the initial tensor to be $T_*$.

This argument constructs Loewner domain states; it does not assert that an
initial non-singleton prime-end impression has a Euclidean landing point.
If the model is restricted to Euclidean continuous curves including their
initial endpoints, retain Theorem~\ref{thm:sl-onegerm} on the regular class.
The extension is valid in the inverse-chart/prime-end formulation of the
all-state affine ansatz used in (AF4).

\begin{proposition}[Pointwise analytic-history consistency is another sufficient premise]
\label{prop:sl-pointwisehistories}
In Theorem~\ref{thm:sl-onegerm}, straight-tip stability may be replaced by
this explicit condition: for every regular initial state and every deterministic
simple piecewise analytic initial arc of the kind used in the proof, the
family's law in the resulting slit state is the ordinary SLE conditional
continuation in the initial fixed affine coordinates. Under this condition,
one fixed metric again normalizes all regular-domain marginals.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Choose the arc ending in a canonical horizontal slit germ. The pointwise
condition gives its tensor equal to the original tensor. Strong locality
and translations make the slit state's tensor $T_*$. No support or continuity
argument is needed.
\end{proof}

This condition is not automatically part of ordinary stochastic DMP. Analytic
deterministic histories have probability zero under Brownian driving, and a
regular conditional kernel is determined only almost surely. Requiring its
specified affine-SLE extension to agree at those histories is extra pointwise
consistency. The proposition is therefore a conditional theorem, not a proof
that the revised three axioms already force one metric.

\paragraph{Precise remaining issue.}
This theorem substantially narrows the stability needed by the support method,
but it does not remove stability altogether. Without (AF4), the support argument
cannot rule out a conserved label encoded in rough source germs. Neither the
existence of such a label nor its impossibility under strong locality and DMP
has been proved. The task of deriving the affine-SLE ansatz from arbitrary
exploration laws remains separate and open.

\section{A nonconcentration estimate bounds affine anisotropy}
\label{sec:rsw-metric-compactness}

This result is a concrete use of a coarse curve estimate. It does not
identify a metric, but makes the possible normalized metrics compact.
It concerns the affine-SLE ansatz in a flat source half-plane, not an
unspecified coloring associated to arbitrary curve laws.

For an orientation-preserving real-linear map $B$ carrying $\mathbb H$
onto itself, let $\mu_B$ be the law of $B\eta$, where
$\eta\sim\mathrm{SLE}_6(\mathbb H;0,\infty)$. Suppose a collection
$\mathcal B$ obeys a uniform small-ball estimate
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{B\in\mathcal B}\sup_{z\in\overline{\mathbb H}\setminus\{0\}}
 \mu_B\{\gamma\cap B(z,\rho|z|)\ne\varnothing\}
 \leq u(\rho),\qquad \lim_{\rho\downarrow0}u(\rho)=0.
 \label{eq:metric-nonconcentration}
\end{equation}
An example of the required numerical form is $u(\rho)=C\rho^\alpha$.
A sufficient input is a first-entry annulus bound which explicitly
includes interior balls when both marked ends are outside the outer ball.
The boundary-unforced G2/C2 estimate used for driver tightness alone does
not supply this: its unforced annulus part is empty if the inner circle
misses the current domain boundary. Thus the displayed nonconcentration
estimate remains an explicit hypothesis here. The later ray-locality
comparison controls a particular driving-confinement event, not this full
interior hitting estimate. No coloring is presumed for an abstract family.

\begin{proposition}[Compactness of normalized metrics]
\label{prop:rsw-metric-compact}
Under \eqref{eq:metric-nonconcentration}, the condition numbers
$\|B\|\,\|B^{-1}\|$ for $B\in\mathcal B$ are uniformly bounded.
Equivalently, the determinant-one conformal tensors of $B^{-1}$ stay
in a compact subset of the positive-definite determinant-one matrices.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Suppose otherwise, and choose a sequence with diverging condition number.
Ordinary SLE$_6$ is invariant under scalar spatial dilations, so replacing
$B_n$ by $B_n/\|B_n\|$ does not change $\mu_{B_n}$ or its condition number.
Pass to a subsequence with $B_n\to M$, $\|M\|=1$, $\det M=0$.
Thus $M$ has rank one and $M\overline{\mathbb H}\subset
\overline{\mathbb H}$. Write $M z=v\ell(z)$ with $|v|=1$ and a nonzero
real-linear functional $\ell$.

An SLE$_6$ trace on any positive capacity interval is almost surely not
contained in a prescribed straight line. One can use the standard
nondegeneracy of Brownian driving here: a strictly growing straight-ray
Loewner trace has a deterministic driver of the form $c\sqrt t$, whereas
Brownian motion almost surely has no such trajectory. Therefore
$\max_{0\leq t\leq1}|\ell(\eta(t))|>0$ almost surely. Choose $\delta>0$
so that this maximum exceeds $2\delta$ with probability greater than
$3/4$. At least one choice of sign $\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}$ has
\[
 \mathbb P\{\max_{0\leq t\leq1}\sigma\ell(\eta(t))>2\delta\}>3/8.
\]
Fix that sign and put $z_0=\sigma\delta v\ne0$. By continuity and
$\eta(0)=0$, $M\eta$ hits $z_0$ on the displayed event. In particular
$z_0\in\overline{\mathbb H}$.

On each sample the compact trace $\eta[0,1]$ is bounded, so
$B_n\eta\to M\eta$ uniformly on this interval. Consequently, for every
fixed $\rho>0$,
\[
 \liminf_n\mu_{B_n}\{\gamma\cap B(z_0,\rho|z_0|)\ne\varnothing\}
 \geq \mathbb P\{M\eta[0,1]\text{ hits }z_0\}>3/8.
\]
Choosing $\rho$ with $u(\rho)<3/8$ contradicts
\eqref{eq:metric-nonconcentration}. Uniform boundedness of the condition
numbers follows. The tensor conclusion is elementary singular-value
normalization.
\end{proof}

The proposition does not force equality of different metrics: every
uniformly bounded collection of affine distortions can still contain many
members. Its use is compactness in attempts to recover metric continuity
from convergence of exploration laws, rather than postulating continuity
of a hidden metric label. Establishing the necessary convergence at rough
slit tips remains separate.

% Investigation under the USER-REVISED source-neighborhood locality axiom.
% No main-file edits. Includes exact conditional statements and failed approaches.
\section{Source locality, microscopic memory, and quantitative target stability}
\label{sec:strong-local-obstructions}

\paragraph{Current status under the revised axiom.}
The present project specification compares any two colored marked domains
which agree near the source, including different targets.  The earlier
terminal-tangent construction does not satisfy this stronger axiom.  This
investigation has not produced an unconditional counterexample to the revised
single-color-convention conjecture.  It does prove a quantitative boundary
stability statement from a precise conditional crossing estimate, and it
isolates the exact additional state-recovery step needed for a source-germ
counterexample.  Near-critical configuration convergence is not used as a
substitute for ordered exploration-kernel convergence.

\subsection{What an affine source-germ counterexample would require}

Let $\mathfrak G_s$ be the space of embedded colored source prime-end germs,
with full end neighborhoods retained.  Fix finitely or countably many
orientation-preserving invertible matrices $A_i$ and a deterministic Borel
label $\pi:\mathfrak G_s\to I$.  Consider
\[
 P_{D,p,q}=\nu^{A_{\pi(G_s(D,p))}}_{D,p,q}.
 \tag{SO1}
\]
Unlike a target label, this label is automatically unchanged when two marked
domains agree in a source neighborhood.  It need not be unchanged after an
exploration step.

\begin{proposition}[A precise conditional source-label construction]
Suppose $\pi$ is translation covariant, and suppose that for every initial
marked domain and every admissible finite stopping time of the fixed-label
affine SLE exploration,
\[
 [A_{\pi(G_s(D_t,p_t))}^{\mathsf T}A_{\pi(G_s(D_t,p_t))}]
 =[A_{\pi(G_s(D,p))}^{\mathsf T}A_{\pi(G_s(D,p))}]
 \qquad\text{almost surely}.
 \tag{SO2}
\]
Then (SO1) has strong DMP, the revised source-neighborhood locality, and
translation covariance.  If two regular source germs receive genuinely
different projective tensors, the ensemble cannot have a single fixed affine
SLE normalization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The rough-domain affine identifiability and DMP criterion in the research
record say exactly that (SO2) is the DMP condition within this deterministic
affine ansatz.  When two source neighborhoods agree, their labels agree;
the two laws therefore use the same fixed matrix.  Ordinary SLE$_6$
source locality, with the matching boundary colors, couples their prefixes
through that neighborhood; pulling back the coupling proves the revised
locality.  Translation covariance follows from that of the label and SLE.
Finally, local affine identifiability at a regular start identifies the
projective tensor from its initial prefix law, excluding a common normalizer
when the assigned tensors differ.
\end{proof}

Condition (SO2) is not established for a nonconstant source-germ label.
The proved initial ordered-trace-germ decoder does not establish it.  A
remaining slit domain has an \emph{unlabelled accessible frontier}, while the
decoder uses the chronological order of a trace started from a smooth
boundary.  Filling swallowed bubbles can erase parts of that order.
Furthermore a usable label must work at every admissible stopping time.
Decoding at each separate deterministic time would not by itself supply
that stronger assertion.

One possible route is to show that a constant affine metric can be recovered
from every nonempty open piece of an SLE accessible frontier.  A simultaneous
statement on a countable spatial basis could then handle all tip
neighborhoods, avoiding the unjustified intersection of uncountably many
probability-one events.  No such frontier theorem is proved here; SLE duality
alone does not give the required universal measurable decoder.  Therefore
(SO1)--(SO2) is a useful construction criterion, not a completed counterexample.

For a finite collection of matrices with bounded condition numbers,
ordinary affine SLE marginals retain uniform versions of the standard
unforced-quad crossing bounds, by quasiconformal modulus distortion.
Consequently marginal crossing bounds would not on their own contradict
a hypothetical conserved-label construction.  This does not assert that
the resulting ensemble comes from a single coloring or satisfies a
configuration-level RSW statement.

\subsection{An independent-metric-noise construction degenerates}

The tempting alternative of putting independent bounded conformal structures
at continuum points cannot produce a measurable random Beltrami field.
Here is a slightly stronger formulation.

\begin{lemma}[No independently scattered bounded random density]
Let $X(\omega,z)$ be a jointly measurable complex random function on
$\mathbb R^2$ with $|X|\leq K$ almost surely.  Suppose the random variables
\[
 X(B)=\int_B X(z)\,dz
\]
are independent for disjoint bounded Borel sets $B$.  Then
$X(\omega,z)=\mathbb E[X(z)]$ for Lebesgue-almost every $z$, almost surely.
In particular a stationary such field is almost surely a deterministic
constant almost everywhere.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Partition a bounded rectangle $B$ into rectangles $B_j$ of maximum area
at most $\delta$.  Independence and boundedness give
\[
 \operatorname{Var}X(B)=\sum_j\operatorname{Var}X(B_j)
       \leq4K^2\sum_j|B_j|^2
       \leq4K^2|B|\delta.
\]
Letting $\delta\downarrow0$ proves that $X(B)$ is deterministic.
Intersecting the full-probability events for rectangles with rational
vertices, and using uniqueness of locally finite absolutely continuous
measures, gives the first assertion.  Under stationarity the mean function
is constant almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

For a bounded Beltrami coefficient $|\mu|\leq k<1$, this rules out nontrivial
independently scattered metric noise.  Nontrivial stationary coefficients
with spatial correlation are possible, but exploration then reveals
information about coefficient values away from the revealed trace.  Annealed
DMP in an undecorated slit domain is not automatic.  That latter statement
is an obstruction to the proposed proof strategy, not a nonexistence theorem
for all random-metric models.

\subsection{A quantitative stability consequence of source locality}

We state the crossing input precisely rather than call it ``RSW'' without
specifying a path implication.  Assume constants $C<\infty$ and $\alpha>0$
such that, conditional on any explored initial segment, the probability
that the remaining curve makes an \emph{unforced} crossing of an annulus
$A(z,r,R)$ is at most
\[
 C(r/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{SO3}
\]
Here a component of the annulus in the remaining target domain is unforced
when it does not separate the current source from the target.  In particular,
if those ends can be joined inside $B(z,r)$ in the remaining domain, every
crossing of this annulus is unforced.  The conditional formulation is crucial.
It is a standard type of hypothesis in the Kemppainen--Smirnov framework;
see A. Kemppainen and S. Smirnov, \emph{Random curves, scaling limits and
Loewner evolutions}, Ann. Probab. 45 (2017), 698--779,
\url{https://arxiv.org/abs/1212.6215}.  The argument below uses (SO3) directly;
it does not claim to derive it from bare rectangle crossing bounds for an
arbitrary path ensemble.

\begin{theorem}[Localized target perturbations are small in curve law]
Consider two colored marked Jordan domains $(D_i,p,q_i)$, $i=1,2$,
whose colored domains agree outside $B(z,\epsilon)$, with common source
$p\notin\overline{B(z,R_0)}$ and targets $q_i\in B(z,\epsilon)$.
Suppose the revised source locality holds and both exploration laws satisfy
(SO3) with the same constants.  Couple the common prefixes through first
entry of $\overline{B(z,2\epsilon)}$.  Assume that, at this entry, the current
source and target can be joined in their respective remaining domains
inside $B(z,4\epsilon)$, almost surely.  Then, for
$4\epsilon<R<R_0$, there is a coupling of the full curves with
\[
 \mathbb P\{d_{\mathrm F}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)>2R\}
       \leq 2C(4\epsilon/R)^\alpha,
 \tag{SO4}
\]
where $d_{\mathrm F}$ is oriented uniform Fr\'echet distance.
Consequently the distances between the two curve laws tend to zero as $\epsilon\downarrow0$; convergence to a common limit follows if either family has a limit.
After fixing a spatial unit, their Prokhorov distance, and their bounded
Lipschitz distance for the truncated Fr\'echet metric, are
$O(\epsilon^{\alpha/(\alpha+1)})$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The two colored domains coincide in the source neighborhood
$\mathbb R^2\setminus\overline{B(z,2\epsilon)}$.  The revised locality
therefore identifies the laws of their prefixes stopped on the circle of
radius $2\epsilon$.  Use a common prefix and the regular conditional tail
kernels to obtain a full coupling with the correct marginals.  No agreement
of the two tails is asserted.

At this stopping time the connection hypothesis makes every crossing of
$A(z,4\epsilon,R)$ unforced in either remaining domain.  Estimate (SO3)
therefore bounds the conditional probability that either tail exits
$B(z,R)$ by $C(4\epsilon/R)^\alpha$.  The union bound gives twice this
quantity for the probability that at least one tail exits.

On the complementary event, the two curves have a common ordered prefix
and both remaining tails stay in a ball of diameter $2R$.  Aligning the
common prefix and then reparameterizing their tails gives
$d_{\mathrm F}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\leq2R$.  This proves (SO4).
The coupling definition of Prokhorov distance gives a bound by
$\max\{2R,2C(4\epsilon/R)^\alpha\}$.  Bounded Lipschitz test functions
give, for example, $2R+4C(4\epsilon/R)^\alpha$.
Taking $R$ proportional to
$\epsilon^{\alpha/(\alpha+1)}$ proves the stated rate.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A sufficient geometric situation for the connection hypothesis.}
Suppose the original domain is a half-plane in $B(z,R_0)$, each modified
domain is Jordan, and all modifications and their target points are inside
$B(z,\epsilon)$.  The half-annulus
$B(z,2\epsilon)\setminus\overline{B(z,\epsilon)}$ in that half-plane
is connected and unchanged.  A target access arc reaches this half-annulus
from inside the smaller ball.  At first entry of the radius-$2\epsilon$
ball the earlier trace has no point in its interior.  Thus the entering
target-side prime end connects through the unchanged half-annulus to the
target access arc inside $B(z,4\epsilon)$, without crossing the explored
trace.  Filling non-target components does not remove this connection.
This verifies the hypothesis in that locally flat situation.  For a wild
target patch one must check the connection condition; proximity of the two
Euclidean endpoints alone does not establish it.

\paragraph{What this rules out and what it leaves open.}
The theorem proves a genuine boundary-continuity consequence of strengthened
locality plus a specified conditional unforced-crossing bound.  Remote
localized target perturbations cannot change a macroscopic path law by a
fixed amount under these hypotheses.  This is relevant to schemes that
store information at a distant target.  It does not establish continuity
under perturbations of the source itself: when two source germs differ,
the revised locality supplies no common initial prefix, and the preceding
coupling argument does not start.  Thus it does not yet eliminate (SO1)--(SO2)
or prove the marked-chart continuity needed by the conditional affine-ansatz
classification.

\subsection{Color orientation is a separate formulation issue}

If a state space includes \emph{both} boundary-color orientations as separate
sectors, black-on-left and black-on-right relative to the oriented
exploration are conserved by DMP, and colored source locality never compares
those two sectors.  Assigning one fixed affine matrix to one sector and a
different fixed affine matrix to the other then gives an exact example with
strong locality, DMP, and translations, with no common matrix across the two
sectors.  Local affine identifiability proves non-equivalence when the tensors
are not proportional.

This is \emph{not} a counterexample when the project notation fixes the usual
one-color convention for every $(D,p,q)$, as we assume in the current core
investigation.  In that convention only one of the sectors is present.
Likewise a two-ended exploration or reversal argument must track the fact
that reversal exchanges the color orientation; no color-exchange symmetry
at a critical density different from $1/2$ is supplied by the three axioms.

% Research fragment; requires amsmath/amssymb/amsthm.
\section{A wrong affine metric cannot have tame driving drift in every bounded domain}
\label{sec:scaling-semimartingale}

\paragraph{Status.}
The conclusion here is stronger than the earlier wrong-coordinate consequence
of drift boundedness.  For a fixed non-similarity affine deformation of SLE$_6$,
regular semimartingale driving with absolutely continuous right-continuous
drift cannot hold in every bounded domain in the wrong Euclidean complex
structure.  No local boundedness of the initial drift functional is needed.
The proof uses exact scale invariance only for the \emph{known fixed-affine
SLE$_6$ half-plane law}; it does not assume that arbitrary critical
subsequential limits are scale invariant.  All characteristic regularity
assumptions remain explicit.

\subsection{Exact Brownian scaling and a regular initial bracket}

\paragraph{Scaling lemma (proved).}
Let $W$ be a continuous real process on $[0,\infty)$ with $W_0=0$, whose law
has exact Brownian scaling:
\[
 (c^{-1/2}W_{ct})_{t\geq0}\ \stackrel{\rm law}{=}\ (W_t)_{t\geq0}
 \qquad(c>0).
 \tag{SM1}
\]
Suppose up to a strictly positive stopping time it is a continuous
semimartingale
\[
 W_t=M_t+\int_0^t b_s\,ds,
 \tag{SM2}
\]
where $M_0=0$ is a continuous local martingale and $b$ is finite and
right-continuous at $0$.  Suppose its bracket has a finite deterministic
initial right derivative
\[
 \lim_{t\downarrow0}\frac{\langle M\rangle_t}{t}=v\in[0,\infty)
 \quad\text{almost surely}.
 \tag{SM3}
\]
Then the entire law of $W$ is that of $\sqrt v B$.  This includes the
identically zero process if $v=0$.

\begin{proof}
Fix $T<\infty$ and stop the local decomposition at a positive initial
localizing time.  As $c\downarrow0$, with probability tending to one the
whole interval $[0,cT]$ lies before that time.  Right-continuity of $b$ gives
\[
 \sup_{t\leq T}\left|c^{-1/2}\int_0^{ct}b_s\,ds\right|
 \leq\sqrt c\,T\sup_{s\leq cT}|b_s|\longrightarrow0
\]
almost surely on the localized event.  Writing $C_t=\langle M\rangle_t$,
(SM3) implies
\[
 \sup_{t\leq T}|C_{ct}/c-vt|\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{SM4}
\]
Indeed $|C_{ct}/c-vt|\leq t\sup_{0<s\leq cT}|C_s/s-v|$.
The continuous-martingale functional central limit theorem now gives
$c^{-1/2}M_{c\cdot}\Rightarrow\sqrt v B$ on $C[0,T]$.
One may see this directly from Dambis--Dubins--Schwarz: extend its Brownian
motion if necessary and write
\[
 c^{-1/2}M_{ct}=B^{(c)}_{C_{ct}/c},
 \qquad B^{(c)}_u=c^{-1/2}\widetilde B_{cu}.
\]
Each $B^{(c)}$ has standard Brownian law.  Uniform bracket convergence to the
deterministic clock and the Brownian modulus of continuity imply the stated
limit; independence between $B^{(c)}$ and the bracket is unnecessary.
Adding the negligible drift gives the same limit for $W$.
By exact scaling, however, each rescaled process already has the law of
$W$ on $[0,T]$.  That law is therefore Brownian with variance $v$.
Arbitrariness of $T$ proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

The regular initial bracket is not automatic for a general self-similar
continuous process.  In the affine-SLE application below it follows from
locality and absolutely continuous drift in two domain charts, using the
local bracket-regularization identity.

\subsection{The half-plane affine-SLE obstruction}

Let $A$ be an invertible orientation-preserving real-linear map and let
$\eta$ be chordal SLE$_6$ in $A\mathbb H$ from $0$ to $\infty$.  Put
$\gamma=A^{-1}\eta\subset\overline{\mathbb H}$ and let $W$ be its
half-plane-capacity driving function.  Its continuous Loewner generation and
infinite capacity lifetime are supplied by the affine-admissibility result
already proved in the research record.  Its law has exact Brownian scaling
(SM1): ordinary SLE is dilation covariant, $A$ commutes with positive scalar
dilations, and capacity scales by the square of physical length.

\paragraph{Proposition (proved).}
If this $W$ satisfies (SM2)--(SM3), then $A$ is a Euclidean similarity.

\begin{proof}
The scaling lemma makes $W$ ordinary Brownian driving of variance $v$.
Thus the trace is ordinary chordal SLE$_v$ in $\mathbb H$.
We identify $v$ without a Hausdorff-dimension theorem.

For $\kappa\leq4$ the nonzero real boundary points are never swallowed by
SLE$_\kappa$: their Loewner distances divided by $\sqrt\kappa$ are Bessel
processes of dimension $1+4/\kappa\geq2$ (the case $\kappa=0$ is immediate).
An affine image of SLE$_6$ swallows boundary points, so $v>4$.
For any $\kappa>4$, the boundary-race calculation gives
\[
 \mathbb P_\kappa(\tau_y<\tau_x)
 =F_\kappa\!\left(\frac{-x}{y-x}\right),\qquad
 F_\kappa(r)=
 \frac{\displaystyle\int_0^r[u(1-u)]^{-4/\kappa}\,du}
 {\displaystyle\int_0^1[u(1-u)]^{-4/\kappa}\,du},
 \quad x<0<y.
 \tag{SM5}
\]
This includes $\kappa\geq8$.  To check it, the ratio
$R=-X/(Y-X)$ has generator
\[
 \frac\kappa2\frac{d^2}{dr^2}
   +\frac{2(1-2r)}{r(1-r)}\frac d{dr}
\]
after $ds=dt/(Y-X)^2$.  Its scale density is
$[r(1-r)]^{-4/\kappa}$, integrable at both ends exactly when $\kappa>4$.
The first swallowing is finite almost surely by the Bessel description;
simultaneous swallowing is impossible since $Y-X$ is positive and
nondecreasing.  Bounded optional stopping gives (SM5).

Rotate $A\mathbb H$ conformally back to $\mathbb H$.  The resulting map
has matrix
\[
 T=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\0&\delta\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad \alpha,\delta>0.
\]
It acts on the real boundary by $x\mapsto\alpha x$, so it preserves the
ratio $-x/(y-x)$ and the topological swallowing order.  The law of
$\gamma$ therefore has boundary-race function $F_6$, while its Brownian
driving gives $F_v$.  Equality for every $r\in(0,1)$ forces $v=6$:
compare the derivatives in (SM5), proportional respectively to
$[r(1-r)]^{-4/v}$ and $[r(1-r)]^{-2/3}$.

Finally $T^{-1}\mathrm{SLE}_6(\mathbb H;0,\infty)$ is ordinary SLE$_6$.
Half-plane affine identifiability now implies $T$ is a similarity.
For completeness, the strictly increasing SLE$_6$ left-passage function
\[
 L(u)=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^u(1+s^2)^{-2/3}\,ds}
 {\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(1+s^2)^{-2/3}\,ds}
\]
and preservation of topological side give
$L(u)=L((\alpha u+\beta)/\delta)$ for all real $u$.
Thus $\alpha=\delta$ and $\beta=0$.  Rotation does not affect whether $A$
is a similarity, proving the proposition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Transfer from bounded-domain regularity to the half-plane germ}

The preceding proposition is already a complete unbounded-domain statement.
The following additional argument shows why requiring the tame drift
structure in \emph{every bounded domain} is enough to trigger it.
We spell out the local coordinate transfer, rather than silently assume
semimartingality is preserved by a general physical affine map.

\paragraph{Bounded-domain obstruction theorem (proved).}
Consider the canonical fixed-affine SLE$_6$ family
\[
 P_{D,p,q}=(A^{-1})_*\operatorname{Law}
       (\mathrm{SLE}_6(AD;Ap,Aq))
\]
on bounded simply connected marked domains.  Suppose in the original
Euclidean complex structure every domain-chart driver is a continuous
semimartingale whose finite-variation part is
$\int_0^t b_s\,ds$, with finite right-continuous drift density and
deterministic initial value $B(f)$, consistently under the usual conditional
kernel interpretation.  Then $A$ is a Euclidean similarity.
No boundedness or continuity of the map $f\mapsto B(f)$ is assumed.

\begin{proof}
Choose a bounded half-disk $D\subset\mathbb H$ with $0$ in the interior of
its real diameter, and a distinct target on that diameter away from $0$.
Choose the boundary colors so they agree near $0$ with the half-plane
problem $(\mathbb H,0,\infty)$.  Let $\phi:D\to\mathbb H$ be its chordal
chart sending these two marks to $0,\infty$.  It extends analytically
across $0$, with $\phi'(0)>0$.

The local bracket-regularization lemma applies to this bounded-domain
driver: delete one further vertical slit in its $\phi$ chart, away from its
marks, with nonzero $h''(0)$.  Both domains have tame absolutely continuous
drift by hypothesis.  The signed-measure identity
\[
 h_t''(W_t)(dC_t-6\,dt)
  =2\bigl[h_t'(W_t)^2\widehat b_{u(t)}
                        -h_t'(W_t)b_t\bigr]dt
\]
therefore gives a finite right-continuous initial bracket density in $D$,
with deterministic initial value $v_D$.

Corrected source-neighborhood locality of ordinary SLE$_6$, preserved by
the fixed map $A^{-1}$, couples this bounded-domain curve to the canonical
affine-SLE$_6$ half-plane curve so that their physical initial segments are
identical until leaving a sufficiently small half-neighborhood of $0$.
This uses ordinary SLE$_6$ locality including distant target changes; it is
not a new hypothesis about an unknown nonconformal family.
On that common initial trace, the coordinate change is the analytic map
$\phi$ at the source.

A local analytic coordinate change and its inverse preserve the tame
semimartingale structure just obtained.  More explicitly, if $t$ is physical
half-plane capacity and $u$ is the bounded-chart capacity, the evolving local
map satisfies
\[
 W_D(u(t))=h_t(W_H(t)),\qquad
 \dot u(t)=h_t'(W_H(t))^2.
\]
Initially $h_0=\phi$, so $h_0'(0)>0$ and all required local derivatives are
finite.  Before the trace leaves the common neighborhood, localize so the
first derivative stays bounded away from zero and the derivatives stay
bounded.  Both capacity clocks are adapted functions of the same initial
trace; their inverses are stopping-time clocks.  The inverse field
$h_t^{-1}$ has finite variation in time, as follows by differentiating the
Loewner composition.  Applying the finite-variation It\^o--Wentzell formula
to the semimartingale $W_D$, and changing the capacity clock, produces a
continuous-semimartingale decomposition of $W_H$ on a positive initial
interval.  Its drift is absolutely continuous and right-continuous, and its
initial bracket density is the same scalar $v_D$.  These conclusions use
only a \emph{conformal local chart transition}, not the nonconformal map $A$.

For an explicit initial check, writing $d=\phi'(0)$, $e=\phi''(0)$,
the transferred rates obey
\[
 V_H=v_D,\qquad
 B_H=dB_D-\left(\frac{v_D}{2}-3\right)\frac ed,
 \tag{SM6}
\]
so both are finite and deterministic.  Thus the canonical half-plane driver
satisfies (SM2)--(SM3).  Its exact scaling and the half-plane proposition
force $A$ to be a similarity.
\end{proof}

Only a bounded domain flat at its source and one of its source-preserving
slit deletions are needed for the bracket part of this contradiction.
The full all-bounded-domain assumption is a convenient sufficient
formulation.  The obstruction does not identify the precise failure for a
particular wrong affine metric: semimartingality, absolute continuity or
right-continuity of the drift, or deterministic initial drift may fail.
It does show that at least one must fail somewhere.  It supplies no argument
that an unknown exploration family admits a correct affine normalization
with tame drift.

% A common-color percolation coupling, stronger than abstract source locality.
\section{A discrete source shield and stability of two exploration starts}
\label{sec:source-shield}

\paragraph{Status and model.}
The deterministic shielding statement and the ensuing probabilistic estimate
are proved here for independent site percolation on a triangular-lattice
triangulation of a Jordan disk. Boundary vertices are wired black or white,
with exactly two color changes, the marked source and target. The exploration
is the nonbranching face-dual black--white interface between those changes.
The same argument applies to a cellular model with the corresponding
nonbranching primal/dual geometry and the stated white RSW bounds.
It is an actual common-color coupling argument. It is \emph{not} inferred
for an arbitrary random-curve family from abstract locality or G2 alone.

Work at mesh $\delta$. Two triangulated domains, their source positions,
and their boundary colors may differ inside $B(p,\epsilon)$, but agree
outside it. Their common target lies outside $B(p,R_0)$.
Outside the source patch and inside this latter ball, assume the common
geometry is either a lattice half-plane with straight boundary, or a
canonical thin straight slit with its two boundary sides. The lattice
approximations are chosen with free first rows adjacent to the wired
boundary rows; slit width is $O(\delta)$. The black and white wired sides
are the same for the two configurations outside the patch.
Couple all free colors outside the patch identically. Those shared colors
have the independent percolation law. The colors inside the patch may be
coupled arbitrarily, with their correct marginal laws.

\subsection{The deterministic one-edge crosscut}

In a triangular face, either zero or two of its primal edges have endpoints
of different colors. Hence the dual interface graph has degree zero or two
at every interior dual vertex. In the Dobrushin disk it has a unique open
simple path between the source and target; other components are loops.

\begin{lemma}[A one-heterochromatic-edge shield]
\label{lem:ss-oneedge}
Let a simple primal crosscut $c$ of a triangulated disk connect a white
wired boundary vertex to a black wired boundary vertex. Suppose every
edge of $c$ is white--white except its final edge $e$, which joins a white
\emph{interior} vertex to the black boundary vertex. Suppose $c$ separates
the entire source patch from the target. Then the exploration must use
the dual edge $e^*$, with the direction determined by its black and white
sides. After using $e^*$ it cannot reenter the source side of the crosscut.

Consequently, if the same such crosscut and its colors occur in both
configurations above, their explorations have identical ordered suffixes
from $e^*$ to the common target.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The source--target dual path must cross the primal crosscut. Primal and
dual vertices are distinct, and dual edges cross primal edges only at
their matched transverse intersections. No interface edge can cross a
white--white primal edge. Thus the crossing must be $e^*$, and matching
colors give the same direction in both configurations.

The dual interface path is simple, so it cannot traverse $e^*$ twice.
All other primal edges of $c$ remain monochromatic, so they cannot be
crossed later either. The path therefore stays on the target side after
using $e^*$. In particular it never returns to the altered source patch.
Outside the patch the two triangulations and all colors coincide.
At each subsequent dual vertex the outgoing edge is uniquely determined
by the local colors and the incoming edge. Starting at the same directed
edge $e^*$, the two paths consequently have the same entire suffix.
\end{proof}

Using boundary \emph{vertices} in this formulation avoids an ambiguity of
cell-exterior pictures: $e$ is an interior primal edge incident to a boundary
vertex, so its dual edge is an ordinary interior edge. Neither a crossing
through a dual/primal common vertex nor an unexplained passage around the
end of a physical boundary edge is being used.

\subsection{Uniform probability of a white arch}

Assume a uniform white RSW lower bound for fixed-aspect rectangles of free
vertices. No connection to a prescribed individual vertex is assumed.
We give the geometry for a horizontal half-plane source, translated to $p=0$,
with black boundary on the negative real side and white boundary on the
positive side. Let $r\gg\epsilon+\delta$.

Use a white vertical crossing in each of
\[
 [-3r,-2r]\times[0,3r],\qquad [2r,3r]\times[0,3r],
\]
and a white horizontal crossing of
\[
 [-3r,3r]\times[r,2r].
\]
At the bottom of a stem, start the rectangle in the first free vertex
row, not in the fixed wired row. Rounding by $O(\delta)$ changes the
aspect ratios by a uniformly bounded amount. The crossings in the
overlapping squares intersect by planarity. Thus their union contains a
white path from a free vertex adjacent to the black boundary to a free
vertex adjacent to the white boundary. Add a white--white edge at the
white endpoint and choose an edge $e$ to a black boundary vertex at the
other endpoint. Trim the white path at its last white-wired-boundary contact before reaching the left endpoint, so that all its subsequent white vertices are interior; then erase loops. This gives a proper simple primal crosscut with just the one heterochromatic edge $e$.

All these vertices lie, allowing lattice rounding, in
$A(0,r/2,6r)$. The crosscut and the boundary arc through the source
enclose the source patch. Its source-side component is inside $B(0,6r)$:
the crosscut and that local boundary arc form a Jordan polygon contained
there, so their bounded interior is also contained there. The common
half-annulus between the patch and crosscut shows that the entire altered
domain portion of the source patch is on that side.

Each rectangle event is increasing in white colors of independent free
vertices. RSW and FKG therefore give a constant $\vartheta>0$, independent
of $r,\delta$ and the contents of the source patch, for this white-arch
event. Importantly, the left endpoint is \emph{some} first-row white vertex
adjacent to the black boundary, selected after the crossing occurs. Requiring
connection to one predetermined microscopic vertex would not have this
uniform lower bound.

\paragraph{Straight slit geometry.}
The same construction works for a finite-width lattice slit along the
negative real axis, with black upper lip and white lower lip, by using
five crossings around a square annulus: an upper-left vertical stem,
a top horizontal crossing, a right vertical crossing, a bottom horizontal
crossing, and a lower-left vertical stem. The stems occupy
$[-3r,-2r]\times[0,3r]$ and
$[-3r,-2r]\times[-3r,0]$ on their respective sides of the slit.
The other three rectangles may be
\[
 [-3r,3r]\times[2r,3r],\quad
 [2r,3r]\times[-3r,3r],\quad
 [-3r,3r]\times[-3r,-2r].
\]
The first free upper row is adjacent to the black wired lip; the first
free lower row is adjacent to the white wired lip. The resulting crosscut
goes around the right side of the slit and separates its tip patch from
the distant target. The boundary arc through the tip, including the two
separate lattice lips, stays inside $B(0,6r)$. The finite-width triangulated
domain is Jordan; one need not identify its two physical boundary rows.
RSW and FKG again give a uniform $\vartheta>0$ after adjusting constants.

\subsection{Polynomial source-patch stability}

\begin{theorem}[Common-color coalescence outside a source patch]
\label{thm:ss-coalescence}
Under the preceding geometric and percolation hypotheses, there are
constants $C<\infty$ and $\alpha>0$ depending only on the RSW bounds and
the fixed geometry such that, for $0<R<R_0/6$, the common-color coupling
of the two full exploration curves satisfies
\[
 \mathbb P\{d_{\mathrm F}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)>2R\}
       \leq C\left(\frac{\epsilon+\delta}{R}\right)^\alpha.
 \tag{SH1}
\]
More concretely, outside an event of the displayed probability the curves
coalesce at a directed dual edge inside $B(p,R)$; both prefixes before
that edge stay inside this ball, and the entire suffixes are identical.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Put $r_0=16(\epsilon+\delta)$ and $r_j=16^j r_0$.
For every $j$ with $6r_j\leq R$, the corresponding white-arch event
depends only on shared free vertices in
$A(p,r_j/2,6r_j)$. These vertex sets are disjoint for different $j$,
up to an immaterial lattice enlargement absorbed by increasing $r_0$.
The events are therefore independent and each has probability at least
$\vartheta$. If there are $N$ available bands, the probability of no
shield is at most $(1-\vartheta)^N$.

On any shield event select a white crosscut by a deterministic finite-graph
rule using only shared colors, for example a lexicographic simple path in
the crossing cluster. The two configurations select the same crosscut and
the same single heterochromatic edge. Lemma~\ref{lem:ss-oneedge} gives a
common suffix. Before crossing the shield, each path stays in its
source-side component, contained in $B(p,6r_j)\subset B(p,R)$.
Synchronize the two prefixes by any increasing reparameterization and then
align their common suffix. The prefixes are in a ball of diameter $2R$,
so their oriented Fr\'echet distance is at most $2R$.

The number of bands is a logarithmic function of $R/(\epsilon+\delta)$.
For instance with
$\alpha=-\log(1-\vartheta)/\log16>0$, enlarging a fixed constant $C$
gives $(1-\vartheta)^N\leq
C((\epsilon+\delta)/R)^\alpha$. This also covers the case with no
available band by making its right-hand side at least one.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Passage to marginal curve limits.}
Suppose along a common mesh subsequence the two exploration laws converge
in the usual Polish oriented uniform-Fr\'echet curve space. The coupled
pairs are tight because their two marginals are tight. Choose a joint
subsequential limit. The set
$\{d_{\mathrm F}>2R\}$ is open in the product curve space; the Portmanteau
inequality transfers (SH1), with $\delta\downarrow0$, to this limiting
coupling. Thus the same quantitative source-patch stability holds for the
two marginal curve limits. It requires neither convergence of random
slit-domain kernels nor a limiting strong DMP theorem.

If source patches themselves shrink and the mesh is negligible relative
to their radii, this gives continuity in law for these particular local
source perturbations. One may first fix $R$ and let $\epsilon\downarrow0$,
then let $R\downarrow0$, or balance the coupling estimate to obtain an
explicit Prokhorov rate.

\paragraph{What is not proved.}
This is not a consequence of abstract unforced-crossing estimates for one
curve. It uses a common independent coloring and the planar nonbranching
interface to force \emph{coalescence at one edge}. It also is not the full
marked inverse-conformal-chart stability condition used in the affine-ansatz
support theorems: arbitrary converging charts may create forced thin fjords,
noncommon microscopic boundaries, or source approaches outside this shared
half-plane/slit geometry. Extending the shield coupling to such approximations
requires additional work. No classification of arbitrary source-local
exploration ensembles follows from (SH1) alone.

% Draft for root integration; not a standalone document.
\subsection{Common curved source annuli and local flattening}
\label{sec:ss-curved}

The source-shield argument extends to a common curved source neighborhood.
Importantly, the two boundaries must still agree outside the altered source
patch. This is sufficient to compare a regular source with its local
flattening; it is not a comparison of two unrelated approximations of a
whole slit.

Here is a precise geometric hypothesis. Let $H$ be an orientation-preserving
ambient $K$-bi-Lipschitz map on a disk about $0$, with $H(0)=p$. In that disk
the reference domain is either the upper half-plane, with its two colored
boundary rays, or the complement of the negative real ray, with distinct
colored lips. Outside $B(p,\epsilon)$, the two discrete domains have the
same graph, colors, and free vertices, and their common source neighborhood
is a compatible lattice approximation of this $H$-image. By compatible we
mean that all ambiguity in discretizing each smooth boundary/lip lies in
a fixed $c\delta$ tube, vertices a distance greater than $c\delta$ on the
interior side are free, and each boundary contact has the prescribed color
of that boundary side. There are no additional boundary components or
fjords outside that tube. The domains inside the altered patch can be
arbitrary Dobrushin disks, with their sole source color change in the
patch. Their common target is outside the source neighborhood. We use
independent Bernoulli colors and uniform two-color RSW for all
fixed-aspect rectangles needed below.

\begin{proposition}[A shield in common bi-Lipschitz source annuli]
\label{prop:ss-curved}
Under these hypotheses there are $C_K<\infty$ and $\alpha_K>0$ such that,
for $R$ sufficiently small relative to the common source neighborhood,
the common-color coupling satisfies
\[
 \mathbb P\{d_{\mathrm F}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)>2R\}
 \le C_K\left(\frac{\epsilon+\delta}{R}\right)^{\alpha_K}.
 \tag{SH2}
\]
On its good event the two prefixes are contained in $B(p,R)$ and the
curves have an exactly common directed suffix beginning at an edge in that
ball. Constants also depend on the fixed discretization constant and RSW
bounds. The same statement holds for two marginal scaling limits through
a joint subsequential coupling, in the precise sense proved below.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Take the three half-plane crossing corridors, or the five slit crossing
corridors, used for the straight-source shield, and apply $H$. Their
dimensions remain comparable to $r$, their overlap regions contain disks
of radius comparable to $r$ with constants depending only on $K$, and
their nonterminal portions stay a distance comparable to $r$ from the
boundary. A finite chain of overlapping axis-aligned rectangles of
bounded aspect forces each required crossing and connection. The number
and aspect bounds depend only on $K$: follow the image of a corridor's
central segment with balls of radius a sufficiently small multiple of
$r/K$, use the bi-Lipschitz bounds to bound both its length and its
clearance from the unwanted sides, and insert overlapping rectangles
along this chain. RSW and FKG therefore give a uniform lower bound for
the connected white crossing configuration.

For the two stems ending at a wired boundary, continue the corridor a
distance comparable to $r$ across that boundary and give its exterior
vertices, including the actual wired-row vertices, independent auxiliary
ghost colors; actual interior free colors are kept. A white crossing of this extended
corridor, followed from the interior, has an actual white free path up
to the vertex immediately preceding its first wired-boundary contact.
This last free vertex is adjacent to the wired vertex. Thus at the black
stem one adds precisely one white--black edge, and at the white stem one
adds a white--white edge. The continuation crosses only the intended
colored boundary side, since the stems are a distance comparable to $r$
from the source and the opposite lip. The $O(\delta)$ boundary tube is
absorbed when $r\gg\delta$. All middle portions are interior and use
actual common colors. Consequently the ghost crossing event implies the
existence of an actual common white arch, and its positive probability
lower bound is a lower bound for the latter event. No ghost colors enter
the selected actual shield.

Trim at the last white-boundary contact and erase loops as in the straight
proof. This gives a simple common primal crosscut with exactly one
heterochromatic edge. It separates the entire altered source patch from
the target. Its source-side region lies in $H(B(0,6r))$, hence in
$B(p,6Kr)$, after enlarging constants for lattice rounding. Its supporting
free vertices lie in the physical annular shell
\[
 A\bigl(p,r/(2K)-c'\delta,6Kr+c'\delta\bigr).
\]
The probability of such a shield is at least a constant $\vartheta_K>0$.

Start at $r_0=c''K(\epsilon+\delta)$, with $c''$ sufficiently large, and
use $r_j=\lambda^jr_0$, where $\lambda=32K^2$ can be enlarged for the
fixed discretization constants. The preceding physical shells are
disjoint. Shield-existence events use disjoint sets of actual shared free
vertices, hence are independent. Every shield with $6Kr_j\le R$ gives
the common directed suffix by Lemma~\ref{lem:ss-oneedge}; the two prefixes
stay in $B(p,R)$. The probability of no shield is at most
$(1-\vartheta_K)^N$ for a logarithmic number $N$ of available shells.
This proves (SH2), for example with
$\alpha_K=-\log(1-\vartheta_K)/\log\lambda$ after changing $C_K$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Application to flattening a regular source.}
If a Jordan domain has a $C^1$ embedded boundary near a regular source,
an ambient bi-Lipschitz chart $H$ of the stated kind exists after shrinking
the neighborhood. Modify only inside $B(p,\epsilon)$ to make a smaller
source neighborhood exactly a straight half-plane, preserving the two
boundary colors and the target. Choose the two lattice approximations
to agree outside the patch. They then satisfy the proposition in the
common curved annuli. The estimate compares the original source germ
with the flat replacement, despite their different germs. An analogous
statement holds for a regular $C^1$ slit tip with a two-sided tubular
chart, provided the common discrete lips have the compatibility stated
