above. Neither operation presumes conformal invariance.

\paragraph{Scope of the extension.}
If two slits differ along an entire thin tube of macroscopic length, a
tip shield does not isolate all discrepancies: its target-side domain
still contains the altered tube farther back. Exact suffix coalescence
does not follow from the present argument. In particular (SH2) does not
prove straight-tip stability under arbitrary inverse-chart convergence.

\subsection{A quantitative input for perturbing a whole straight slit}

One can at least exclude three-arm propagation along a finite straight
boundary segment. Write $A_3^+(x;r,R)$ for three disjoint alternating
free-color arms across the half-annulus based at $x$ on a fixed straight
line. The external RSW-and-arm-separation estimate
\[
 \mathbb P(A_3^+(x;r,R))\le C(r/R)^2
 \tag{BA1}
\]
holds in critical triangular Bernoulli percolation; see P.~Nolin,
\emph{Near-critical percolation in two dimensions}, Theorem~23(2),
together with half-plane arm separation/quasi-multiplicativity in
Section~4.6, \url{https://arxiv.org/abs/0711.4948}.
It is an external theorem, not a new exponent computation here.

Cover a straight segment of length $L$ by at most $C(1+L/\epsilon)$
balls of radius $\epsilon$, and let $\mathcal B$ be the event that some
such ball has three alternating arms in one specified side of the line
to distance $\eta$, with $\delta\le\epsilon\ll\eta$. Recentring an
event at an arbitrary point of the segment to a covering center only
changes the inner and outer radii by fixed factors. A union bound in
(BA1) gives
\[
 \mathbb P(\mathcal B)
 \le C\frac{(L+\epsilon)\epsilon}{\eta^2}.
 \tag{BA2}
\]
This tends to zero, for example, with $\eta=\epsilon^\beta$ for any
$0<\beta<1/2$ at fixed $L$. The two sides of a slit cost only a factor
two. This is a concrete useful estimate for boundary pivotal effects
away from the two slit endpoints. To turn it into a whole-tube
Fr\'echet coupling, however, one still needs a deterministic implication
from a macroscopic ordered-exploration disagreement to one of these
three-arm events (or a specified replacement), while controlling the
endpoints. That implication has not been proved here; (BA2) alone is
not a curve-stability theorem.

\section{An affine tensor is readable from a regular endpoint germ}
\label{sec:regular-endpoint-decoder}

\paragraph{Status.}
The argument below extends the initial half-plane trace-germ decoder to
analytic regular source germs. By ordinary SLE$_6$ reversibility, it gives
a decoder from the ordered terminal trace germ at any analytic regular
target, even when the other endpoint and the rest of the domain are rough.
It does not decode the unlabelled accessible frontier at a random tip.
All almost-sure assertions are made separately under each fixed affine
tensor; no uncountable intersection of full-probability events is used.

Let $\mathcal X$ be the usual Polish space of oriented unparameterized
continuous finite curves, with the uniform Fr\'echet metric. Curves are
identified under increasing reparameterization, including deletion of
pauses. For a continuous path $Y$ starting at $0$ and exiting every disk,
write $\tau_a(Y)$ for its first hit of $\{|z|=a\}$ and
$\operatorname{Stop}_aY=Y[0,\tau_a(Y)]$.
The operations used here are Borel in standard continuous-curve coding;
first hits can equivalently be defined by rational-time approximation.
For a rough opposite prime end the full curve need not land continuously
there. Only finite prefixes near the decoded regular endpoint are coded
in $\mathcal X$; the unbounded half-plane curves exit every finite disk.
This avoids any assumption of a singleton impression at the other mark.

\begin{lemma}[Unit-radius stopping is almost surely continuous]
\label{lem:ed-goodradius}
Let $Y$ have a law invariant under positive scalar spatial dilation and
suppose it is continuous, starts at $0$, and exits every disk. Then almost
surely
\[
 \tau_{1-\delta}(Y)\uparrow\tau_1(Y),\qquad
 \tau_{1+\delta}(Y)\downarrow\tau_1(Y)
 \quad(\delta\downarrow0).
\]
Consequently the diameter of
$Y[\tau_{1-\delta},\tau_{1+\delta}]$ tends to zero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first assertion on the left holds pathwise by continuity. The
nondecreasing function $a\mapsto\tau_a(Y)$ has only countably many
right jumps. Thus the set of radii with
$\tau_{a+}(Y)>\tau_a(Y)$ has Lebesgue measure zero for every sample.
Spatial dilation invariance implies that the probability of such a jump
is the same at every fixed radius. Fubini's theorem on $[1,2]$ makes this
probability zero. Continuity of $Y$ on a compact time interval containing
these exit times then gives the diameter assertion. The argument is
independent of the chosen continuous strictly increasing curve clock.
\end{proof}

For an orientation-preserving real linear map $L$ carrying $\mathbb H$
onto itself, let $\mu_L$ be the law of $L\eta$, where $\eta$ is chordal
SLE$_6$ in $\mathbb H$ from $0$ to $\infty$. Scalar multiples of $L$
give the same law. Normalize
\[
 L=\begin{pmatrix}1&u\\0&v\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 (u,v)\in\Theta:=\mathbb R\times(0,\infty).
 \tag{ED1}
\]
The normalized metric represented by this law is
$[(L^{-1})^{\mathsf T}L^{-1}]$.

Choose a countable bounded Lipschitz determining class
$(f_m)_{m\geq1}$ on $\mathcal X$, with values in $[0,1]$ and Lipschitz
constant at most one, allowing constant rescaling of an arbitrary
countable bounded Lipschitz determining class. Such a class can be built
from truncated distances to a countable dense set and finite rational
combinations. Set
\[
 v_m(L)=\mathbb E_{\mu_L}
       f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1Y).
 \tag{ED2}
\]
The vector $v(L)=(v_m(L))_m$ is Borel in $L$. Indeed, linear image and
first-exit stopping are jointly Borel, so integration against the fixed
ordinary SLE law preserves Borel measurability.

Brownian spatial scaling is mixing: under the Lamperti transform
$Z_s=e^{-s/2}B_{e^s}$ it becomes a shift of a stationary Gaussian process
with covariance $e^{-|s-u|/2}$. Its SLE trace factor, and each fixed
linear image, are therefore dilation mixing. This gives the ergodicity
used below without a separate path-space independence assumption.

The map $L\mapsto v(L)$ is injective. If two unit-radius stopped laws
agreed, scaling invariance would identify their stopped laws at every
radius. Those restrictions determine the entire ordered unbounded curve,
contradicting the half-plane affine identifiability theorem unless the
normalized matrices agree. Therefore the Lusin--Souslin theorem makes
$v(\Theta)$ Borel and its inverse Borel.

Fix $\rho>1$ and $r_n=\rho^{-n}$. For a curve $\gamma$ starting at
$p$ define the local observable
\[
 X_n(\gamma,p)=r_n^{-1}
       \bigl(\gamma[0,\tau_{r_n}(\gamma-p)]-p\bigr).
 \tag{ED3}
\]
If a finite curve fails to exit one of the large initial disks, assign a
cemetery value to that term. It exits all sufficiently small disks, and
finitely many cemetery values do not affect the limits below.
Define
\[
 \ell_m(\gamma,p)=\lim_{N\to\infty}
       \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_m(X_n(\gamma,p)),
 \tag{ED4}
\]
using a cemetery vector if any limit fails to exist. Each limit is a
function of every sufficiently small ordered initial trace prefix:
deleting finitely many summands changes no Ces\`aro limit. In particular
this is a Borel ordered-trace-germ statistic.

\begin{lemma}[The decoder survives a quadratic local error]
\label{lem:ed-linearization}
Let $\eta$ be ordinary half-plane SLE$_6$ and let $\Phi$ be a local
$C^1$ embedding at $0$, defined on a half-neighborhood of $0$, with
\[
 \Phi(0)=0,\qquad \Phi(z)=Lz+O(|z|^2),
 \tag{ED5}
\]
where $L$ is invertible. Assume after a fixed rotation that $L$ maps
$\mathbb H$ onto itself. Let $\gamma$ agree with $\Phi(\eta)$ up to
the first exit from this local chart. Then almost surely
\[
 \ell_m(\gamma,0)=v_m(L)\quad\hbox{for every }m.
 \tag{ED6}
\]
Scalar normalization of $L$ is understood in (ED2).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $Y_n=r_n^{-1}L\eta$. Spatial dilation of the trace includes the
induced capacity reparameterization. Brownian scaling is mixing; its
trace factor and every fixed affine image are therefore dilation
ergodic, as proved in the initial half-plane decoder theorem. Hence
\[
 \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N
 f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1Y_n)\longrightarrow v_m(L)
 \quad\hbox{almost surely}.
 \tag{ED7}
\]
One must control the error in this average; pointwise stopping continuity
at all the scaled random samples is not asserted.

For $0<\delta<1/4$ define
\[
 h_\delta(Y)=\min\!\left\{2,
  \operatorname{diam}
   Y[\tau_{1-\delta}(Y),\tau_{1+\delta}(Y)]\right\}.
 \tag{ED8}
\]
The linearization error on a fixed bounded scaled chart is at most
$Cr_n$, uniformly over the sample path until its linear image exits the
disk of radius $1+\delta$. This uniformity is deterministic: the inverse
linear map bounds the relevant preimage disk, and (ED5) bounds the error
there. For all sufficiently large $n$, the first time that
$r_n^{-1}\Phi(\eta)$ reaches radius one lies between the corresponding
$Y_n$ exit times at radii $1-\delta$ and $1+\delta$.
Thus the two stopped curves consist of matched portions with pointwise
error at most $Cr_n$, together with an unmatched terminal portion whose
diameter is bounded by the diameter in (ED8). Increasing
reparameterizations, with pauses allowed, give
\[
 \left|f_m(X_n(\gamma,0))-
 f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1Y_n)\right|
       \leq Cr_n+h_\delta(Y_n).
 \tag{ED9}
\]
The bounded range of $f_m$ justifies capping the diameter by two.

Take a countable sequence $\delta_k\downarrow0$. Birkhoff's theorem
applied to every $h_{\delta_k}$ gives
\[
 \limsup_N\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N
 \left|f_m(X_n(\gamma,0))-
 f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1Y_n)\right|
 \leq\mathbb E_{\mu_L}h_{\delta_k}(Y)
 \tag{ED10}
\]
simultaneously for all $m,k$. Lemma~\ref{lem:ed-goodradius} and dominated
convergence make the right side tend to zero as $k\to\infty$. Combine
this with (ED7). Only the initial segment of $\eta$ enters the argument,
since all sufficiently small physical exits occur inside the fixed local
chart. This proves (ED6).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A universal analytic-endpoint tensor decoder]
\label{thm:ed-analytic}
There is a Borel rule, depending only on the ordered initial trace germ,
the endpoint, and its oriented tangent half-plane, that recovers the
projective affine tensor from a single affine-SLE$_6$ sample in every
bounded simply connected marked domain with analytic regular source.
The rest of its boundary and its target may be rough prime-end data.
There is also a terminal version of the rule for every domain with
analytic regular target; the source may then be rough. The rule is the
same for two domains agreeing near that regular endpoint.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let the law be
$(A^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(AD,Ap,Aq)$ and assume $p$ is an analytic
regular source. A conformal map $f:\mathbb H\to AD$ with marks
$0,\infty$ extends conformally across $0$ by analytic boundary
reflection, with $f(0)=Ap$ and $f'(0)\ne0$. In a physical tangent frame
$Q$ carrying the interior tangent half-plane to $\mathbb H$, the local
map is
\[
 \Phi(z)=Q(A^{-1}f(z)-p)=Lz+O(|z|^2),
 \qquad L=QA^{-1}M_{f'(0)},
 \tag{ED11}
\]
where $M_c$ denotes multiplication by the nonzero complex number $c$.
The linear map $L$ carries $\mathbb H$ onto itself and has positive
diagonal entries in the boundary frame. Apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:ed-linearization}. The Borel inverse of (ED2) recovers
the scalar-normalized $L$, hence the projective tensor
\[
 [(L^{-1})^{\mathsf T}L^{-1}]
       =[QA^{\mathsf T}AQ^{\mathsf T}].
 \tag{ED12}
\]
Conjugating back by the known orthogonal frame $Q$ recovers
$[A^{\mathsf T}A]$. The derivative of the conformal map contributes
only a positive scalar to this tensor. This rule uses the endpoint
germ and tangent half-plane, not the rest of the conformal chart.

Ordinary SLE$_6$ reversibility, already an external theorem used in the
research record, says the reversed curve has the same affine-SLE form
with marks swapped. Apply the source decoder to that reversed curve at
the regular target. Reversibility on prime-end domains follows by
conformal transport from the half-plane theorem; only the regular
target germ needs a Euclidean analytic boundary extension.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Terminal singularity and identifiable mixtures]
\label{cor:ed-mixtures}
For one fixed marked domain with analytic regular target, affine-SLE$_6$
laws with distinct projective affine tensors are mutually singular on
the ordered terminal trace-germ sigma-field. More strongly, if
$\pi$ is any Borel probability on normalized affine tensors and
$P_\pi=\int P_T\,\pi(dT)$, then the terminal decoder has law $\pi$
under $P_\pi$. Consequently $\pi\mapsto P_\pi$ is injective, and no
nontrivial affine mixture can equal one pure affine-SLE law.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The same Borel decoder equals $T$ almost surely under $P_T$.
Integrating that equality proves the distribution statement. Distinct
pure laws concentrate on disjoint decoder fibers.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Additional initial-germ obstruction for random mixtures.}
At an analytic regular source, the initial decoder likewise returns the
latent tensor almost surely. Therefore a nontrivial mixture of affine
metrics has a nontrivial initial trace-germ sigma-field. At every
positive exploration time the metric posterior has already collapsed to
a point, even though its prior was nontrivial. This rules out such mixtures under an initial-germ zero-one premise.
Under the current literal strong DMP in the usual right-continuous
natural filtration, that premise is automatic by
Lemma~\ref{lem:dmp-zero-germ}. The independent-color version remains
useful before a limiting strong DMP theorem is available. A pure statewise fixed-metric assignment remains a distinct problem.
Section~\ref{sec:one-switch-construction} permits one boundary-recorded
change of metric and proves its restart consistency.

\begin{proposition}[DMP forces restarted mixing measures to be pure]
\label{prop:ed-posterior}
Suppose an all-state family has the mixed-affine representation
$P_S=\int\nu^T_S\,\pi_S(dT)$, with Borel mixing kernel $\pi_S$.
Start it in a domain with analytic regular source and target. If it has
strong DMP, then at each admissible stopping time $\tau>0$ before the
target is reached,
\[
 \pi_{S_\tau}=\delta_{D_s(\gamma)}\qquad\hbox{almost surely},
 \tag{ED13}
\]
where $D_s$ is the initial tensor decoder. In particular a rule that
uses a nondegenerate prior at every restarted state cannot satisfy DMP.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Sample the latent tensor $T$ with law $\pi_S$ and then sample the
fixed-$T$ affine SLE. The source decoder gives $D_s(\gamma)=T$ almost
surely, and this variable is measurable from every positive prefix,
including the prefix up to $\tau$. Conditional on this prefix,
ordinary fixed-$T$ SLE DMP therefore gives the pure continuation law
$\nu^{D_s(\gamma)}_{S_\tau}$. The family's DMP gives the mixed law with
mixing measure $\pi_{S_\tau}$. Since $\tau$ precedes reaching the
target, the surviving target end has the same analytic regular germ:
the compact prefix avoids a sufficiently small target neighborhood.
Corollary~\ref{cor:ed-mixtures} applied to this current state makes
the two mixing measures equal, proving (ED13).
\end{proof}


\section{Exact suffix coalescence survives curve limits}
\label{sec:coalescence-bridge}

The source-shield estimate contains more information than a small
Fr\'echet distance. Its exact common suffix is stable under taking a
joint curve limit, provided that the prefixes stay in a fixed closed set
away from the target. This allows the discontinuous terminal metric
decoder to be used without asserting that it is a continuous observable.

Let $K\subset\mathbb C$ be closed and consider two oriented continuous
curves. Define $E_K$ to be the relation
that there exist cut times $s_1,s_2$ in continuous representatives on
$[0,1]$ such that
\[
 \gamma_i[0,s_i]\subset K\quad(i=1,2),\qquad
 \gamma_1[s_1,1]=\gamma_2[s_2,1]
 \quad\text{as ordered unparameterized curves}.
 \tag{CB1}
\]
The relation is independent of the chosen representatives and allows
pauses, as does the usual uniform Fr\'echet curve space. It implies a
common endpoint, which may belong to $K$ in this definition. All
applications below have limiting common target $q\notin K$.

\begin{lemma}[A closed relation of common ordered suffixes]
\label{lem:cb-closed}
$E_K$ is closed in the product uniform-Fr\'echet curve space.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Suppose $(\gamma_1^n,\gamma_2^n)\to(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$ and the pairs
belong to $E_K$. Choose representatives of each convergent sequence
converging uniformly to fixed continuous representatives of its limit.
This is possible by the definition of the Fr\'echet metric; reparameterize
the cut times along with the curves. Pass to a subsequence on which both
cut times $s_i^n$ converge to $s_i\in[0,1]$.

Closedness of $K$ and uniform convergence give
$\gamma_i[0,s_i]\subset K$. Linear rescaling of $[s_i^n,1]$ to $[0,1]$ makes
the restricted curves converge uniformly to the restrictions on
$[s_i,1]$, by uniform continuity of $\gamma_i$ (a cut at $s_i=1$
gives a constant curve). The two restrictions
at stage $n$ have Fr\'echet distance zero. Their limits therefore also
have distance zero and are the same ordered curve. Thus the limiting
pair belongs to $E_K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Coalescence transfer and comparison of metric priors]
\label{prop:cb-priors}
Suppose pairs of discrete curves with a common target converge marginally
to laws $P_1,P_2$ on the oriented curve space, and their couplings satisfy
\[
 \mathbb P(E_K)\ge1-e_n,\qquad \limsup_n e_n\le e<1,
 \tag{CB2}
\]
where $K$ is a fixed closed ball not containing the target. Each limiting
domain is analytic and regular near that target, and the two colored
target germs agree. Suppose
\[
 P_i=\int P_{i,T}\,\pi_i(dT),
 \tag{CB3}
\]
where $P_{i,T}$ is the affine-SLE$_6$ law with normalized tensor $T$ in
domain $i$, and $\pi_i$ are Borel probability measures on tensors.
Then, with total variation defined as $\sup_B|\pi_1(B)-\pi_2(B)|$,
\[
 \|\pi_1-\pi_2\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le e.
 \tag{CB4}
\]
In particular if both marginals are pure affine-SLE laws, any positive
uniform lower bound on the discrete coalescence probability forces their
tensors to be equal.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Tightness of the marginals makes the coupled pairs tight. Take a joint
subsequential limit. Lemma~\ref{lem:cb-closed} and the closed-set
Portmanteau inequality give $\mathbb P(E_K)\ge1-e$ in the limit.
Because the target is outside $K$, the common suffix on $E_K$ includes
a nonconstant ordered terminal germ at that target. The universal
terminal decoder of Theorem~\ref{thm:ed-analytic} is the same for the
two domains, since their colored target germs agree. Let $T_i$ be its
value on curve $i$. The laws of these random variables are $\pi_i$,
and on $E_K$ they are equal. Hence
\[
 \sup_B|\mathbb P(T_1\in B)-\mathbb P(T_2\in B)|
       \le\mathbb P(T_1\ne T_2)\le e.
\]
For two distinct point masses the left-hand side equals one, proving the
last assertion. The proof uses exact suffix equality, not continuity of
the decoder and not just small curve distance.
\end{proof}

For the source-shield coupling, take $K=\overline B(p,R)$ with the
regular target outside this ball. Common discrete targets may vary with
mesh, provided $q_n\to q\notin K$; the closed-relation proof already allows
this. Equation (SH1) then gives
\[
 \|\pi_1-\pi_2\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
   \le C(\epsilon/R)^\alpha
 \tag{CB5}
\]
for two source-patch perturbations with common flat or straight-slit
annuli. This is a quantitative stability statement for mixing measures,
and yields exact tensor equality for pure marginals once the right side
is less than one. It is conditional on the affine-mixture marginal
ansatz; no such ansatz is derived from RSW here.

\section{Independent color resampling, initial germs, and a common affine metric}
\label{sec:affine-mixture-purity}


\begin{theorem}[Initial-germ zero-one law from independent colors and shielding]
\label{thm:ed-germzeroone}
Let $P$ be a subsequential exploration-curve limit of the independent
color model in a fixed domain with flat source germ. Assume the Polish
oriented Fr\'echet curve topology and the flat-source shield estimate of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ss-coalescence}. Then the initial ordered trace-germ
sigma-field is $P$-trivial. Precisely, if $\sigma_r\gamma$ denotes the
ordered prefix stopped on the radius-$r$ circle about the source, this
field is $\bigcap_{r>0}\overline{\sigma(\sigma_r\gamma)}^{\,P}$.
It is the usual ordered positive-prefix germ, with no clock chosen.
No affine ansatz, terminal tensor decoder,
limiting DMP theorem, or continuum configuration-measurability theorem
is required.

More quantitatively, for each sufficiently small $r$, every bounded
observable $F$ of the prefix stopped at radius $r$ and every bounded
Lipschitz observable $G$ of the entire curve satisfy
\[
 \left|\mathbb E_P(FG)-\mathbb E_PF\,\mathbb E_PG\right|
 \leq C\|F\|_\infty
   (\operatorname{Lip}G+\|G\|_\infty)r^{\alpha/(1+\alpha)},
 \tag{EDG}
\]
first at almost every stopping-continuity radius $r$. Constants and
length units are fixed by the source geometry. The zero-one assertion
itself holds on the full initial-germ sigma-field.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix a source-patch radius $\epsilon$ and couple two discrete
configurations with independent colors inside $B(p,\epsilon)$ and shared
independent colors outside. For any fixed $r<\epsilon/3$ and all small
enough meshes, the first exploration stopped at radius $r$ is determined
entirely by its own interior colors. It is therefore independent of the
\emph{entire second exploration}, which uses only the shared outer colors
and the second interior color set.

Take a joint subsequential curve limit. Both marginals equal $P$. Choose
an almost-sure stopping-continuity radius $r<\epsilon/3$. The continuous
mapping theorem preserves the product law, so the stopped prefix of
$\gamma_1$ is independent of the full curve $\gamma_2$. Consequently
any bounded initial-germ observable $F(\gamma_1)$ is independent of
$G(\gamma_2)$ for every bounded Lipschitz $G$ on the full curve space.
This step concerns the limiting product law; it does not attempt to pass
an arbitrary discontinuous germ statistic through a discrete limit.

The shield estimate supplies, in this same coupling, for a fixed small
$R$ and $\epsilon\ll R$,
\[
 \mathbb P\{d_{\mathrm F}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)>2R\}
       \leq C(\epsilon/R)^\alpha.
\]
Thus
\begin{align*}
 \left|\mathbb E_P(FG)-\mathbb E_PF\,\mathbb E_PG\right|
 &=\left|\mathbb E
       F(\gamma_1)(G(\gamma_1)-G(\gamma_2))\right|\\
 &\leq\|F\|_\infty
   \left(2\operatorname{Lip}(G)R+
           2C\|G\|_\infty(\epsilon/R)^\alpha\right).
 \tag{EDG1}
\end{align*}
The left side depends only on the marginal law $P$. Let
$\epsilon\downarrow0$ with $R$ fixed and then $R\downarrow0$.
It vanishes. Bounded Lipschitz functions determine finite signed
measures on a Polish space, so every bounded initial-germ observable
is independent of the full curve sigma-field. Since it belongs to
that sigma-field, it is almost surely constant. This proves initial
trace-germ triviality, including its completion.

For the quantitative prefix assertion, start with a fixed good radius
$r$, use $\epsilon=4r$, and take
$R=r^{\alpha/(1+\alpha)}$ after fixing the reference length unit.
For sufficiently small $r$ this is an admissible radius with
$\epsilon\ll R$. Formula (EDG1) gives (EDG).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Pure affine limit at a flat source]
\label{prop:ed-resampling}
Let one independent site-percolation model satisfy the flat-source shield
coupling of Theorem~\ref{thm:ss-coalescence}. Let $D$ have a flat source
germ. Suppose along one mesh subsequence its
exploration converges to
\[
 P=\int \nu^T_{D,p,q}\,\pi(dT),
 \tag{ED14}
\]
where $\pi$ is a Borel probability on projective affine tensors. Assume
the standard Polish oriented curve topology. Then $\pi$ is a point mass.
No limiting domain Markov theorem or continuum configuration-measurability
theorem is needed.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The regular source endpoint decoder has distribution $\pi$ under (ED14)
and is measurable in the initial trace-germ sigma-field. This sigma-field
is trivial by Theorem~\ref{thm:ed-germzeroone}. Thus the decoder is almost
surely one deterministic tensor, and $\pi$ is a point mass. The target
need only meet the curve-convergence assumptions here; analytic target
regularity enters the source-comparison theorem below.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Splicing a regular colored source germ]
\label{lem:ed-splicing}
Fix a bounded reference domain $D_0$ with flat regular source $p=0$,
analytic regular target $q$, and one fixed boundary-color convention.
Given any analytic regular colored source germ $G$ at $0$ with that
convention, and any sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, there is a bounded
marked domain $D_{G,\epsilon}$ agreeing with $D_0$ outside
$B(0,\epsilon)$ and having exactly the germ $G$ at $0$. Its target
germ at $q$ agrees with $D_0$. The boundary can be chosen smooth except
for any corners already present in $D_0$; only its two marked germs
need to be analytic.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For any analytic regular colored source germ $G$ at $p$ and any small
$\epsilon$, an orientation-preserving smooth diffeomorphism supported in
$B(p,\epsilon)$ can map the source germ of one fixed flat-source
reference domain to $G$, fixing $p$ and the distant regular target.
For an explicit local construction, use a radial twist to rotate a small
core to the required tangent, followed by a cutoff graph shear to realize
the analytic curvature in a still smaller core. The graph derivative can
be made arbitrarily small, so the cutoff shear remains a diffeomorphism.
Call the resulting domain $D_{G,\epsilon}$. It agrees with the reference
outside the source patch and agrees with $G$ in a still smaller neighborhood.
The support avoids $q$, and ambient orientation preserves the boundary
color order. Applying the diffeomorphism to $D_0$ proves the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One deterministic metric from a coherent affine mixture]
\label{thm:ed-coherent}
Consider a translation-covariant source-local exploration family arising
from one independent Bernoulli nonbranching cellular percolation model
admitting the flat-source shield coupling of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ss-coalescence}. Suppose each bounded-domain marginal
with analytic regular marks is a Borel mixture of fixed affine-SLE$_6$
laws. Require the family to include the source splices of
Lemma~\ref{lem:ed-splicing}, and require coherent subsequential limits
for these comparisons: for every such splice and the reference, compatible
common-color discrete approximations along one common mesh subsequence
realize the two specified marginal limits.

Then one deterministic projective affine tensor is shared by every
such marginal, and each marginal is the pure affine-SLE$_6$ law for
that tensor. Strong DMP and marked inverse-chart continuity are not
premises of this theorem.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The reference mixing measure $\pi_0$ is a point mass by
Proposition~\ref{prop:ed-resampling}. Fix another original domain with
analytic regular source germ $G$. Translation covariance allows its source
to be placed at $0$ without changing its physical projective tensor.
For every small $\epsilon$, source locality identifies the original
initial prefix law with that in $D_{G,\epsilon}$ through a common source
neighborhood. The source tensor decoder is a function of this prefix
and the common germ. Its distribution is the mixing measure in either
state, so both have one common mixing measure $\pi_G$, independent of
$\epsilon$.

Coherence and the common-color shield coupling give a coupling of the
two limiting curves in $D_{G,\epsilon}$ and $D_0$ such that their ordered
terminal segments agree outside an event of probability at most
$C(\epsilon/R)^\alpha$, for one fixed small $R$ in the shared flat
outer geometry. Their target germs coincide. Apply the two terminal
decoders; the resulting tensor variables have respective laws
$\pi_G,\pi_0$ and are equal on the coalescence event. The coupling
inequality for total variation therefore gives
\[
 \|\pi_G-\pi_0\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
       \leq C(\epsilon/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{ED16}
\]
Here total variation is $\sup_B|\pi_G(B)-\pi_0(B)|$.
Letting $\epsilon\downarrow0$ gives $\pi_G=\pi_0$. Since $\pi_0$ is
a point mass, the original marginal is pure and shares the same tensor.
The argument applies separately to each original regular-source state;
no simultaneous uncountable full-probability assertion is required.
\end{proof}

Thus, subject to the explicitly stated coherent independent-color scaling
and shielding premises, the mixed-affine ansatz on regular domains
already collapses to one deterministic common affine metric. This does
not derive the mixed-affine ansatz for an arbitrary curve law, and does
not classify arbitrary abstract three-axiom families.

\paragraph{Initial characteristic limits become deterministic.}
At any source to which the preceding zero-one argument applies, a finite
initial limit in probability of an adapted characteristic $A_t/t$ or
$C_t/t$ is deterministic. To see germ measurability, stop the characteristic
at the first exit from an arbitrary source ball. That exit has strictly
positive capacity time; the stopped and unstopped limits therefore agree.
A limit in probability of variables measurable from the stopped prefix has
a measurable version in its completed sigma-field. This holds for every
small radius, so the limit belongs to the trivial initial germ.
This removes the deterministic-value premise at these starts once existence
of a tangent and natural-trace-filtration compatibility are known; neither
tangent existence nor the required slit-uniform bound is obtained here.


\section{Total-variation decoupling of an initial prefix and a distant suffix}
\label{sec:spatial-beta-mixing}

The closed exact-coalescence relation gives a stronger consequence than
bounded-Lipschitz covariance. It controls arbitrary measurable observables
of the distant terminal suffix in total variation. This uses the actual
independent-color source-shield coupling, not just abstract locality or
an unforced-crossing bound.

Fix a bounded domain with source $p$, target $q$, flat source geometry,
and a marginal curve scaling limit $P$ satisfying the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ed-germzeroone}. Let $\sigma_r\gamma$ be the initial
ordered prefix stopped on the radius-$r$ circle about $p$. For
$R<|q-p|$ define $\lambda_R\gamma$ to be the ordered terminal suffix
beginning at the \emph{last} visit to $\overline B(p,R)$. For any
continuous representative on $[0,1]$ this means restriction from
\[
 t_R(\gamma)=\max\{t\in[0,1]:|\gamma(t)-p|\leq R\}
\]
to $1$, followed by linear reparameterization. The resulting ordered
curve does not depend on the representative or pauses.

Both stopping operations are Borel maps on the assumed Polish oriented
Fr\'echet curve space. For completeness, on the continuous-parameter
curve space, $t_R$ is Borel because, for fixed $a$,
\[
 \{t_R\geq a\}
 =\left\{\min_{a\leq t\leq1}|\gamma(t)-p|\leq R\right\}
\]
is closed. Restriction and rescaling are jointly continuous in a curve
and its cut parameter. Hence the terminal-suffix map to the quotient
curve space is Borel on representatives and invariant under
reparameterization. It descends to a Borel map on the Polish quotient:
the preimages of Borel sets are saturated Borel sets, whose images and
complements under the continuous quotient map are analytic; both are
therefore Borel. First-exit stopping has the same Borel property.

\begin{theorem}[Prefix--suffix decoupling]
\label{thm:spatial-beta}
There exist $C<\infty$ and $\alpha>0$, determined by the source-shield
constants, such that for all sufficiently small $R$ and all
$0<r<R/64$,
\[
 \left\|\mathcal L_P(\sigma_r\gamma,\lambda_R\gamma)
       -\mathcal L_P(\sigma_r\gamma)
           \otimes\mathcal L_P(\lambda_R\gamma)\right\|_{\rm TV}
 \leq C(r/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{SB1}
\]
Here total variation is $\sup_A|\mu(A)-\nu(A)|$ on the product
space. In particular this estimate holds at \emph{every} small radius
$r$, not merely almost every stopping-continuity radius.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Set $\epsilon=8r$. At the discrete level take two configurations with
independent colors in $B(p,\epsilon)$ and identical independent colors
outside that ball. Both marginal explorations have the same scaling
limit $P$. Their joint laws are tight. Choose a radius
$\rho\in(r,2r)$ at which first-exit stopping is $P$-almost surely
continuous; such radii have full Lebesgue measure by
Lemma~\ref{lem:lt-stopcontinuity}.
The first discrete exploration up to radius $\rho$ uses only the first
configuration's interior colors, once the mesh is sufficiently small.
It is consequently independent of the entire second exploration.
Continuous mapping along a joint subsequential limit gives
\[
 \sigma_\rho\gamma_1\ \perp\!\!\!\perp\ \gamma_2.
\]
Stopping the limiting first prefix again, now at radius $r$, is a Borel
operation and requires no continuity assertion at $r$. Thus
$\sigma_r\gamma_1$ is independent of the full curve $\gamma_2$.

Apply the source-shield estimate with shield radius $R/2$. Outside an
event of probability at most $C(r/R)^\alpha$, both prefixes before
coalescence stay in $K=\overline B(p,R/2)$ and the ordered suffixes
are identical. Lemma~\ref{lem:cb-closed} and the closed-set Portmanteau
inequality preserve this statement in the same joint subsequential
limit. Since $q\notin\overline B(p,R)$, each full curve's last visit
to $\overline B(p,R)$ is on the common suffix. Therefore
\[
 \mathbb P\{\lambda_R\gamma_1\ne\lambda_R\gamma_2\}
 \leq C(r/R)^\alpha.
\]
The pairs
$(\sigma_r\gamma_1,\lambda_R\gamma_1)$ and
$(\sigma_r\gamma_1,\lambda_R\gamma_2)$ form a coupling of the
joint law in (SB1) with the displayed product law. The usual coupling
bound for total variation proves (SB1).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Expected conditional total-variation stability]
\label{cor:spatial-conditional-tv}
Let $K_r(x,\cdot)$ be a regular conditional law of $\lambda_R\gamma$
given $\sigma_r\gamma=x$. Then
\[
 \int\left\|K_r(x,\cdot)-\mathcal L_P(\lambda_R\gamma)
          \right\|_{\rm TV}
       \,\mathcal L_P(\sigma_r\gamma)(dx)
 \leq C(r/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{SB2}
\]
Consequently for bounded Borel functions $F,G$ of the two respective
variables,
\[
 |\operatorname{Cov}_P(F(\sigma_r\gamma),G(\lambda_R\gamma))|
 \leq 2C\|F\|_\infty\|G\|_\infty(r/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{SB3}
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
On standard Borel spaces, total variation between a joint law and the
product of its marginals is the integral of the total variations of
the corresponding regular conditional laws. One may verify the identity
using a countable generating algebra to compute the variation of each
signed conditional measure. This gives (SB2). Integrating the bounded
product observable against the difference of the two probability laws
costs at most twice its supremum norm times the convention for total
variation used in (SB1), proving (SB3).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Variants and limitation.}
The same proof applies to any standard-Borel-valued observable $Y_R$
that agrees whenever two curves belong to $E_{\overline B(p,R/2)}$.
For example one may use the compact trace outside the open radius-$R$
ball instead of the ordered distant suffix. Measurability of a given
variant should be checked separately. Completed prefix sigma-fields
are harmless, since versions may be chosen modulo their common
marginal null sets.

This is quantitative spatial loss of memory at a deterministic regular
source. It does not identify a conditional continuation kernel in an
arbitrary random slit domain: the proof has no shield estimate uniform
over rough explored frontiers. In particular it neither proves strong
DMP in a curve limit nor the whole-slit stability premise of the
driving-rigidity results. The whole-tube approach based on boundary
three-arm estimates still needs the deterministic implication from
ordered curve disagreement to an appropriate arm event.


\section{Annular crossing statistics self-average at a regular source}
\label{sec:annular-scenery}

The same source resampling argument applies to a macroscopic observable
whenever the closed exact-coalescence relation forces its equality. It
therefore controls annular crossings made \emph{before the first outer
exit}, even if the full curve later returns arbitrarily close to the
source. No source nonreturn assumption is needed.

\begin{proposition}[Decoupling for coalescence-invariant observations]
\label{prop:scenery-decoupling}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:spatial-beta}, let $Y_R$ be
a Borel map from the oriented curve space into a standard Borel space.
Assume
\[
 (\gamma,\widetilde\gamma)\in E_{\overline B(p,R/2)}
       \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 Y_R(\gamma)=Y_R(\widetilde\gamma).
 \tag{AS1}
\]
Then, for $0<r<R/64$,
\[
 \bigl\|\mathcal L_P(\sigma_r\gamma,Y_R(\gamma))
      -\mathcal L_P(\sigma_r\gamma)
                    \otimes\mathcal L_P(Y_R(\gamma))\bigr\|_{\rm TV}
 \le C(r/R)^\alpha.
 \tag{AS2}
\]
The same conclusion holds if the first variable is any Borel function
of $\sigma_r\gamma$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use exactly the independent-inner/shared-outer coupling in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:spatial-beta}, including a good stopping-continuity
radius between $r$ and $2r$. Its limiting curves obey
$\sigma_r\gamma_1\perp\!\!\!\perp\gamma_2$ and
\[
 \mathbb P\bigl(E_{\overline B(p,R/2)}\bigr)
       \ge1-C(r/R)^\alpha.
\]
Assumption (AS1) implies
$\mathbb P\{Y_R(\gamma_1)\ne Y_R(\gamma_2)\}
\le C(r/R)^\alpha$. Couple the two pairs with common first variable
$\sigma_r\gamma_1$, as in the earlier proof. The second pair has the
product law by independence. This proves (AS2). Applying a Borel map
to the first variable cannot increase total variation.
\end{proof}

Fix $a>0$ sufficiently small that the source-shield construction applies
up to scale $a$, and $|q-p|>a$. Put
\[
 r_n=a2^{-n},\qquad n=1,2,\ldots.
\]
For a continuous representative of $\gamma$ define
\[
 \tau_n=\inf\{t:|\gamma(t)-p|=2r_n\},\qquad
 \eta_n=\max\{t\le\tau_n:|\gamma(t)-p|\le r_n\}.
 \tag{AS3}
\]
Let $B_n(\gamma)$ be the ordered restriction to $[\eta_n,\tau_n]$,
translated by $-p$ and dilated by $r_n^{-1}$. This is an oriented curve
in $\{1\le |z|\le2\}$ from its inner to its outer boundary. It is the
last inner-to-outer crossing before the first outer hit. Only this
crossing is recorded; later returns of the full curve are immaterial.

The map $B_n$ is Borel and invariant under changes of representative.
Indeed $\tau_n$ is a Borel first-hitting operation. Restrict first to
$[0,\tau_n]$, linearly reparameterized on $[0,1]$. On this stopped
curve, $\eta_n$ is a Borel last visit to the closed inner ball, by the
last-visit argument in Section~\ref{sec:spatial-beta-mixing}. Successive
restriction, translation, dilation, and passage to the oriented quotient
are Borel. In particular $B_n$ is a Borel function of
$\sigma_{2r_n}\gamma$.

There is a second property crucial to the proof: if
$(\gamma,\widetilde\gamma)\in E_{\overline B(p,r_n/2)}$, then
$B_n(\gamma)=B_n(\widetilde\gamma)$. The prefixes before the common
suffix remain strictly inside the inner circle, and the common suffix
starts there. Both first outer hits thus occur on that suffix, and so
do their last inner-ball visits before those hits. The corresponding
ordered crossing segments agree exactly.

\begin{theorem}[Exponential absolute regularity across dyadic source scales]
\label{thm:scenery-beta}
Under the same source-shield and independent-color limit hypotheses,
the annular crossing sequence $(B_n)_{n\ge1}$ satisfies
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\bigl\|\mathcal L_P\bigl((B_1,\ldots,B_m),
                                  (B_{m+k},B_{m+k+1},\ldots)\bigr)\\
 &\hspace{14mm}
 -\mathcal L_P(B_1,\ldots,B_m)
             \otimes\mathcal L_P(B_{m+k},B_{m+k+1},\ldots)
                         \bigr\|_{\rm TV}
 \le C_0 2^{-\alpha k},\qquad m,k\ge1.
 \end{split}
 \tag{AS4}
\]
The countable product on the fine-scale side is a standard Borel space.
Constants are independent of $m$ and $k$. No stationarity or dilation
covariance of the marginal curve limit is assumed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For $k\ge8$, apply Proposition~\ref{prop:scenery-decoupling} with
$R=r_m$ and $r=2r_{m+k}$. The vector
$Y_R=(B_1,\ldots,B_m)$ satisfies (AS1): every one of its inner
radii is at least $r_m$, so coalescence inside the radius-$r_m/2$
ball occurs before all the relevant inner and outer visits. The whole
countable vector $(B_{m+k},B_{m+k+1},\ldots)$ is a Borel function of
$\sigma_{2r_{m+k}}\gamma$, because every corresponding outer radius
is at most $2r_{m+k}$. Thus (AS2) gives the upper bound
\[
 C(2r_{m+k}/r_m)^\alpha=C2^{\alpha}2^{-\alpha k}.
\]
Enlarge $C_0$ to cover $1\le k<8$ by the trivial total-variation bound
one. Interchanging the two coordinates of a joint law preserves total
variation, giving (AS4) in the displayed order.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A strong law for bounded annular observations]
\label{cor:scenery-slln}
Let $f_n$ be any deterministic sequence of Borel real functions of an
annular oriented crossing, with $|f_n|\le1$. Put
$Z_n=f_n(B_n)$. Then
\[
 \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N\{Z_n-\mathbb E_P Z_n\}
          \longrightarrow0\qquad P\text{-almost surely}.
 \tag{AS5}
\]
In particular, for a fixed bounded Borel $f$, if the deterministic
Ces\`aro means
\[
 \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N\mathbb E_P f(B_n)
\]
converge to $c_f$, then the empirical means
$N^{-1}\sum_{n\le N}f(B_n)$ converge almost surely to $c_f$.
These assertions hold simultaneously for any prescribed countable
collection of choices of the sequence $(f_n)$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The total-variation estimate (AS4) and $|Z_n|\le1$ imply
\[
 |\operatorname{Cov}_P(Z_n,Z_j)|
       \le2C_0 2^{-\alpha|n-j|}\quad(n\ne j).
\]
Consequently, for $S_N=\sum_{n\le N}(Z_n-\mathbb E_P Z_n)$,
\[
 \mathbb E_P S_N^2\le C_1 N.
\]
Chebyshev's inequality and Borel--Cantelli give
$S_{\ell^2}/\ell^2\to0$ almost surely, since for every $\zeta>0$,
\[
 \sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
 \mathbb P\{|S_{\ell^2}|>\zeta\ell^2\}
 \le\frac{C_1}{\zeta^2}
                  \sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\ell^{-2}<\infty.
\]
For $\ell^2\le N<(\ell+1)^2$, boundedness of the centered summands
gives
$|S_N-S_{\ell^2}|\le2(2\ell+1)$. Dividing by $N$ yields (AS5).
Add the deterministic expectations for the second assertion, and
intersect countably many probability-one events for the last one.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Meaning and remaining gap.}
Annular crossing statistics of one limiting exploration self-average
over logarithmically many source scales. The centering sequence is
deterministic, even when the scaling limit itself has not been shown
dilation invariant. Neither the convergence of those deterministic
expectations nor their values is asserted. A source law that is
invariant under dyadic dilation would make the expectations constant,
but such invariance is an additional premise and is not inferred from
precompactness of the collection of scaling limits. The result supplies
a rigorous scale-statistics diagnostic without an affine-SLE ansatz;
it does not classify those deterministic statistics or produce random
slit-domain exploration kernels.


\subsection{A martingale approximation and a variance criterion for Gaussian scale fluctuations}
\label{sec:annular-martingale}

The exponential absolute regularity in (AS4) yields more than the centered
strong law. The following statements require no stationarity or dilation
covariance. Their time variable counts source scales, rather than Loewner
capacity.

Let $Z_j=f_j(B_j)$ with deterministic Borel $|f_j|\le1$, put
$Y_j=Z_j-\mathbb E Z_j$, and write
$\mathcal A_j=\sigma(B_1,\ldots,B_j)$, with $\mathcal A_0$ trivial.
Then $|Y_j|\le2$. For constants $C,c>0$ depending only on (AS4),
\[
 \left\|\mathbb E[Y_{j+k}\mid\mathcal A_j]\right\|_1
       \le Ce^{-ck},\qquad j\ge0,\ k\ge1.
 \tag{AM1}
\]
Indeed, test the joint law of the coarse vector and $Y_{j+k}$ against
a bounded sign of this conditional expectation times $Y_{j+k}$;
the product-law expectation vanishes. Boundedness then gives, for every
real $p\ge1$,
\[
 \left\|\mathbb E[Y_{j+k}\mid\mathcal A_j]\right\|_p
       \le C_1 e^{-ck/p}.
 \tag{AM2}
\]
Here $C_1$ can be chosen independent of $p$, by
$\|V\|_p^p\le2^{p-1}\|V\|_1$ when $|V|\le2$.

\begin{proposition}[An exponentially integrable annular corrector]
\label{prop:annular-martingale}
Under (AS4), define in $L^p$, for any finite $p\ge1$,
\[
 U_j=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbb E[Y_{j+k}\mid\mathcal A_j],
 \qquad U_0=0,
 \qquad D_j=Y_j+U_j-U_{j-1}.
 \tag{AM3}
\]
The definitions give common versions for all finite $p$ and all integer
$j$. The process $M_N=\sum_{j=1}^N D_j$ is a square-integrable
$\mathcal A_N$-martingale and
\[
 S_N:=\sum_{j=1}^N Y_j=M_N-U_N.
 \tag{AM4}
\]
For constants $a,C_2>0$,
\[
 \sup_j\mathbb E e^{a|U_j|}<\infty,
 \qquad \sup_j\mathbb E e^{a|D_j|}<\infty,
 \qquad
 \max_{0\le j\le N}|U_j|=O(\log N)\quad\hbox{almost surely}.
 \tag{AM5}
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Minkowski's inequality and (AM2) imply
\[
 \|U_j\|_p\le C_1\sum_{k\ge1}e^{-ck/p}
      =\frac{C_1}{e^{c/p}-1}\le\frac{C_1}c p.
\]
Thus the series converges in each $L^p$, with the same $L^1$ limit.
The tower property and termwise conditioning, justified by $L^1$
convergence, give
\[
 \mathbb E[Y_j+U_j\mid\mathcal A_{j-1}]=U_{j-1},
\]
so the $D_j$ are martingale differences. Telescoping gives (AM4).
The displayed $L^p$ bound and
$\|D_j\|_p\le2+\|U_j\|_p+\|U_{j-1}\|_p$ give uniform bounds
$\|U_j\|_p+\|D_j\|_p\le C_3p$.
Expanding the exponential into its power series and using
$m!\ge(m/e)^m$ proves the two exponential-moment assertions for
sufficiently small $a>0$. In particular
$\mathbb P(|U_j|>x)\le C_4e^{-ax}$ uniformly in $j$.
The union bound gives
\[
 \mathbb P\left(\max_{j\le N}|U_j|>A\log N\right)
       \le C_4 N^{1-aA}.
\]
Choose $aA>2$ and sum over integers $N\ge2$.
Borel--Cantelli proves the last assertion of (AM5).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Deterministic and conditional variance are distinct.}
Orthogonality of martingale differences and (AM5) show
\[
 \left|\operatorname{Var}(S_N)-\sum_{j\le N}\mathbb E D_j^2\right|
       =O(\sqrt N).
 \tag{AM6}
\]
A martingale central limit theorem would normally ask for convergence of
conditional variance averages. For the original bounded annular sequence,
absolute regularity allows a direct blocking proof using only a
deterministic variance limit, as follows.

\begin{theorem}[A deterministic variance criterion for an annular invariance principle]
\label{thm:annular-clt}
Suppose (AS4) holds and, for a deterministic $\sigma^2\ge0$,
\[
 \frac1N\operatorname{Var}(S_N)\longrightarrow\sigma^2.
 \tag{AM7}
\]
Let $\mathcal S_N$ be the continuous polygonal interpolation with
$\mathcal S_N(j/N)=S_j/\sqrt N$. On every fixed compact time interval,
\[
 \mathcal S_N\ \Longrightarrow\ \sigma B
       \quad\hbox{in the uniform topology}.
 \tag{AM8}
\]
Consequently $S_N/\sqrt N$ tends in law to a centered Gaussian of
variance $\sigma^2$. Condition (AM7) is an additional deterministic
premise; it is not inferred from RSW or (AS4).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
We give a blocking and moment proof to separate (AM7) from a hidden
stationarity or conditional-variance assumption. The covariance estimate
from (AS4) gives
\[
 |\operatorname{Cov}(Y_i,Y_j)|\le C e^{-c|i-j|},\qquad i\ne j.
 \tag{AM9}
\]
Thus the variance of a sum over any finite subset of indices is at most
a constant times its cardinality. The covariance of sums over any two
disjoint consecutive intervals, in either order, is uniformly bounded:
sum the geometric bound over the two distances from their common boundary.

First consider a centered bounded triangular array whose row is a
deterministic bounded weighting of $O(N)$ successive variables $Y_j$
and whose row-sum
variance divided by $N$ has a limit $v$. The absolute-regularity and
covariance bounds remain uniform. Split the row into big blocks of length
$p_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor$, separated by gaps of length
$q_N=\lceil A\log N\rceil$, and omit the final remainder. The number of
omitted indices is $O(Nq_N/p_N+p_N+q_N)=o(N)$, so their sum divided by
$\sqrt N$ tends to zero in $L^2$.

The total-variation distance between the joint big-block law and the
product of its block marginals is at most
$C(N/p_N)e^{-cq_N}=o(1)$ when $A$ is sufficiently large. To see this,
separate the final block from the entire preceding block vector using
(AS4), and iterate; total variation adds under this telescoping argument.
For independent blocks with these marginals, the sum of block variances
divided by $N$ tends to $v$. Indeed replacing the omitted variables
changes the original variance by $o(N)$, and the sum of cross-block
covariances is at most $CNp_Ne^{-cq_N}=o(N)$, by (AM9).
Each independent block divided by $\sqrt N$ is deterministically bounded
in magnitude by $Cp_N/\sqrt N=o(1)$. Taylor expansion of its characteristic
function through second order has total remainder bounded by this maximum
times the sum of the normalized block variances. Taking the product of
characteristic functions therefore gives the limit $e^{-v\theta^2/2}$.
The total-variation approximation and the omitted $L^2$ remainder prove
the same Gaussian limit for the original weighted row. This argument also
covers $v=0$; alternatively that case follows from Chebyshev's inequality.

Apply this triangular-array statement to every fixed linear combination
of increments of $S_{\lfloor Nt\rfloor}/\sqrt N$. Assumption (AM7) gives
$N^{-1}\operatorname{Var}(S_{\lfloor Nt\rfloor})\to\sigma^2t$.
The uniform bound on covariances between consecutive interval sums then
shows that a linear combination with increment coefficients $a_\ell$
and time intervals $(t_{\ell-1},t_\ell]$ has variance limit
$\sigma^2\sum_\ell a_\ell^2(t_\ell-t_{\ell-1})$.
Cram\'er--Wold thus gives the Brownian finite-dimensional limits.

For tightness, write an ordered quadruple of indices as
$i\le i+a\le i+a+b\le i+a+b+d$. Its centered-product expectation is
bounded in absolute value by
\[
 C e^{-c\max(a,d)}.
 \tag{AM10}
\]
When $a>0$, separate the first centered factor from the other three
using (AS4); when $d>0$, separate the last centered factor instead.
Use the stronger of these two estimates, or the trivial boundedness
estimate if both gaps vanish. This argument permits repeated middle
indices and repeated endpoint pairs. Summing (AM10) over quadruples
in any interval of $m$ indices gives
\[
 \mathbb E\left|\sum_{j=r+1}^{r+m}Y_j\right|^4\le C_5m^2,
 \tag{AM11}
\]
because $\sum_{a,d\ge0}e^{-c\max(a,d)}<\infty$ while the starting
index and the middle gap have $O(m^2)$ choices. For the polygonal
process, (AM11) gives
$\mathbb E|\mathcal S_N(t)-\mathcal S_N(s)|^4\le C_6|t-s|^2$
when $|t-s|\ge N^{-1}$: the two fractional endpoint pieces have
total magnitude at most $4/\sqrt N$, which is absorbed in this
fourth-moment bound. For shorter intervals use the deterministic
slope bound $2\sqrt N$, giving the same estimate. The usual
Kolmogorov tightness criterion, together with $\mathcal S_N(0)=0$,
proves tightness in the uniform topology. Combining this with the
finite-dimensional limits proves (AM8).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A two-scale criterion for the variance limit.}
As a more explicit sufficient target, suppose every fixed lag $k\ge0$
has a deterministic Ces\`aro covariance limit
\[
 c_k=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
          \sum_{j=1}^{N-k}\operatorname{Cov}(Y_j,Y_{j+k}).
\]
Then (AM9) and dominated summation imply (AM7), with the absolutely
convergent formula
\[
 \sigma^2=c_0+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_k.
 \tag{AM12}
\]
This provides a condition in terms of two-scale expectations, without
requiring exact stationarity. No convergence of these expectations is
asserted for the percolation scaling limits under study.

\paragraph{The variance premise cannot be dropped.}
Even independent bounded observations can have no asymptotic variance
rate. For example let $Y_j=a_j\varepsilon_j$, with independent symmetric
signs $\varepsilon_j$ and deterministic $a_j\in\{0,1\}$. Choose
alternating $0$ and $1$ blocks whose successive lengths dominate all
previous lengths. Then (AS4) holds with zero error, but
$N^{-1}\operatorname{Var}(S_N)=N^{-1}\sum_{j\le N}a_j$ has subsequential
limits $0$ and $1$. Thus the new invariance principle isolates another
deterministic scale-statistics limit; it does not identify universal
expectations or fluctuation variances and does not control Loewner-capacity
increments.


% Checked affine-stabilizer argument; the application to frontiers remains open.
\section{An isotropic whole-plane law identifies its affine tensor}
\label{sec:wholeplane-decoder}

\begin{lemma}[Only similarities stabilize an isotropic scale-invariant curve law]
\label{lem:wp-stabilizer}
Let $\mu$ be the law of an almost surely nonconstant oriented continuous
curve starting at $0$, with compact continuous prefixes up to every
finite spatial exit. Suppose its law is invariant under every rotation
and every positive scalar dilation. If $L\in GL^+(2,\RR)$ preserves
$\mu$, then $L$ is a similarity. Consequently
\[
 A_*\mu=B_*\mu
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 AA^{\mathsf T}=cBB^{\mathsf T}\text{ for some }c>0.
 \tag{WP1}
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If $L$ is not a similarity, singular value decomposition, together
with the rotation and scalar symmetries, makes
$D=\operatorname{diag}(1,a)$, $0<a<1$, another symmetry of $\mu$.
For $Y\sim\mu$, the law of $X=\sup|\operatorname{Re}Y|$
is invariant under all positive scalar multiplications; hence
$X\in\{0,\infty\}$ almost surely. The event $X=0$ says the
nonconstant curve is contained in one specified line through $0$.
Its images under countably many distinct rotations are disjoint and
have equal probabilities, so each has probability zero. Thus $X=\infty$
almost surely. In particular the first time $T$ with
$|\operatorname{Re}Y(T)|=1$ is finite almost surely, and
$M=\sup_{s\le T}|\operatorname{Im}Y(s)|<\infty$.

The first unit-radius exit of $D^nY$ occurs by $T$; its initial
trace stays within distance $a^nM$ of the real axis. Since $D^nY$
has law $\mu$, for every $\epsilon>0$ the probability that the
unit-radius stopped trace leaves the $\epsilon$-strip is at most
$\mathbb P(a^nM>\epsilon)\to0$. Thus the unit-radius initial
trace lies on the real axis almost surely. Rotation invariance
also puts it on the imaginary axis almost surely, a contradiction.
Finally $A_*\mu=B_*\mu$ is equivalent to $B^{-1}A$ stabilizing
$\mu$, and a direct matrix calculation gives (WP1).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A conditional whole-plane initial-germ decoder]
\label{prop:wp-fixed-decoder}
Suppose additionally that the curve space is Polish, affine image and
first-radius stopping are jointly Borel, and spatial dilation by one
fixed factor $\rho>1$ is ergodic under $\mu$. Then there is one Borel
initial ordered trace-germ decoder $D$ such that, for every fixed
$A\in GL^+(2,\RR)$,
\[
 A_*\mu\left\{D(Y)=[(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}]\right\}=1.
 \tag{WP2}
\]
No tangent half-plane or explicit SLE passage formula is needed.
This first version concerns a fixed deterministic affine map.
Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} below gives a stronger simultaneous
statement for sample-dependent local differentiable maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Parameterize the tensors by positive definite determinant-one matrices
$T$, choosing $A_T=T^{-1/2}$. Rotation and scalar invariance imply
that every affine-image law has exactly this representation.
Take a countable bounded determining family $(f_m)$ for unit-radius
stopped curves, and set
$v_m(T)=\mathbb E_{(A_T)_*\mu}f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1Y)$.
This vector is Borel by the assumed joint coding. It is injective:
equal unit-radius laws give equal stopped laws at every radius by
dilation invariance, then equal full laws, and hence equal $T$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:wp-stabilizer}. Lusin--Souslin gives a Borel inverse
on the image of $v$.

Under any fixed $(A_T)_*\mu$, Birkhoff averages of
$f_m(\operatorname{Stop}_1(\rho^nY))$ tend almost surely to
$v_m(T)$. Dilation ergodicity transfers because affine image
commutes with scalar dilation. Each sufficiently late term uses
only an arbitrarily small initial prefix of $Y$.
The vector of limiting averages is therefore germ measurable.
Apply the Borel inverse of $v$ when the vector lies in its image,
and otherwise use a cemetery value. This proves (WP2).
\end{proof}

% Candidate continuation: full spherical empirical measures yield a robust
% ordered-germ tensor decoder.  Core files are maintained by the parent.
\subsection{A decoder stable under random local differentiable maps}
\label{sec:wp-robust-decoder}

The fixed-affine decoder can be strengthened so that its exceptional set
does not depend on the local coordinate map.  The useful change is to
average whole curves in the Riemann sphere before extracting the tensor;
first-radius stopping is then used only for Borel germ coding, not for
continuity of an averaging observable.

We make an additional explicit properness and coding assumption.
Let $\mathcal C$ be a Polish space of oriented continuous curves in the
Riemann sphere from $0$ to $\infty$, modulo increasing reparameterization
and pauses, with bounded spherical Fr\'echet metric $d_{\mathcal C}$,
induced by the chordal metric on the sphere.
Assume the affine action and scalar dilation are continuous on this
space and first-radius stopping is Borel.  Require the proper and
source-avoiding curve sets to be Borel in this coding.  These are properties of the
coding, not consequences of the exploration axioms.  A curve is
\emph{proper} here if it has a continuous planar representative
$y:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb C$ with $|y(t)|\to\infty$ as $t\to\infty$.

\begin{lemma}[No later visit to the source]
\label{lem:wp-no-source-return}
Let $\mu$ be a dilation-invariant law supported on proper curves from
$0$ to $\infty$.  Then almost surely the curve has no visit to $0$
after it first leaves $0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $R>0$, let $E_R$ be the event of a visit to $0$ after the first
radius-$R$ exit.  Dilation invariance makes $\mu(E_R)$ independent of
$R$.  The events decrease as $R$ increases.  For every proper continuous
curve, all visits to $0$ occur in a finite time interval, whose initial
trace has finite radius.  Thus $\bigcap_{R>0}E_R$ is empty on the
proper-curve set, and $\mu(E_R)=0$ for every $R$.  A nontrivial later
visit to $0$ belongs to $E_R$ for some positive rational $R$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform spherical linearization]
\label{lem:wp-spherical-linearization}
Let $\psi$ be an orientation-preserving $C^1$ diffeomorphism on a
neighborhood of $0$, with $\psi(0)=0$ and derivative $L\in GL^+(2,\RR)$.
It has a global bi-Lipschitz extension $\Psi$ agreeing with $\psi$ on
a smaller neighborhood and fixing $\infty$, such that
\[
 \sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathbb C}}
 d_{\rm sph}\bigl(\lambda\Psi(z),\lambda Lz\bigr)
       \longrightarrow0\quad(\lambda\to\infty).
 \tag{WP3}
\]
Consequently the same upper bound controls
$d_{\mathcal C}(\lambda\Psi Y,\lambda LY)$ for every curve $Y$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $\psi(z)=Lz+r(z)$, where $r(0)=0$ and $Dr(z)\to0$.
Choose a smooth radial cutoff $\chi_\delta$, equal to one in
$B(0,\delta)$ and zero outside $B(0,2\delta)$, and set
$\Psi(z)=Lz+\chi_\delta(z)r(z)$, with the perturbation zero outside
the chart.  For sufficiently small $\delta$ the Lipschitz norm of
the perturbation is less than half the smallest singular value of
$L$.  Hence $\Psi$ is globally bi-Lipschitz.  Surjectivity follows,
for example, from the contraction mapping applied to
$z=L^{-1}(w-\chi_\delta(z)r(z))$ for each $w$.

There is $c>0$ with $|\Psi(z)|\ge c|z|$ and $|Lz|\ge c|z|$.
Given $\eta>0$, choose $a>0$ such that
$|\Psi(z)-Lz|\le\eta|z|$ for $|z|\le a$.
The chordal formula (our convention for $d_{\rm sph}$)
\[
 d_{\rm sph}(u,v)=
 \frac{|u-v|}{\sqrt{1+|u|^2}\sqrt{1+|v|^2}}
\]
then bounds the distance in (WP3), for $|z|\le a$, by
\[
 \frac{\eta\lambda|z|}{1+c^2\lambda^2|z|^2}
       \le\frac{\eta}{2c}.
\]
For $|z|\ge a$, the triangle inequality through $\infty$ bounds
the distance by $2/(c\lambda a)$.  First let $\lambda\to\infty$
and then $\eta\downarrow0$.  Pairing identical curve parameters
gives the Fr\'echet bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Changing a distant tail is invisible after magnification]
\label{lem:wp-tail-replacement}
Suppose two proper source-avoiding curves share a nonconstant initial
ordered segment.  Then
\[
 d_{\mathcal C}(\lambda Y,\lambda\widetilde Y)
       \longrightarrow0\quad(\lambda\to\infty).
 \tag{WP4}
\]
Here source avoidance means that neither curve visits $0$ after its
initial departure.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Synchronize parameters along a common initial segment ending at a
nonzero point.  Each remaining tail is a compact subset of the sphere
disjoint from $0$; its finite planar points therefore have modulus at
least some positive $b$.  After multiplication by $\lambda$, both
tails lie within chordal distance $1/(\lambda b)$ of $\infty$.
Match their remaining parameters arbitrarily.  The common prefixes
agree exactly and the two tails have mutual distance at most
$2/(\lambda b)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A robust affine decoder on initial ordered germs]
\label{thm:wp-robust-decoder}
Let $\mu$ be a nonconstant rotation- and dilation-invariant law on
$\mathcal C$, supported on proper curves, and suppose dilation by
one factor $\rho>1$ is ergodic.  There is one Borel tensor-valued
decoder $D$, measurable from every positive-radius initial prefix
on the proper source-avoiding curve set, with the following
simultaneous property.  There is a single $\mu$-conull set
$\mathcal G$ such that, for every $Y\in\mathcal G$, every
$A\in GL^+(2,\RR)$, and every local orientation-preserving
$C^1$ diffeomorphism $\psi$ fixing $0$, with derivative $L$,
\[
 D\bigl(\psi(AY)\bigr)
    =\bigl[((LA)^{-1})^{\mathsf T}(LA)^{-1}\bigr].
 \tag{WP5}
\]
The argument of $D$ can be any proper source-avoiding completion
of the indicated local curve segment; the value is independent of
the completion.  In particular $A$ and $\psi$ may depend arbitrarily
on $Y$ and on additional randomness, provided the displayed local
regularity holds for each realization.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $T$ act by multiplication by $\rho$, and form empirical orbit
measures in the Polish probability-measure space $\mathcal P(\mathcal C)$:
\[
 \Pi_N(Y)=\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N\delta_{T^nY}.
\]
The ergodic theorem applied to a countable bounded continuous
convergence-determining family gives a single conull Borel set
$\mathcal G$ on which $\Pi_N(Y)\Rightarrow\mu$.  Intersect with
the proper source-avoiding set, using
Lemma~\ref{lem:wp-no-source-return}.

For every $Y\in\mathcal G$ and every fixed linear $B$, commutation
of $B$ with $T$ and continuity of its action give
\[
 \Pi_N(BY)=B_*\Pi_N(Y)\Rightarrow B_*\mu.
 \tag{WP6}
\]
This is a deterministic consequence of weak convergence for the
given $Y$, so it holds simultaneously for all $B$; it does not
intersect uncountably many probability-one events.

Extend $\psi$ to $\Psi$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:wp-spherical-linearization}.  Applying that lemma
to $\Psi\circ A$ gives
$d_{\mathcal C}(T^n\Psi AY,T^nLAY)\to0$.
For every bounded Lipschitz test function, the difference of its
empirical averages consequently tends to zero by Ces\`aro averaging.
Together with (WP6), this gives
\[
 \Pi_N(\Psi AY)\Rightarrow(LA)_*\mu.
 \tag{WP7}
\]
Again this is a pathwise assertion for every admissible $A,\psi$.

To decode the limiting law, parameterize projective positive
definite tensors by determinant-one matrices $Q$ and set
$A_Q=Q^{-1/2}$.  The map
\[
 Q\longmapsto(A_Q)_*\mu\in\mathcal P(\mathcal C)
\]
is continuous by the continuous affine action and is injective by
Lemma~\ref{lem:wp-stabilizer}.  Its image is Borel and its inverse
is Borel by Lusin--Souslin.  Apply that inverse to the weak limit
of $\Pi_N$ when the limit exists and belongs to this image; otherwise
assign a cemetery value.  This defines a Borel decoder.
Rotation and scalar invariance identify the tensor of the law on
the right side of (WP7) as the class in (WP5).

It remains to check germ measurability.  For a proper source-avoiding
curve $Y$, replace the tail after its first radius-$r$ exit by the
radial ray to $\infty$, obtaining $E_rY$.  This is a Borel function
of the initial prefix: use Borel stopping followed by continuous
concatenation with the radial ray in the spherical curve coding.
Lemma~\ref{lem:wp-tail-replacement} gives
$d_{\mathcal C}(T^nY,T^nE_rY)\to0$.  Their empirical measures
therefore have weak limits simultaneously, with the same limit
when it exists.  More precisely, their bounded-Lipschitz distance is at most
$N^{-1}\sum_{n\le N}d_{\mathcal C}(T^nY,T^nE_rY)\to0$.
Hence $D(Y)=D(E_rY)$ for every $r>0$, including when the decoder
has the cemetery value.  The same
argument makes the decoder independent of the completion of a
local curve segment and completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Scope for random tip charts.}
If $L$ is conformal, the decoded tensor changes by the usual
covariant rule $Q\mapsto[L^{-\mathsf T}QL^{-1}]$.
If a random physical local curve is obtained by first applying a
random conformal local map to $Y$ and then a fixed affine map $A$,
its derivative is $A(aR)$ and (WP5) decodes
$[(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}]$, independently of the random
similarity $aR$.  Thus dependence of the local map on the sampled
curve is not by itself an obstruction once this proper,
dilation-ergodic whole-plane model and a $C^1$ local chart are known.
This theorem still supplies an ordered-germ decoder.  It does not
select a canonical point of an unlabelled frontier, prove an
intrinsic typical-tip theorem, establish compatibility at arbitrary
exploration stopping times, or derive the required whole-plane law
and dilation ergodicity from the three exploration axioms.


% Additional candidate: full discrete-scale ergodicity of the stationary
% whole-plane SLE driver, independently of the one-dimensional Z statistic.
\subsection{Full scaling ergodicity from the stationary cylinder driver}
\label{sec:wp-cylinder-ergodicity}

Zhan's construction in Section 3 of \cite{zhan-tip} gives more than
an ergodic one-dimensional angular statistic once the full cylinder
process is retained.  The following argument supplies the full
discrete-dilation ergodicity premise; it uses the classical
H\"ormander hypoellipticity theorem for the smoothing step.

\begin{proposition}[Every stationary cylinder skeleton is ergodic]
\label{prop:wp-cylinder-ergodic}
Let $\kappa>0$, $\varrho\ge\kappa/2-2$, and
$a=\varrho/2+1>0$.  Let $Z_t\in(0,2\pi)$ be the two-sided stationary
diffusion
\[
 dZ_t=\sqrt\kappa\,dB_t+a\cot(Z_t/2)\,dt,
 \qquad
 \pi_Z(dz)=c\sin(z/2)^{4a/\kappa}\,dz.
 \tag{WP8}
\]
Let $q_0$ be uniform modulo $2\pi$, independent of the entire
stationary path $Z$, and set
\[
 q_t=q_0-\int_0^t\cot(Z_s/2)\,ds\pmod{2\pi}.
 \tag{WP9}
\]
Then the cylinder process $(q_t,Z_t)$ is stationary, and its
two-sided stationary path law is ergodic under every fixed time
shift $h\ne0$.  Consequently any Borel whole-plane Loewner trace
constructed from $e^{i\lambda_t}$, $\lambda_t=q_t+Z_t$, is ergodic
under multiplication by every fixed scalar $e^h\ne1$ as an
unparameterized curve, on a conull invariant set where the trace is defined.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The dimension of the radial Bessel diffusion is
$1+4a/\kappa\ge2$, so its endpoints are not hit; this is the
stationary construction in \cite[Section 3 and Appendix B]{zhan-tip}.
Uniform independent $q_0$ makes $q_t$ uniform conditional on the
entire path $Z$, proving stationarity and giving the invariant
probability $\pi=(dq/(2\pi))\pi_Z(dz)$, with full support.

The Markov generator on the open cylinder is
\[
 \mathcal L=\frac\kappa2\partial_z^2
       +a\cot(z/2)\partial_z-\cot(z/2)\partial_q.
 \tag{WP10}
\]
Write $V_1=\sqrt\kappa\,\partial_z$ and
$V_0=a\cot(z/2)\partial_z-\cot(z/2)\partial_q$.  Then
\[
 [V_1,V_0]
   =\frac{\sqrt\kappa}{2}\csc^2(z/2)
                         (-a\partial_z+\partial_q).
 \tag{WP11}
\]
Thus $V_1$ and $[V_1,V_0]$ span the spatial tangent space at every
interior point.  The parabolic H\"ormander theorem applied to
$\mathcal L-\partial_t$ gives the strong Feller property at every
positive time.  Here is the localization needed for the singular
endpoints.  On a relatively compact subcylinder, the killed backward
semigroup applied to a bounded Borel function solves the homogeneous
backward Kolmogorov equation in the interior at positive times, in
the distributional sense.  H\"ormander's theorem~\cite[Theorem 1.1]{hormander} makes this solution
smooth there.  For any initial interior point and finite horizon,
endpoint nonattainment gives a compact subcylinder containing the
entire path with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$.  Choose a
slightly larger subcylinder.  Coupling nearby starting points with
the same Brownian motion, local Lipschitz continuity and Gronwall's
inequality show that their paths remain in the larger subcylinder
on this same event, for all sufficiently close starting points.
The full semigroup thus differs from that killed semigroup by at
most $\|f\|_\infty\varepsilon$, uniformly near the given point.
Let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ to obtain continuity for every bounded
Borel $f$.  H\"ormander smoothing is an external analytic input,
not a consequence of stationarity alone.

For clarity, topological irreducibility at every fixed time $h>0$
can be checked directly, without a general support theorem.  Given
two cylinder points $(q,z)$ and $(q',z')$, choose a smooth controlled
path $z(s)$, $0\le s\le h$, from $z$ to $z'$, remaining in a
compact subinterval of $(0,2\pi)$.  It can be chosen so that
\[
 -\int_0^h\cot(z(s)/2)\,ds=q'-q\pmod{2\pi}.
\]
Indeed let the path move to a variable interior level, remain there
for a positive time, and return to $z'$.  As that level ranges from
$0$ to $2\pi$, the integral varies continuously from $-\infty$
to $+\infty$.  The required deterministic Brownian control is
\[
 w(s)=\kappa^{-1/2}\left(z(s)-z-
                     a\int_0^s\cot(z(u)/2)\,du\right).
\]
It is a Cameron--Martin path.  Brownian motion has positive
probability of lying in every uniform neighborhood of $w$.
Local Lipschitz dependence of the additive-noise SDE and its
integrated $q$ coordinate shows that the uncontrolled process ends
in any prescribed neighborhood of $(q',z')$ with positive
probability.  Hence each skeleton kernel $P_h$ is topologically
irreducible.

A strong Feller irreducible Markov kernel has at most one invariant
probability, and its stationary probability is an extreme invariant
probability.  Equivalently its two-sided stationary Markov path
law is shift-ergodic.  Apply this standard Markov-kernel fact to
$P_h$ for every fixed $h>0$.  The same conclusion holds on the
full continuous path space under time shift $h$.  Indeed consider
the Markov chain of path segments $(X_{nh+s})_{0\le s\le h}$,
where $X=(q,Z)$.  Its transition law depends only on the previous
endpoint.  The endpoint law of any invariant segment law must be
invariant for $P_h$, hence must equal $\pi$.  The segment law is
then necessarily the law obtained by starting the diffusion from
$\pi$ and recording its next length-$h$ segment.  Thus the segment
chain has a unique invariant law and is ergodic; concatenation
intertwines its shift with the full continuous-path shift by $h$.
Negative shifts have the same invariant events.

Finally the deterministic whole-plane Loewner correspondence
intertwines shifting $\lambda_t$ to $\lambda_{t+h}$ with mapping
the capacity-parameterized trace $\gamma(t)$ to
$e^{-h}\gamma(t+h)$.  After forgetting the parameter this is
scalar multiplication by $e^{-h}$.  A Borel factor of an ergodic
transformation is ergodic, proving the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Application and remaining premise.}
For $0<\kappa<4$ and $\varrho=\kappa+2$, the stationary
whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ driver of
\cite[Section 3]{zhan-tip} therefore has full dilation ergodicity,
not only the ergodicity of its coordinate $Z$ used in Section 6
of that paper.  Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} still
requires its stated Borel spherical curve coding and proper
continuous trace from $0$ to $\infty$; this proposition supplies
the ergodicity input but does not prove those geometric inputs.

\begin{corollary}[An affine tensor at a deterministic simple-SLE tip]
\label{cor:wp-deterministic-tip-tensor}
Fix $0<\kappa<4$ and assume the proper spherical curve coding
premises of Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} for the whole-plane
SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ law.  Let $\beta$ be a chordal SLE$_\kappa$
trace, let $t_0>0$ be a deterministic half-plane capacity time,
and let $A\in GL^+(2,\RR)$ be fixed.  The same decoder applied
to the reversed ordered germ of $A\beta$ at $A\beta(t_0)$,
translated to start at $0$, almost surely returns
\[
 [(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}].
 \tag{WP12}
\]
For a general simply connected domain, use a conformal parameter
chart and its corresponding deterministic chordal capacity time.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Theorem 5.3 of \cite{zhan-tip} maps the reversed past of $\beta$
at $t_0$ by a random conformal map to an initial segment of a
whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ trace.  Its local inverse at
$0$ has a random nonzero conformal derivative $aR$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:wp-cylinder-ergodic} supplies the whole-plane
discrete-dilation ergodicity.  Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} to this random inverse chart
followed by $A$.  The derivative is $A(aR)$, whose tensor class
is exactly (WP12).  The decoder needs only the initial ordered
germ, so the whole-plane segment can be completed arbitrarily.
\end{proof}

This corollary holds simultaneously at any prescribed countable
set of deterministic capacity times.  It does not assert correctness
at every tip, at a tip chosen by an arbitrary intrinsic rule, or
at a stopping time selected by the trace.



\subsection{An intrinsic measure class without affine Minkowski convergence}
\label{sec:intrinsic-tube-measure}

There is a way to address the measure-selection part of the frontier
problem without first proving existence of Minkowski content after every
affine deformation. This does not supply a typical-tip theorem or a
Borel choice of the relevant local frontier arc.

Fix $0<d<2$. For a nonempty compact planar set $E$ and $\epsilon>0$ put
\[
 m_{E,\epsilon}(dx)=\epsilon^{d-2}
       \mathbf1_{\{\operatorname{dist}(x,E)<\epsilon\}}\,dx.
 \tag{IM1}
\]
Call $E$ content regular if these finite measures converge weakly, as
$\epsilon\downarrow0$, to a finite nonzero measure $\mu_E$.
This is an explicit local Minkowski-content hypothesis. The theorem below
is deterministic; no SLE or exploration assumption enters its proof.

\begin{theorem}[A Euclidean canonical affine-stable measure class]
\label{thm:intrinsic-tube-measure}
There is a Borel map $E\mapsto\nu_E$ from compact planar sets to finite
positive measures, supported on $E$, with the following properties.
It has exact $d$-dimensional similarity covariance: for
$F(x)=sRx+t$, $s>0$ and $R$ orthogonal,
$\nu_{FE}=s^d F_*\nu_E$.
For every content-regular $E$, $\nu_E=\mu_E$. If $A$ is invertible
real-linear, with smallest and largest singular values $a,b>0$, then
\[
 |\det A|\,b^{d-2} A_*\mu_E
       \ \le\ \nu_{AE}\ \le\
 |\det A|\,a^{d-2} A_*\mu_E
 \tag{IM2}
\]
as measures. In particular, $\nu_{AE}$ and $A_*\mu_E$ have precisely
the same null sets. No convergence of the tube measures of $AE$ is
assumed. Exact affine covariance of the selected measure is not claimed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Consider the Borel set of compact sets
\[
 \mathcal E_d=\left\{E:
 0<\liminf_{\epsilon\downarrow0}m_{E,\epsilon}(\mathbb R^2)
   \le\limsup_{\epsilon\downarrow0}m_{E,\epsilon}(\mathbb R^2)<\infty\right\}.
\]
The tube-measure map in (IM1) is Borel in the Hausdorff hyperspace of
compact sets: the distance is jointly continuous in $(E,x)$, so integrals
against bounded continuous test functions are Borel by measurable
integration. The tube measure also depends continuously on positive
$\epsilon$. Indeed every positive distance level has zero area:
if $\operatorname{dist}(x,E)=r>0$, a nearest point of $E$ gives, in
each sufficiently small ball about $x$, a ball of one quarter its radius
where the distance is strictly below $r$. Thus the level set has no
Lebesgue density points. Dominated convergence now proves the asserted
continuity. All small-radius limit operations may consequently be coded
using rational radii, proving that $\mathcal E_d$ is Borel.
For $E\in\mathcal E_d$, the sufficiently small-radius tube measures have
uniformly bounded masses and a common compact support, namely the closed
unit neighborhood of $E$. Their weak cluster set $K(E)$ as $\epsilon\downarrow0$ is therefore
nonempty compact. Every cluster measure is supported on $E$, and its
mass lies between the displayed positive liminf and finite limsup.
Let $C(E)$ be the closed convex hull of $K(E)$; it is compact, since it
lies in the compact set of measures supported on $E$ with the same
uniform mass bounds.

For clarity, these compact-valued constructions admit Borel coding.
Choose a complete metric for weak convergence of finite positive measures.
For every open set $O$ in that measure space,
\[
 K(E)\cap O\ne\varnothing
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{for some }k,\text{ for every }m,\text{ some }
           \epsilon\in\mathbb Q\cap(0,1/m)\\
 \text{has }\operatorname{dist}(m_{E,\epsilon},O^c)>1/k.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{IM3}
\]
Precompactness proves both implications. The right side is Borel.
Using a countable basis of balls and (IM3), choose a point of $K(E)$
in each basis open set which meets it: successively take the first
smaller basis ball meeting $K(E)$ whose closure lies in the preceding
open ball, with radii tending to zero. The centers form a Cauchy
sequence; its limit lies in $K(E)$ and in the original basis open set.
All choices are least-integer choices of Borel predicates.
This gives a Borel countable dense family in $K(E)$, and its rational
convex combinations give a Borel countable dense family in $C(E)$.

Select $\nu_E$ to minimize the Gaussian energy
\[
 J_E(\nu)=\iint e^{-|x-y|^2/\operatorname{diam}(E)^2}\,\nu(dx)\nu(dy)
 \tag{IM4}
\]
over $C(E)$. The diameter is positive on $\mathcal E_d$, since a singleton
has tube mass of order $\epsilon^d\to0$. The energy is continuous for weak convergence of finite
positive measures, and its dependence on $E$ is Borel. It is strictly convex: for a nonzero finite signed
measure $\eta$, the Gaussian Fourier identity gives
\[
 \iint e^{-|x-y|^2/\operatorname{diam}(E)^2}\,\eta(dx)\eta(dy)
   =c_E\int e^{-\operatorname{diam}(E)^2|\xi|^2/4}
                  |\widehat\eta(\xi)|^2d\xi>0,
\]
where $c_E>0$. The last inequality
uses continuity and uniqueness of the Fourier transform of a finite
measure. Thus the minimizer exists and is unique. It is Borel in $E$:
the infimum over the countable dense family in $C(E)$ is Borel;
choose its first member within $1/j$ of that infimum. Compactness and
uniqueness force these approximate minimizers to converge to $\nu_E$.
Set $\nu_E=0$ outside $\mathcal E_d$, also for the empty set.

For a similarity $F(x)=sRx+t$, the tube change of variables gives
$m_{FE,\epsilon}=s^d F_*m_{E,\epsilon/s}$. The full-radius cluster
construction therefore gives $C(FE)=s^d F_*C(E)$. Also
