For the raw pure-sign energy only the boundedness equivalence is asserted;
the terminal excursion can cause a uniformly bounded energy error.
By (EX7), condition 1 is equivalently boundedness along some radii of
$\mathbb E\sum_{\sigma^r_k<\tau}(\beta^r_k)^2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For a bounded deterministic-time elementary predictable process
$H=\sum_jH_j\mathbf1_{(t_j,t_{j+1}]}$, use the indices and grid strategy
$L_k$ from the preceding proof, now with the bound $\sup|H|$.
Set $L^{r}(t)=L_k$ on $(\sigma^r_k,\sigma^r_{k+1}]$, and zero beyond
the first exit after $\tau$. The vanishing grid mesh and (EX8) imply
\[
 \mathbb E\int_0^\infty|L^{r}(t)|^2dt
       \longrightarrow\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|H_t|^2dt.
 \tag{EX11}
\]
Indeed on $[0,\tau]$ there is bounded pointwise convergence away from the
finitely many partition endpoints; the expected time of the excess
terminal interval is $O(r^2)$. Predictability gives
\[
 \mathbb E\sum_kL_k^2d^r_k
       =\mathbb E\int_0^\infty|L^{r}(t)|^2dt.
\]
Both $\mathbb E\sum_kL_kb^r_k$ and
$\mathbb E\sum_kL_k\widetilde b^r_k$ tend to
$\ell(H):=\mathbb E\sum_jH_j(X_{t_{j+1}}-X_{t_j})$;
the first uses the $O(nr\sup|H|)$ discretization error, and the second
also uses (EX6). Cauchy--Schwarz, followed by (EX11), yields
\[
 |\ell(H)|\le
  \sqrt{\liminf_{r\downarrow0}\mathcal E_r}\,
        \left(\mathbb E\int_0^\tau H_t^2dt\right)^{1/2},
\]
and the same inequality with $\widetilde{\mathcal E}_r$.
Bounded deterministic-time elementary predictable processes are dense
in the predictable $L^2(\mathbb P\,dt)$ space. Riesz representation
therefore gives a predictable $v$, zero after $\tau$, such that
$\ell(H)=\mathbb E\int_0^\tau H_tv_tdt$, and its squared norm is
bounded by the corresponding liminf energy. Testing a single interval
and every bounded $\mathcal F_s$ variable shows that
$X-\int_0^{\cdot\wedge\tau}v_tdt$ is a martingale. Its integrable
dominating variable is $L+\int_0^\tau|v_t|dt$, so it is uniformly
integrable; continuity gives the continuous martingale decomposition.

Conversely, assume condition 3. Conditional Cauchy--Schwarz on each
excursion, including its possible truncation at $\tau$, gives
\[
 (\widetilde b^r_k)^2\le d^r_k\,
  \mathbb E\left[\int_{\sigma^r_k}^{\sigma^r_{k+1}\wedge\tau}
               v_s^2ds\,\middle|\,G^r_k\right]
      \quad\text{on }\{\sigma^r_k<\tau\}.
\]
Thus $\widetilde{\mathcal E}_r\le\mathbb E\int_0^\tau v_s^2ds$.
The lower norm bound from the Riesz argument gives the reverse liminf,
proving (EX10). Finally, set
$p^r_k=\mathbb P\{\sigma^r_k<\tau<\sigma^r_{k+1}\mid G^r_k\}$.
Then $|b^r_k-\widetilde b^r_k|\le2rp^r_k$ on active starts, and
\[
 \mathbb E\sum_{\sigma^r_k<\tau}
       \frac{|b^r_k-\widetilde b^r_k|^2}{d^r_k}
   \le\frac4{a_1}\mathbb E\sum_kp^r_k\le\frac4{a_1}.
\]
The triangle inequality in the indexed $L^2$ space proves equivalence
of the two bounded-energy conditions. This also shows why replacing
the truncated energy by the raw sign energy need not preserve the
numerical limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Any subquadratic sign energy distinguishes AC drift from singular drift]
\label{cor:exit-subquadratic-energy}
Fix $1<p<2$, and define
\[
 \mathcal E_{r,p}=\mathbb E\sum_{\sigma^r_k<\tau}
                  \frac{|b^r_k|^p}{(d^r_k)^{p-1}}.
 \tag{EX12}
\]
Then $\liminf_{r\downarrow0}\mathcal E_{r,p}<\infty$ is equivalent to
$X=M+\int_0^{\cdot\wedge\tau}v_sds$ with
$\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|v_s|^pds<\infty$. In that case the \emph{raw}
pure-sign energy has the exact limit
\[
 \lim_{r\downarrow0}\mathcal E_{r,p}
       =\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|v_s|^pds.
 \tag{EX13}
\]
By (EX7), boundedness is equivalently boundedness of
$r^{2-p}\mathbb E\sum_{\sigma^r_k<\tau}|\beta^r_k|^p$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Repeat the preceding proof with H\"older conjugate $p'=p/(p-1)$.
The grid strategy satisfies
\[
 \mathbb E\sum_k|L_k|^{p'}d^r_k
       \longrightarrow\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|H_t|^{p'}dt.
\]
Weighted H\"older's inequality and duality between predictable
$L^{p'}$ and $L^p$ therefore identify a predictable density $v$ with
$\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|v_s|^pds$ bounded by the liminf energy.
For the terminal-truncated means, conditional H\"older gives
\[
 |\widetilde b^r_k|^p\le(d^r_k)^{p-1}
  \mathbb E\left[\int_{\sigma^r_k}^{\sigma^r_{k+1}\wedge\tau}
                 |v_s|^pds\,\middle|\,G^r_k\right]
\]
on active starts. Summation supplies the reverse upper bound, so the
truncated-mean energy converges to the asserted $L^p$ norm.

For the full-exit means, the terminal correction obeys
\[
 \mathbb E\sum_{\sigma^r_k<\tau}
  \frac{|b^r_k-\widetilde b^r_k|^p}{(d^r_k)^{p-1}}
 \le \frac{2^p}{a_1^{p-1}}\,r^{2-p}
             \mathbb E\sum_k(p^r_k)^p
 \le \frac{2^p}{a_1^{p-1}}\,r^{2-p}\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{EX14}
\]
The triangle inequality for the corresponding weighted $L^p$ norms
therefore transfers the same numerical limit to the raw sign energy.
The exponent $2-p>0$ is essential for this last assertion. At $p=1$
the limiting object is an arbitrary finite-variation drift measure,
as in Proposition~\ref{prop:exit-budget-exact}; for every $p>1$ in the
stated range, finiteness forces a density with respect to capacity time.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Order-$r$ exit-sign bias supplies bounded drift]
\label{cor:exit-bias-bounded-drift}
If, for all sufficiently small radii and all active starts,
$|\beta^r_k|\le Kr$ with a deterministic constant $K$, then
$X=M+\int_0^{\cdot\wedge\tau}v_sds$ with
$|v_s|\le K/a_1$ almost everywhere.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
By (EX7), $|b^r_k|\le Kr^2\le(K/a_1)d^r_k$.
The predictable test argument therefore bounds
$|\ell(H)|$ by $(K/a_1)\mathbb E\int_0^\tau|H_t|dt$.
The $L^1$--$L^\infty$ dual representation supplies the claimed bounded
predictable density and hence the martingale decomposition.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The classification gap is now quantitatively explicit.}
Unsigned estimates give $|\beta^r_k|\le1$ and order $T/r^2$ exit
terms. The exact finite-variation criterion asks for bounded
$r\mathbb E\sum|\beta^r_k|$. The square-integrable drift criterion
asks for bounded $\mathbb E\sum(\beta^r_k)^2$. The stronger pointwise
bound $|\beta^r_k|\le Kr$ gives the bounded conditional-bias premise
used in Theorem~\ref{thm:cbias-rigidity}. To apply that theorem one must
still control these constants along mirrored shrinking-slit states:
with uniform $a_1$, the required rate is
$\epsilon K_\epsilon\to0$. Pointwise bounded drift in each unrelated
state does not supply that rate.

Even bounded drift alone gives no initial tangent without locality's
additional comparison: $v(t)=\sin(\log t)$ has bounded magnitude but
$t^{-1}\int_0^t v(s)ds=\tfrac12(\sin\log t-\cos\log t)$ has no limit
at zero. Brownian motion plus this deterministic integral is only a
process illustration, not a proposed family obeying the curve axioms.
All these criteria are in a specified coordinate system. Neither finite
raw exit-sign budget nor bounded sign energy is asserted in every affine
structure, and no preferred affine tensor has been constructed.


\section{Source-linear exit energy is sufficient for locality rigidity}
\label{sec:exit-energy-rigidity}

The preceding exit criteria can be linked to classification using a
weaker hypothesis than pointwise bounded driving drift.  The new input
is a bound on \emph{expected initial drift energy per unit capacity},
expressible entirely in exit-sign observables.  It still requires a
quantitative rate along the locality-related shrinking-slit states.
Neither that rate nor the energy bound is derived from RSW here.

Fix $1<p<2$ and one physical conformal structure. Assume the Borel family,
continuous Loewner generation, strong natural-filtration DMP (KR1), and
source locality for the mirrored tests (DR6), with the invertible
capacity-clock and rough-prime-end requirements used previously. Assume
also the uniform upper conditional driving moments and diffusive exit
estimates already obtained from the explicit crossing/locality inputs.
Thus Proposition~\ref{prop:moment-bracket-domination} and the exit-energy
criteria apply once their stated signed hypotheses hold.

For each state $f$, write $\mathcal E^f_{r,p}(\theta)$ for the raw energy
in (EX12), stopped at a bounded stopping time $\theta$ at which the
stopped driver is bounded.  The proofs of
Propositions~\ref{prop:exit-budget-exact}--\ref{prop:exit-energy-ac} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:exit-subquadratic-energy} apply to every such $\theta$:
only boundedness of the stopped driver, the stopping-time property, and
the residual-exit estimate are used. In particular $\theta$ need not
be exactly the first exit from a symmetric driving interval.

\begin{theorem}[Conditional classification from initial exit energy]
\label{thm:exit-energy-rigidity}
Suppose the following additional signed conditions hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For every deterministic state $f$ and $L,T>0$, with
 $\tau_{L,T}=T\wedge\inf\{t:|W_t|\ge L\}$,
 \[
  \liminf_{r\downarrow0}\mathcal E^f_{r,p}(\tau_{L,T})<\infty.
  \tag{ER1}
 \]
\item For each deterministic state $f$, there is a bounded stopping time
 $\rho_f>0$ almost surely, with bounded stopped driver, and a finite
 deterministic constant $K(f)$ such that
 \[
  \limsup_{h\downarrow0}\frac1h
       \liminf_{r\downarrow0}\mathcal E^f_{r,p}(h\wedge\rho_f)
                \le K(f)^p.
  \tag{ER2}
 \]
\item For each state $f$, along some sequence $\epsilon_n\downarrow0$,
 the mirrored deleted states obey
 \[
  \epsilon_n\max_{\sigma=\pm}K(f_{\epsilon_n}^{\sigma})
                    \longrightarrow0.
  \tag{ER3}
 \]
\end{enumerate}
Then every driver is $\sqrt6 B$ in the specified conformal structure,
and the exploration family is chordal SLE$_6$ in that structure.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The exit-energy criterion applied to (ER1), and uniqueness on overlapping
stopped intervals, supply the global continuous canonical decomposition
\[
 W=M+A,\qquad A_t=\int_0^t b_sds,
\]
where $b$ is predictable and locally $L^p(\mathbb P\,dt)$ after bounded
driving localization. The upper conditional moment estimate and
Proposition~\ref{prop:moment-bracket-domination} give
$C=\langle M\rangle=\int_0^t v_sds$ with a locally bounded predictable
density.  The exact energy limit (EX13) turns (ER2) into
\[
 \limsup_{h\downarrow0}\frac1h
   \mathbb E_f\int_0^{h\wedge\rho_f}|b_s|^pds\le K(f)^p.
 \tag{ER4}
\]
No pointwise bound or right limit of $b$ is assumed.

Fix a deterministic state $f$ and one $\epsilon$. Couple its initial
trace to each mirrored deleted-domain trace by locality and use their
invertible capacity clocks. Put $d=d_\epsilon$,
$e=e_\epsilon^+$, and
$K_\epsilon=\max_\sigma K(f_\epsilon^\sigma)$.
The canonical characteristic identity (BI3) reads, before contact,
\[
 p_t^\sigma b_t+\tfrac12q_t^\sigma(v_t-6)
        =(p_t^\sigma)^2\widehat b^\sigma_{u_\sigma(t)},
 \qquad du_\sigma(t)=(p_t^\sigma)^2dt.
 \tag{ER5}
\]
Here $p_0^\sigma=d$ and $q_0^\pm=\pm e$. There is an almost surely
positive stopping time $\lambda_\epsilon$ on which the original and
both deleted initial localizations remain valid, $p_t^\sigma$ lies
between $d/2$ and $2d$, and each $q_t^\sigma$ remains within
$|e|/2$ of its initial value. The two-by-two system in (ER5) is then
uniformly invertible in these relative bounds. With a numerical constant
$C$ independent of $\epsilon$,
\[
 |b_t|\le Cd\sum_{\sigma=\pm}
                 |\widehat b^\sigma_{u_\sigma(t)}|,
 \qquad
 |v_t-6|\le C\frac{d^2}{|e|}\sum_{\sigma=\pm}
                 |\widehat b^\sigma_{u_\sigma(t)}|
 \tag{ER6}
\]
for almost every $t<\lambda_\epsilon$.

Changing variables in the first inequality, using
$u_\sigma(h\wedge\lambda_\epsilon)\le4d^2h$ and stopping before
$\rho_{f_\epsilon^\sigma}$, gives
\[
 \mathbb E\operatorname{Var}(A;[0,h\wedge\lambda_\epsilon])
 \le\frac Cd\sum_{\sigma=\pm}
   \mathbb E\int_0^{4d^2h\wedge\rho_{f_\epsilon^\sigma}}
                     |\widehat b^\sigma_u|du.
\]
H\"older's inequality on probability times capacity and (ER4) imply,
at fixed $\epsilon$,
\[
 \mathbb E\int_0^{4d^2h\wedge\rho_{f_\epsilon^\sigma}}
           |\widehat b^\sigma_u|du
       \le4d^2h\bigl(K_\epsilon+o_\epsilon(1)\bigr),
       \qquad h\downarrow0.
\]
The second inequality in (ER6) is treated identically. Thus
\begin{align}
 \limsup_{h\downarrow0}\frac1h\,
  \mathbb E\operatorname{Var}(A;[0,h\wedge\lambda_\epsilon])
       &\le CdK_\epsilon,\label{eq:energy-drift-slit-bound}\\
 \limsup_{h\downarrow0}\frac1h\,
  \mathbb E\operatorname{Var}(C-6\operatorname{id};
                            [0,h\wedge\lambda_\epsilon])
       &\le C\frac{d^2}{|e|}K_\epsilon.
       \label{eq:energy-bracket-slit-bound}
\end{align}
In applying (ER4), the deleted drift is evaluated in its own canonical
filtration and capacity clock. Its law is the deleted-state law by
locality; the time change in (ER5) is not replaced by a deterministic
capacity equality.

For any $a>0$, Markov's inequality and
$\mathbb P\{\lambda_\epsilon<h\}\to0$ give
\[
 \limsup_{h\downarrow0}\mathbb P\left\{
       h^{-1}\sup_{s\le h}|A_s|>a\right\}
           \le C a^{-1}dK_\epsilon,
\]
and the corresponding bound for $C_s-6s$ with
$dK_\epsilon$ replaced by $d^2K_\epsilon/|e|$.
Since $d_\epsilon\asymp\epsilon$ and
$|e_\epsilon|\asymp\epsilon^{-1}$, condition (ER3), first taking
$h\downarrow0$ at fixed $\epsilon_n$ and then $n\to\infty$, proves
\[
 h^{-1}\sup_{s\le h}|A_s|\longrightarrow0,\qquad
 h^{-1}\sup_{s\le h}|C_s-6s|\longrightarrow0
          \quad\text{in probability}
 \tag{ER7}
\]
in every deterministic state.

It remains to propagate the initial conclusion without assuming a
tangent of the original density. Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-characteristics}
and strong DMP transport the canonical shifted characteristics at each
deterministic time $t$. Along any fixed sequence $h_n\downarrow0$,
for almost every conditioned history their law is the restarted state's
canonical law. Statement (ER7) holds in that state. Conditional
probabilities are bounded by one, so dominated convergence under the
regular conditional law yields
\[
 \frac{A_{t+h_n}-A_t}{h_n}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \frac{C_{t+h_n}-C_t}{h_n}\longrightarrow6
          \quad\text{in probability}.
 \tag{ER8}
\]
On the other hand, absolute continuity and Fubini imply that for
Lebesgue-almost every deterministic $t$, these same quotients converge
almost surely to $b_t$ and $v_t$. Uniqueness of limits in probability
gives $b_t=0$ and $v_t=6$ almost surely for almost every $t$.
Another Fubini argument gives $A=0$ and $C_t=6t$ up to
indistinguishability. L\'evy's characterization identifies $W=\sqrt6 B$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What is sufficient, and what is not proved.}
This theorem replaces pointwise order-$r$ exit-sign bias by averaged
order-$r$ signed energy.  Conditions (ER1)--(ER3) are still missing
theorems for the unknown family in an appropriate common structure.
Finite exit energy alone supplies AC drift and bounded bracket but no
rate in the slit comparison. The drift and variance anomaly rates are
respectively $\epsilon K_\epsilon$ and $\epsilon^3K_\epsilon$.
All estimates are conditional classification tools, not a construction
of the affine normalizer. In arbitrary initial coordinates an adequate
regularity statement may need a correction involving an unknown constant
physical Beltrami coefficient before these signed-energy hypotheses can
be expected to hold.


\subsection{A necessary energy blow-up at characteristic Lebesgue states}
\label{sec:visited-energy-necessity}

The shrinking-slit energy condition is quantitatively adapted to the
actual characteristic anomalies. Mere finiteness of energy in each
state cannot replace it. The following necessity statement applies on
the states visited by a driver with locally $L^p$ drift, without any
initial energy-density assumption at every deterministic state.

Assume the family and locality premises of
Theorem~\ref{thm:exit-energy-rigidity}, and (ER1), with $1<p<2$.
For a deterministic state $g$ and a bounded stopping time $\rho_g>0$
with bounded stopped driver, define the extended nonnegative quantity
\[
 \underline K_p(g;\rho_g)=
 \left(\liminf_{h\downarrow0}\frac1h
       \liminf_{r\downarrow0}
          \mathcal E^g_{r,p}(h\wedge\rho_g)\right)^{1/p}.
 \tag{VN1}
\]
No finiteness of this quantity is assumed.

\begin{proposition}[Anomalies force inverse-scale source energy]
\label{prop:visited-state-energy-necessity}
Fix an initial law $P_f$ with canonical characteristics
$A_t=\int_0^t b_sds$ and $C_t=\int_0^t v_sds$.
For Lebesgue-almost every deterministic time $t$, for almost every
conditioned history at that time, put $g=f_t$, $b=b_t$, and $v=v_t$.
For every fixed admissible mirrored-slit scale $\epsilon$, choose any
positive bounded initial localizations $\rho_\epsilon^\pm$ in the two
deleted states. With $d_\epsilon$ and $e_\epsilon=e_\epsilon^+$ as
in (DR9), one has
\[
 \underline K_p(g_\epsilon^\pm;\rho_\epsilon^\pm)
 \ge\left|\frac b{d_\epsilon}
       \mathbin{\pm}\frac{e_\epsilon}{2d_\epsilon^2}(v-6)\right|.
 \tag{VN2}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \max_{\pm}\underline K_p(g_\epsilon^\pm;\rho_\epsilon^\pm)
 \ge \frac{|b|}{d_\epsilon}
       +\frac{|e_\epsilon|}{2d_\epsilon^2}|v-6|.
 \tag{VN3}
\]
The assertion holds simultaneously for every scale in any prescribed
countable sequence. Thus a nonzero variance anomaly forces at least
order $\epsilon^{-3}$ source-energy roots, while nonzero drift forces
at least order $\epsilon^{-1}$ roots.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Local absolute continuity and pathwise Lebesgue differentiation,
followed by Fubini, give for almost every deterministic $t$, almost
surely,
\[
 \frac1h\int_t^{t+h}|b_s-b_t|ds\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \frac1h\int_t^{t+h}|v_s-v_t|ds\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{VN4}
\]
Condition on the past at such a time and use strong DMP and canonical
restart compatibility. Outside a null set of histories, the restarted
canonical drift and bracket satisfy (VN4) at zero with the frozen
deterministic constants $b_t,v_t$. In particular their variation per
unit time is bounded near zero, and their integrated initial tangents
are respectively $b$ and $v$.

For a fixed $\epsilon$, couple the restarted trace with each deleted
initial trace by locality. The analytic characteristic identity gives
\[
 \widehat A^\pm_{u_\pm(h)}
 =\int_0^h p_s^\pm b_sds
   +\frac12\int_0^h q_s^\pm(v_s-6)ds,
 \qquad
 \frac{u_\pm(h)}h\longrightarrow d_\epsilon^2.
\]
Continuity of $p^\pm,q^\pm$, their initial values
$d_\epsilon,\pm e_\epsilon$, and (VN4) show that the deleted
integrated drift has the deterministic initial tangent
\[
 B_\epsilon^\pm=\frac b{d_\epsilon}
       \mathbin{\pm}\frac{e_\epsilon}{2d_\epsilon^2}(v-6).
 \tag{VN5}
\]
These are initial germ statements; the locality coupling is only
needed until its strictly positive comparison stopping time.

For any positive bounded localization $\rho$ in a deleted law,
H\"older's inequality gives
\[
 \frac1h\,\mathbb E\int_0^{h\wedge\rho}|\widehat b_s|^pds
 \ge\mathbb E\left|
          \frac{\widehat A_{h\wedge\rho}}h\right|^p.
\]
The random variables inside the right side converge in probability to
$|B_\epsilon^\pm|^p$. Fatou's lemma along an almost surely converging
subsequence proves the corresponding liminf lower bound. The exact
raw exit-energy identity (EX13), applied at $h\wedge\rho$, converts
this into (VN2). For real $x,y$,
$\max\{|x+y|,|x-y|\}=|x|+|y|$, proving (VN3). Countable intersections
give the simultaneous sequence claim, and the scales follow from
$d_\epsilon\asymp\epsilon$,
$|e_\epsilon|\asymp\epsilon^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

This is a restriction on possible regularity estimates rather than a
new derivation of signed cancellation. At almost every visited state,
the rate $\epsilon\max_\pm K(g_\epsilon^\pm)\to0$ can hold only if
both characteristic anomalies vanish. Obtaining that rate from the
curve axioms therefore remains the substantive classification step.


\section{Analytic clock families can acquire singular time change}
\label{sec:analytic-clock-obstruction}

The affine-prefix results give analytic dependence of the capacity clock
on a small deformation parameter in the uniform path norm, and very high
temporal regularity of its first parameter derivative. These properties,
even supplemented by strict monotonicity and global capacity comparison,
do not alone prove absolute continuity of a finite-deformation clock.
The following explicit example separates this analytic issue from the
unresolved geometry of the actual affine-SLE clock.

\begin{proposition}[A singular analytic clock family]
\label{prop:singular-analytic-clock-family}
There is a family of continuous strictly increasing clocks
$s_\epsilon:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$, $|\epsilon|<1$, with the following
properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $s_0(t)=t$, $s_\epsilon(t)=t$ outside $[1,2]$, and
$\tfrac12t\le s_\epsilon(t)\le2t$ for every $t\ge0$.
\item On every compact time interval the family extends holomorphically
in $\epsilon$ into the Banach space of continuous paths on the disk
$|\epsilon|<4$. In particular it has uniform Taylor remainders of all
orders on any smaller parameter disk.
\item Every finite parameter derivative at zero is temporally H\"older
of every exponent below one. Its quadratic-increment sums vanish along
every sequence of partitions of vanishing mesh. Its mixed sums with any
path H\"older of some positive exponent also vanish, after choosing an
appropriate exponent for the derivative path.
\item For every real $0<|\epsilon|<1$, $ds_\epsilon$ has a nonzero
singular continuous component relative to $dt$. The first parameter
derivative $\partial_\epsilon s_\epsilon|_0$ is not of bounded variation.
\end{enumerate}
This example has no asserted realization by Loewner hulls.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Work first on the circle $[0,2\pi]$. For real $|\epsilon|<1$, set
\[
 p_{\epsilon,N}(x)=\prod_{n=1}^N
                  (1+\epsilon\cos(5^n x)),\qquad
 \mu_{\epsilon,N}(dx)=p_{\epsilon,N}(x)\frac{dx}{2\pi}.
\]
Every factor is positive. In the Fourier expansion, frequencies are
$k=\sum_{n=1}^N\sigma_n5^n$, $\sigma_n\in\{-1,0,1\}$, with coefficient
$(\epsilon/2)^{\#\{n:\sigma_n\ne0\}}$. Such representations are unique:
the largest nonzero digit in a difference of two representations has
magnitude at least $5^m$, whereas all lower difference digits have total
magnitude at most $2\sum_{n<m}5^n<5^m$. In particular the constant
Fourier coefficient is one and these are probability measures.
Their Fourier coefficients stabilize at every fixed integer frequency,
so compactness and uniqueness of Fourier coefficients give a unique
weak limit $\mu_\epsilon$.

Let
\[
 C_{\epsilon,N}(x)=\int_0^x p_{\epsilon,N}(u)\,du.
\]
The nonconstant term with highest digit $m$ has frequency of magnitude
at least $5^m-\sum_{n<m}5^n\ge(3/4)5^m$. Summing the absolute
coefficients of all terms with this highest digit gives
$|\epsilon|(1+|\epsilon|)^{m-1}$. Since
$|\int_0^x e^{iku}du|\le2/|k|$, the largest-digit block contributes
at most
\[
 C5^{-m}|\epsilon|(1+|\epsilon|)^{m-1}
 \tag{SC1}
\]
in uniform norm. Therefore $C_{\epsilon,N}$ converges uniformly in $x$
and locally uniformly for complex $|\epsilon|<4$ to a holomorphic
$C([0,2\pi])$-valued function $C_\epsilon$. For real $|\epsilon|<1$
it is increasing, $C_\epsilon(0)=0$, and $C_\epsilon(2\pi)=2\pi$.
The uniform convergence also shows that the weak measure limit has no
atoms and $dC_\epsilon=2\pi\,d\mu_\epsilon$.

Now define
\[
 s_\epsilon(t)=
 \begin{cases}
 t,&t\notin[1,2],\\
 1+\dfrac{x+C_\epsilon(x)}{4\pi},
       &t=1+x/(2\pi),\quad0\le x\le2\pi.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{SC2}
\]
On the middle interval its increment measure has one-half Lebesgue
part, so the clock is strictly increasing; all joins are continuous.
It maps $[1,2]$ onto $[1,2]$, giving the stated global comparisons.
Analyticity and uniform Taylor bounds follow from (SC1).

The first derivative is zero outside $[1,2]$ and inside equals
\[
 \left.\partial_\epsilon s_\epsilon(t)\right|_0
    =\frac1{4\pi}\sum_{n\ge1}5^{-n}\sin(5^nx).
 \tag{SC3}
\]
For a temporal separation $h$ its increment has magnitude at most
$C|h|(1+|\log|h||)$: split the sum at $5^n|h|=1$ and use
$|\sin(a+b)-\sin a|\le\min\{|b|,2\}$. More generally, in the
coefficient of $\epsilon^j$ the sum of absolute Fourier coefficients
in largest-digit block $m$ is
$\binom{m-1}{j-1}$, up to constants depending only on $j$.
The same split gives the modulus
\[
 C_j|h|(1+|\log|h||)^j.
 \tag{SC4}
\]
This holds across the joins as well, since all derivative paths vanish
at both endpoints. Thus each derivative is H\"older-$\alpha$ for every
$\alpha<1$. An exponent $\alpha>1/2$ gives vanishing quadratic sums.
For mixed sums with a H\"older-$\beta$ path, choose
$\alpha<1$ with $\alpha+\beta>1$ and bound their absolute sum by
$C T\operatorname{mesh}(\pi)^{\alpha+\beta-1}$.

We prove singularity directly. Under $\mu_\epsilon$,
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mu_\epsilon}\cos(5^n x)=\epsilon/2,
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_{\mu_\epsilon}[\cos(5^n x)\cos(5^m x)]
                  =\epsilon^2/4\quad(n\ne m).
\]
These identities are the Fourier coefficients at $5^n$ and
$5^n\pm5^m$. Hence the centered variables are pairwise orthogonal
and bounded. Their Cesaro average converges in $L^2(\mu_\epsilon)$
to $\epsilon/2$, with squared error at most $C/N$. At $N=k^2$,
Chebyshev and Borel--Cantelli give almost sure convergence; boundedness
interpolates between consecutive squares. Under normalized Lebesgue
measure the same argument gives almost sure limit zero. For
$\epsilon\ne0$ the two full-measure sets are disjoint, proving
$\mu_\epsilon\perp dx$. Formula (SC2) therefore has a nonzero singular
continuous increment component in $[1,2]$.

Finally put $F(x)=\sum_{n\ge1}5^{-n}\sin(5^nx)$. If $F$ were BV,
its periodic distributional derivative would be a finite signed measure
$\lambda$ with distributional Fourier series $\sum_{n\ge1}\cos(5^nx)$.
Convolution with the normalized Fejer kernel of degree $M=2\cdot5^N$
would have $L^1$ norm at most $\|\lambda\|_{\rm TV}$ and would equal
\[
 P_N(x)=\sum_{n=1}^N
        \left(1-\frac{5^n}{M+1}\right)\cos(5^nx).
\]
All weights are between $1/2$ and one. Orthogonality gives
$\|P_N\|_2\ge c\sqrt N$. The fourth moment is at most $CN^2$:
in a zero signed sum of four powers of five the largest exponent must
cancel with an opposite copy, since three lower powers have magnitude
less than the largest. Only pairings remain. Therefore
$\|P_N\|_4\le C\sqrt N$. Interpolation gives
$\|P_N\|_1\ge\|P_N\|_2^3/\|P_N\|_4^2\ge c\sqrt N$, a contradiction.
This proves the claimed failure of BV.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Consequence for the research strategy.}
Even an all-order affine-parameter Taylor theory whose clock derivatives
have every temporal exponent below one does not, by itself, settle
absolute continuity of the finite affine capacity clock. One needs an
additional estimate that controls the increment measures, or new
information from the actual Loewner/SLE geometry. The example does not
decide the affine-SLE clock question, and is not a counterexample to the
exploration classification conjecture.

\subsection{An exact increment-measure target for clock regularity}

There is a positive measure-theoretic formulation of the missing estimate.
It concerns a martingale in the dyadic \emph{time variable}; it makes no
semimartingale assumption about a Loewner driver.

\begin{proposition}[A dyadic clock criterion]
\label{prop:dyadic-clock-entropy}
Let $s$ be a continuous increasing function on $[0,T]$, with $s(0)=0$
and $s(T)=m>0$. For dyadic cells $I$ at level $n$, put
\[
 a_n(t)=\frac{s(\sup I)-s(\inf I)}{|I|},\quad t\in I.
\]
Then $ds\ll dt$ if and only if $(a_n)$ is uniformly integrable
relative to Lebesgue measure on $[0,T]$. A sufficient condition is
\[
 \sup_n H_n<\infty,\qquad
 H_n=\int_0^T a_n\log\frac{a_n}{m/T}\,dt.
 \tag{SC5}
\]
Write $m_I=s(\sup I)-s(\inf I)$ and $I_-,I_+$ for the children of $I$.
Condition (SC5) is equivalent, up to absolute multiplicative constants,
to finiteness of
\[
 \sum_{I\text{ dyadic}}
       \frac{(m_{I_-}-m_{I_+})^2}{m_I},
 \tag{SC6}
\]
where a zero-mass summand is defined to be zero. If the clock is random,
a uniform bound on $\mathbb E H_n$ is sufficient for almost sure
absolute continuity.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The $a_n$ form a nonnegative martingale on the probability space
$([0,T],dt/T)$ with the increasing dyadic sigma-fields. They converge
almost everywhere to the density of the absolutely continuous part of
$ds$, by differentiation of measures. Uniform integrability is
equivalent to preservation of their common integral $m$ at the limit,
which is equivalent to absence of singular mass. If $ds=a\,dt$,
then $a_n$ are the dyadic conditional averages of the integrable
function $a$ and hence are uniformly integrable.

The entropy condition gives uniform integrability by the elementary
$x\log x$ tail bound. Jensen's inequality makes $H_n$ nonnegative
and increasing. At a split of a positive-mass cell, put
$p=m_{I_-}/m_I$. The entropy increment is exactly
\[
 m_I\{p\log(2p)+(1-p)\log(2(1-p))\}.
\]
For $\delta=2p-1$, its bracket equals
$\sum_{k\ge1}\delta^{2k}/[2k(2k-1)]$, and is bounded between
$\delta^2/2$ and $(\log2)\delta^2$. Summing the increments proves
the equivalence with (SC6). Finally a uniform expected entropy bound
and monotone convergence give finite limiting entropy almost surely,
which implies the pathwise criterion.
\end{proof}

This supplies a concrete new model task: control the dyadic mass-split
energy of the actual affine-SLE capacity clock. Uniform integrability,
rather than merely temporal H\"older regularity of clock tangents,
is exactly what is needed for clock absolute continuity. The entropy
bound is sufficient, not necessary; no such bound is proved here from
the exploration axioms or for affine SLE. Clock AC would remain only a
necessary condition in the existing wrong-driver semimartingale test.


\section{Selecting an unknown affine tensor through finite signed tests}
\label{sec:finite-affine-energy-selection}

One need not identify the affine tensor before formulating a useful
signed regularity estimate. It can be retained as a compact parameter
and selected jointly with the estimates. The following conditional
principle makes this precise without extending from a dense collection
of domains or assuming a domain Feller property.

Fix $1<p<\infty$ and $q=p/(p-1)$. On $C([0,T],\mathbb R)$ let
$W_t(w)=w(t)$, and suppose all coordinate variables are integrable
under the law $P$. Let $\mathcal H_T$ consist of processes
\[
 H_t=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}h_i(W|_{[0,t_i]})
                       \mathbf1_{(t_i,t_{i+1}]}(t),
 \qquad 0=t_0<\cdots<t_n=T,
\]
where every $h_i$ is bounded and continuous on its past-path space.
Put
\[
 \mathcal J_{p,T}(P)=\sup_{H\in\mathcal H_T}
 \left\{p\,\mathbb E_P\sum_i h_i(W)(W_{t_{i+1}}-W_{t_i})
       -(p-1)\mathbb E_P\int_0^T|H_t|^qdt\right\}.
 \tag{FS1}
\]

\begin{proposition}[A signed-test characterization of $L^p$ drift]
\label{prop:signed-test-lp-drift}
The functional in (FS1) is finite if and only if, in the completed
right-continuous natural filtration, there is a predictable
$b\in L^p(P\otimes dt)$ such that
\[
 W_t=W_0+M_t+\int_0^t b_sds
 \tag{FS2}
\]
with $M$ a continuous martingale starting at zero. In that case
\[
 \mathcal J_{p,T}(P)=\mathbb E_P\int_0^T|b_s|^pds.
 \tag{FS3}
\]
The drift is unique up to $P\otimes dt$-null sets.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write $L(H)=\mathbb E\sum_i h_i\Delta W_i$ and
$\|H\|_q=(\mathbb E\int|H|^qdt)^{1/q}$. Scaling $H$ by any real
constant in (FS1), and optimizing that scalar, shows
\[
 |L(H)|\le\mathcal J_{p,T}(P)^{1/p}\|H\|_q.
 \tag{FS4}
\]
In particular $L$ respects equivalence modulo $P\otimes dt$.
Continuous bounded past-path functions are dense in every finite
$L^q$ space of past-measurable variables, since path space is Polish.
Consequently $\mathcal H_T$ is dense in the predictable $L^q$ space.
For the usual augmentation one may first approximate any augmented
past variable by raw past variables at times $t+\delta$, then delay
the start of its elementary interval by $\delta$; the lost interval
has vanishing $P\otimes dt$ norm. This gives the same density result.

Extend $L$ continuously and apply $L^q$ duality on the raw predictable
sigma-field (completed for $P\otimes dt$) to obtain a predictable
$b\in L^p$, choosing a raw predictable version, with
$L(H)=\mathbb E\int Hb\,dt$ and
$\|b\|_p\le\mathcal J_{p,T}(P)^{1/p}$.
Testing one interval $(s,t]$ against a bounded continuous functional of
$W|_{[0,s]}$ gives zero conditional mean for increments of
$W-\int bdt$. The identity extends to every bounded past-measurable
variable: it is equality of finite signed measures on the Polish
past-path space, tested against all bounded continuous functions.
Thus the continuous integrable process $M$ in (FS2) is a martingale
in the raw natural filtration. It extends to the usual augmentation:
it is closed by its integrable terminal value, and right limits of
conditional expectations agree with its continuous paths.

Conversely, under (FS2), martingale increments contribute zero, so
$L(H)=\mathbb E\int Hb\,dt$. Pointwise convex duality gives
\[
 \sup_{h\in\mathbb R}\{phb-(p-1)|h|^q\}=|b|^p.
\]
Approximate the optimizing predictable variable
$\operatorname{sgn}(b)|b|^{p-1}$ in $L^q$ by members of
$\mathcal H_T$ to obtain equality (FS3). Uniqueness follows from
uniqueness of the continuous semimartingale decomposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Lower semicontinuity and common-tensor selection]
\label{prop:finite-affine-selection}
Let $\mathcal K$ be a compact parameter space of normalized affine
tensors. For each state $f$ in an arbitrary index set and each
horizon $T$, suppose $A\mapsto P_{f,T}^A$ is weakly continuous on
$C([0,T],\mathbb R)$, and, for each deterministic time $t$, the
family of random variables $W_t$ is uniformly integrable over these
laws. Then $A\mapsto\mathcal J_{p,T}(P_{f,T}^A)$ is lower
semicontinuous.

Fix a deterministic finite envelope $B_{f,T}$ for every desired
state/horizon pair. If every finite collection of elementary signed
tests, from any finite collection of state/horizon pairs, admits
one common $A\in\mathcal K$ for which all the individual test
expressions in (FS1) are at most their prescribed $B_{f,T}$, then
there exists one $A_*\in\mathcal K$ such that
\[
 \mathcal J_{p,T}(P_{f,T}^{A_*})\le B_{f,T}
 \quad\hbox{for every desired }(f,T).
 \tag{FS5}
\]
In particular all these drivers simultaneously have absolutely
continuous $L^p$ drift with the corresponding envelopes.
The same conclusion holds if finite collections can only be satisfied
up to an arbitrary common error $\eta>0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For fixed $H$, its penalty expectation is a bounded continuous
function of the law. Each increment expectation is continuous as
well: its integrand is continuous, and its absolute value is at
most a fixed multiple of $|W_{t_i}|+|W_{t_{i+1}}|$; uniform
integrability allows truncation. Therefore each elementary test
expression is continuous in $A$. Their supremum is lower
semicontinuous.

The constraint that one test expression is at most $B_{f,T}$ is
closed in $\mathcal K$. The simultaneous finite-test hypothesis is
exactly the finite-intersection property for these closed sets.
Compactness gives a point in their entire intersection, and taking
the supremum over tests gives (FS5). In the approximate version,
compactness and continuity first let $\eta\downarrow0$ for each fixed
finite collection, giving the exact finite-intersection property.
No countability of the state
index set and no dense-state extension are used.
\end{proof}

One possible compact tensor class is
$\{Q=Q^{\mathsf T}>0:\det Q=1,\ \lambda^{-1}I\le Q\le\lambda I\}$.
The common ellipticity bound $\lambda$ must be proved or assumed.
The finite envelopes must also be specified in advance; the claim
that each finite test collection has some finite but uncontrolled
budget is not enough for the compactness conclusion.

\subsection{Physical affine-coordinate continuity for a fixed state}

The law-continuity premise above can be proved pathwise for changes
of affine coordinates. The transported coefficient is essential.
This is not a continuity assertion as the physical state itself varies.

\begin{proposition}[Uniform driver continuity under asymptotically conformal changes]
\label{prop:qc-coordinate-driver-continuity}
Let $K_t$, $0\le t\le T_0$, be a continuously driven half-plane
Loewner chain with driver $W_t$ and $K_t\subset B_R$. Let
$F_n:\mathbb H\to\mathbb H$ be quasiconformal homeomorphisms fixing
$0,1,\infty$, with maximal dilatations tending to one. Suppose the
image hull chains admit their continuous capacity parametrizations.
Write
\[
 s_n(t)=\tfrac12\operatorname{hcap}(F_nK_t),\qquad
 U_n(t)=\widehat g^{\,n}_{s_n(t)}(F_n\gamma(t)),
\]
where tips are interpreted as prime ends. Then
\[
 \sup_{t\le T_0}|s_n(t)-t|\longrightarrow0,\qquad
 \sup_{t\le T_0}|U_n(t)-W_t|\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{FS6}
\]
Consequently their own-capacity drivers converge uniformly to $W$
on every $[0,T]$ with $T<T_0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Reflect $F_n$ across the real line. Normalized quasiconformal
compactness gives $F_n\to\operatorname{id}$ uniformly in spherical
distance, and hence uniformly on compact subsets of the plane.
The image hulls all lie in a fixed disk $B_{R'}$. At each fixed $t$,
their complements converge in the Caratheodory kernel sense to
$\mathbb H\setminus K_t$: any compact subset persists by uniform
convergence of $F_n^{-1}$, and any purported larger kernel
neighborhood would contain points $F_n(z_n)\in F_nK_t$ converging
to a point of $K_t$. Hydrodynamic maps converge locally uniformly.
Since all hulls are uniformly bounded, the maps reflect conformally
across the real line outside a fixed disk; convergence on a sufficiently
large fixed circle gives convergence of their $1/z$ coefficients,
hence $s_n(t)\to t$. Monotonicity in $t$ and a finite-grid sandwich
against the continuous limit give uniform clock convergence.

Set $f_t=g_t^{-1}$ and consider
\[
 Q_{n,t}=\widehat g^{\,n}_{s_n(t)}\circ F_n\circ f_t.
\]
This is a quasiconformal self-homeomorphism of the half-plane,
fixing infinity, with dilatation tending to one uniformly in $t$.
Uniform hydrodynamic bounds for hulls in a fixed disk give, for
every sufficiently large fixed $Y$,
\[
 \sup_{t\le T_0}|Q_{n,t}(iY)-iY|
       \le CR^2/Y+o_n(1),
 \tag{FS7}
\]
enlarging $R$ by a fixed factor if necessary. Indeed
$f_t(iY)=iY+O(R^2/Y)$ uniformly in $t$, $F_n$ converges uniformly
on the compact set of these values, and the transformed
hydrodynamic map changes its argument by $O(R^2/Y)$.

For any sequence $t_n$, the images of the three distinct sphere
points $\infty,iY,2iY$, with one sufficiently large fixed $Y$,
remain respectively infinity and in separated compact neighborhoods
of $iY,2iY$. Normalized quasiconformal compactness therefore keeps
the reflected $Q_{n,t_n}$ away from degenerate limits. Any subsequential normalized
quasiconformal limit is therefore a conformal sphere
homeomorphism fixing infinity and preserving $\mathbb H$, hence
$z\mapsto az+b$ with $a>0$ and $b\in\mathbb R$. The estimate
(FS7), for arbitrarily large fixed $Y$, forces $a=1,b=0$.
Thus $Q_{n,t}\to\operatorname{id}$ uniformly on bounded closed
half-plane sets, uniformly in $t$. Since $W_t$ remains bounded,
$U_n(t)=Q_{n,t}(W_t)$ proves the driver assertion in (FS6).
Uniform inverse-clock convergence on $[0,T]$, followed by uniform
continuity of $W$, proves the final statement.
\end{proof}

For a fixed physical state $f_0:\mathbb H\to D_0$, choose one
third marked prime end $f_0(1)$ to normalize its chordal charts.
If physical affine maps $A_n\to\operatorname{id}$, the comparison
$F_n=\varphi_n\circ A_n\circ f_0$, where
$\varphi_n:A_nD_0\to\mathbb H$ sends the three corresponding
ends to $0,1,\infty$, has coefficient
\[
 \mu_{F_n}(z)=\mu_{A_n}
             \frac{\overline{f_0'(z)}}{f_0'(z)}.
 \tag{FS8}
\]
Its norm tends to zero, so the preceding proposition applies.
At a general base affine map $A_0$, use the reference domain
$A_0D$ and the relative maps $A_nA_0^{-1}$. For each deterministic
convergent sequence of affine maps, this gives samplewise convergence
of normalized driving paths on horizons strictly inside the available
capacity lifetime, on the common probability-one event of admissibility
for that countable sequence. Bounded continuous functionals then give
weak law continuity by dominated convergence. Admissibility almost
surely for each individual affine map suffices for this argument;
a universal event for an uncountable tensor class is not asserted.
No domain Feller assertion is used. Uniform integrability
of increments remains a separate requirement, for example from
uniform crossing tails over a compact ellipticity class.

\begin{corollary}[The selection continuity assumptions under uniform crossings]
\label{cor:affine-selection-continuity-crossings}
Suppose a fixed physical-state law has the uniform avoidable-quad
crossing upper bound used earlier, and its curves are continuously
Loewner admissible after every affine map in a compact normalized
ellipticity class $\mathcal K$, with infinite capacity lifetime in each such chart.
Normalize each initial chart by three
corresponding prime ends. Then the associated own-capacity driver
laws are weakly continuous in $A\in\mathcal K$ and have uniformly
integrable coordinate increments on each finite capacity horizon.
Thus the lower semicontinuity premise of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-affine-selection} holds for that state.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Weak continuity is the preceding deterministic argument, coupled
through the fixed physical curve sample. If all affine maps have
quasiconformal dilatation at most $K_*$, modulus distortion shows
that a transformed quad of modulus at least $K_*M$ pulls back to
one of modulus at least $M$. Avoidability and the crossing event
are topological, so the same probability upper bound survives
uniformly over the class, with the enlarged modulus threshold.
The established driving upper-tail theorem consequently supplies
uniform exponential tails for the driving supremum divided by
$\sqrt T$, with constants depending only on the original crossing
constants and $K_*$. This gives uniform integrability, indeed all
finite moments, for the finitely many coordinate increments in
each elementary test.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Status and role.}
These propositions are compactness and stochastic duality tools.
They do not prove the simultaneous finite-test bound or a common
ellipticity class from the exploration axioms. They show that a regularity
theorem may select its affine tensor as part of its conclusion.
It is unnecessary to know the tensor first, but it remains
necessary to prove an estimate uniform over finite collections
of tests. Mere $L^p$ drift, without the additional locality/slit
control in the rigidity theorems, is not claimed to finish
classification.


\section{Strong restart excludes a shared smooth random coordinate medium}
\label{sec:random-smooth-medium-rigidity}

A natural possible counterexample is to sample a translation-invariant
smooth random geometry and run SLE$_6$ in that geometry.  The following
argument excludes this class. In fact the prior on the coordinate map
need not be stationary: translation covariance of the resulting family
is enough. This result does not assume an analogous representation for
the unknown exploration family.

\begin{lemma}[The literal time-zero restart makes the initial germ trivial]
\label{lem:dmp-zero-germ}
Let $P_f$ be one law in the family, with deterministic initial state $f$,
and suppose strong DMP (KR1) holds at $\tau=0$ in its usual
right-continuous natural driver filtration, with $f_0=f$. Then
$\mathcal F^f_0$ is $P_f$-trivial. In particular every Borel ordered
initial-trace-germ observable is almost surely constant.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For each bounded Borel functional $F$ of the entire future driver,
the time-zero restart identity says
\[
 \mathbb E_f[F(W)\mid\mathcal F^f_0]=\mathbb E_fF(W).
 \tag{RM1}
\]
The usual initial sigma-field is contained in the completed sigma-field
of the full driver. Apply (RM1) to a full-driver version of any event in
$\mathcal F^f_0$: its indicator equals its probability almost surely.

For completeness an ordered initial-trace-germ statistic belongs to
$\mathcal F^f_0$. It is measurable from the prefix stopped on every
source circle of radius $r$. The corresponding capacity stopping times
decrease to zero almost surely as $r\downarrow0$, by continuity and
strict initial Loewner growth. For any fixed $t>0$, the statistic is
therefore $\mathcal F^f_t$-measurable, by taking the union of events on
which those stopping times are at most $t$. Right continuity gives its
measurability in $\mathcal F^f_0$.
\end{proof}

This is a deduction from the \emph{literal} time-zero strong restart
convention. It is not justified merely by DMP at positive times in an
unaugmented filtration. The independent-color germ theorem proved
earlier remains useful without assuming a limiting strong DMP theorem.

\begin{lemma}[Endpoint tensor decoding only needs a first-order error]
\label{lem:endpoint-decoder-first-order}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ed-linearization} remains valid with its quadratic error
replaced by
\[
 \Phi(z)=Lz+o(|z|),\qquad z\to0
 \tag{RM2}
\]
uniformly on its closed half-neighborhood. Consequently the same
universal physical endpoint decoder recovers
$[Dh(p)^{\mathsf T}Dh(p)]$ from a sample with law
\[
 (h^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(hD;hp,hq)
 \tag{RM3}
\]
whenever $D$ is a bounded smooth Jordan domain and $h$ is an
orientation-preserving $C^{1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ diffeomorphism
of the plane, $0<\alpha<1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
In the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ed-linearization}, the scaled error on a
fixed bounded preimage disk is now at most $\omega(Cr_n)$, for a
deterministic modulus $\omega(s)\to0$, in place of $Cr_n$. For every
fixed radial tolerance $\delta>0$, it is eventually smaller than
$\delta$. The same stopping sandwich therefore bounds the observable
difference by $\omega(Cr_n)+h_\delta(Y_n)$. Ces\`aro averaging removes
the first term; ergodicity bounds the second by
$\mathbb E h_\delta(Y)$; let $\delta\downarrow0$ as in the original
proof. No rate of the first-order error is needed.

To apply this to (RM3), choose a conformal map
$\phi:\mathbb H\to hD$ sending $0,\infty$ to $hp,hq$.
The image boundary is $C^{1,\alpha}$ and hence Dini-smooth. The
classical boundary differentiability theorem gives
\[
 \phi(z)=hp+c z+o(|z|),\qquad c\ne0,
\]
for unrestricted approach in the closed half-plane; see
\cite[Theorem 1]{warschawski-boundary-1961}. Composition with the
differentiable map $h^{-1}$ gives a first-order local embedding into $D$.
After rotating the known physical tangent half-plane to $\mathbb H$ by
$Q$, its derivative is
$L=QDh(p)^{-1}M_c$, where $M_c$ is complex multiplication by $c$.
The endpoint decoder recovers
\[
 [(L^{-1})^{\mathsf T}L^{-1}]
       =[QDh(p)^{\mathsf T}Dh(p)Q^{\mathsf T}],
\]
so conjugating back by $Q$ gives the asserted tensor in physical axes.
The same Borel decoder is used for every map $h$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A common smooth random-coordinate prior collapses to one affine metric]
\label{thm:random-smooth-chart-rigidity}
Let $\Pi$ be a probability measure on orientation-preserving global
$C^{1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ diffeomorphisms of $\mathbb R^2$, and
consider the family
\[
 P_{D,p,q}=\int
 (h^{-1})_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(hD;hp,hq)\,\Pi(dh).
 \tag{RM4}
\]
The \emph{same prior $\Pi$} is used for every deterministic marked
domain. Suppose the family is Borel, has the standing continuous
Loewner admissibility, is physically translation covariant, and obeys
the time-zero strong restart identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:dmp-zero-germ}.
It suffices to impose these assumptions for translations of one smooth
bounded Jordan domain with a regular marked source.

Then there is one deterministic positive-definite projective tensor
$T_*$ such that, for $\Pi$-almost every $h$,
\[
 h(x)=a_h A x+b_h,
 \tag{RM5}
\]
where $A=T_*^{1/2}$ after choosing determinant-one normalization,
$a_h\ne0$ acts by complex multiplication, and $b_h$ is a translation.
Consequently (RM4) is the pure fixed-affine SLE$_6$ family with metric
$T_*$; the random similarities $a_h,b_h$ disappear from its law.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take a reference disk $D_0$ whose source is $0$, and translate it to
$D_p=D_0+p$ with translated target. For a fixed $p$, condition first
on $h$. The endpoint decoder of the preceding lemma recovers
\[
 T_h(p)=[Dh(p)^{\mathsf T}Dh(p)]
 \tag{RM6}
\]
almost surely under the conditional curve law. Integrating over the
common prior shows that the decoder under $P_{D_p,p,q_p}$ has exactly
the $\Pi$-distribution of $T_h(p)$. It is an initial-germ statistic,
so Lemma~\ref{lem:dmp-zero-germ} makes this distribution a point mass,
say at $t(p)$.

The physical tangent frames of the translated disks are translates of
the same frame. Physical translation covariance and the universal
decoder therefore give $t(p)=t(0)=T_*$ for every deterministic $p$.
For all rational-coordinate points simultaneously, (RM6) equals $T_*$
outside one $\Pi$-null set. Continuity of $Dh$ extends this equality
to all $x\in\mathbb R^2$. Thus
\[
 Dh(x)^{\mathsf T}Dh(x)=c_h(x)T_*,\qquad c_h(x)>0.
 \tag{RM7}
\]
With $A=T_*^{1/2}$, the derivative of $k_h(y)=h(A^{-1}y)$ is an
orientation-preserving Euclidean similarity at every point. Its $C^1$
coordinate functions satisfy the Cauchy--Riemann equations, so $k_h$ is
an entire conformal map. It is a global homeomorphism and hence proper;
an entire proper injective map is a polynomial of degree one. This gives
(RM5). SLE similarity covariance now makes every conditional law in
(RM4) equal to $A^{-1}_*\operatorname{SLE}_6(AD;Ap,Aq)$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Scope of this obstruction.}
No RSW estimate, independent-color coupling, or stationarity assumption
on the random chart is used. The theorem rules out a smooth random
coordinate medium sampled from one common prior as a source of additional
families. It does not establish a smooth-coordinate representation for
the unknown family. It leaves open rough coordinate media and rules
using a different prior for each source/domain, including possible
source-germ assignments whose restart consistency remains unresolved.
Full natural-filtration strong DMP at zero is essential to this argument.



\section{Why a short analytic deletion does not amplify unsigned information}
\label{sec:unsigned-jet-horizon}

The finite-mesh locality identities do not by themselves improve the
unsigned moment bounds to a signed estimate. There is a general geometric
scale obstruction behind the earlier particular shrinking-slit example.

\begin{lemma}[A source collar bounds its deletion jet]
\label{lem:deletion-jet-collar}
Suppose a deletion map $h$ extends conformally and injectively to the
disk $B(0,\rho)$, and put $p=h'(0)>0$, $q=h''(0)$. Then
\[
 \frac{|q|}{p}\le\frac4\rho.
 \tag{JH1}
\]
For a hydrodynamic half-plane deletion whose removed hull misses
$B(0,\rho)$, Schwarz reflection gives the required extension and
univalence across the source interval.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Normalize $F(z)=(h(\rho z)-h(0))/(\rho h'(0))$. It is univalent in the
unit disk, $F(0)=0$, $F'(0)=1$, and its second coefficient is
$a_2=\rho q/(2p)$. The classical bound $|a_2|\le2$ gives (JH1).
It follows directly from the area theorem: the odd univalent map
$G(z)=\sqrt{F(z^2)}=z+(a_2/2)z^3+\cdots$ has exterior reciprocal
$1/G(1/\zeta)=\zeta-(a_2/2)\zeta^{-1}+\cdots$, whose first negative
coefficient has modulus at most one.

For the last assertion the reflected upper- and lower-half disk images
lie in opposite half-planes, while the real source interval is mapped
strictly increasingly. Injectivity therefore persists across that
interval.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A bounded variance anomaly is below Brownian size on a perturbative collar]
\label{prop:unsigned-locality-jet-horizon}
In a second-order matched-prefix deletion comparison, consider a bounded
putative variance-six defect $\delta$ over an original capacity interval
of length $s$. Its initial-jet contribution to the transformed mean is
$\tfrac12q\delta s$. Relative to the Brownian-order transformed driving
scale $p\sqrt s$, one has
\[
 \frac{|q\delta|s/2}{p\sqrt s}
       \le2|\delta|\frac{\sqrt s}{\rho}.
 \tag{JH2}
\]
Thus if the comparison is restricted to the perturbative regime
$s/\rho^2\to0$, this contribution is $o(p\sqrt s)$ even when
$\delta\ne0$. A large boundary second derivative cannot remove this
scale obstruction by itself: its ratio to the first derivative is
limited by the source collar radius.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Substitute (JH1). The scale $p\sqrt s$ is the correct unsigned comparison
scale: on a relative derivative collar, the deleted capacity time is
bounded by a constant times $p^2s$, and the upper driving estimate bounds
its endpoint by the supremum up to that deterministic time. The exact
endpoint is still at an inherited random stopping time; it is not
identified with a deterministic deleted-capacity increment.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What this rules out as an argument.}
One cannot force a variance defect to vanish merely by making the initial
second derivative of an analytic deletion large and bounding both mean
increments by their unsigned Brownian scales. During the short interval
on which that initial jet is perturbatively valid, the anomalous signed
term fits inside those bounds. At times of order $\rho^2$, the initial
jet approximation no longer has a vanishing relative error without
additional analysis. This is a limitation of this particular use of the
locality identity, not a counterexample to the curve axioms or an
impossibility result for multiscale signed comparisons. A successful
argument needs cancellation or compatibility information beyond separate
unsigned size estimates.


% Reconstructed checked result after execution interruption, 29 October 2026.
% Scratch fragment only; the parent integrates the common repository sources.
\section{Signed calibration energies force affine compactness}
\label{sec:affine-tensor-coercivity}

Compactness of the unknown affine structure can follow from signed
calibration bounds, without an affine-SLE representation. Two remote
analytic deletions control both driving characteristics. Two physical
orientations then exclude degenerating anisotropy. The bounds used are
four full signed energies in candidate-dependent auxiliary charts;
finite collections of scalar tests do not supply them automatically.

\subsection{Two opposite deletions control both characteristics}

Fix $A^\pm=\{\pm10+iy:0\le y\le1\}$ in $\mathbb H$. There is a
deterministic $h_0>0$ such that every continuous driver $W_0=0$,
stopped at
\[
 \theta=h_0\wedge\inf\{t:|W_t|\ge1\},
 \tag{TC1}
\]
has evolving deletion maps $h_t^\pm$ with uniformly bounded analytic
jets near the driving interval and
\[
 0<m\le p_t^\pm=(h_t^\pm)'(W_t)\le P,\qquad
 |p_t^+q_t^- -p_t^-q_t^+|\ge d_0>0,
 \quad q_t^\pm=(h_t^\pm)''(W_t).
 \tag{TC2}
\]
All constants are deterministic and independent of the driver. At
time zero the hydrodynamic maps are
$h_0^\pm(z)=\pm10+\sqrt{(z\mp10)^2+1}$, with the square-root branch
asymptotic to $z\mp10$. On $[-1,1]$ their first derivatives are
positive, whereas $q_0^+<0<q_0^-$. The determinant is therefore
bounded away from zero. For drivers bounded by one, the Loewner ODE
on a fixed neighborhood of each remote slit gives
$g_t=\operatorname{id}+O(t)$ there with all required derivatives,
uniformly over drivers. The image slits remain uniformly separated
from the driving interval and converge in this analytic
parameterization to the original slits. Hydrodynamic uniformization
and reflection give uniform convergence of the deletion-map jets
near $[-1,1]$. Decreasing $h_0$ preserves (TC2).

Put $u_\pm(t)=\int_0^{t\wedge\theta}(p_s^\pm)^2ds$, and let
$\widehat W^\pm$ be the corresponding deleted drivers in their own
capacities, frozen after $u_\pm(\theta)$ and centered at zero. Then
\[
 \widehat W^\pm_{u_\pm(t)}
       =h_{t\wedge\theta}^\pm(W_{t\wedge\theta})-h_0^\pm(0).
 \tag{TC3}
\]
The same physical prefix is retained throughout. Its reconstruction
from either driver identifies the stopped natural trace filtrations
and makes these increasing clocks stopping-time changes. The same
statements hold with any additional observable terminal prefix stop
included in $\theta$.

\begin{proposition}[Two remote signed energies regularize both characteristics]
\label{prop:two-slit-characteristic-control}
Let $1<p<\infty$. Suppose the two stopped own-capacity drivers have
continuous martingale decompositions
\[
 \widehat W^\pm_u=\widehat M^\pm_u+
    \int_0^{u\wedge u_\pm(\theta)}\widehat b^\pm_vdv,
 \qquad
 \mathbb E\int_0^{u_\pm(\theta)}|\widehat b^\pm_v|^pdv\le B.
 \tag{TC4}
\]
Then $X_t=W_{t\wedge\theta}$ is a continuous semimartingale with
absolutely continuous drift and bracket,
\[
 X_t=M_t+\int_0^{t\wedge\theta}b_sds,\qquad
 \langle M\rangle_t=\int_0^{t\wedge\theta}v_sds,\qquad
 \mathbb E\int_0^\theta\bigl(|b_s|^p+|v_s-6|^p\bigr)ds\le C_pB.
 \tag{TC5}
\]
For $0<h\le h_0$ and $0<\delta<1$ one consequently has
\[
 \mathbb P\{\sup_{t\le h}|X_t|>\delta\}
 \le C_p\delta^{-2}\{h+B^{1/p}h^{1-1/p}\}
          +C_p\delta^{-p}Bh^{p-1}.
 \tag{TC6}
\]
Constants depend only on the fixed slits, $h_0$, and $p$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Pull the first deleted driver back through its stopping-time clock.
It is a semimartingale in the original trace filtration. The inverse
analytic field $(h_t^+)^{-1}$ is adapted, absolutely continuous in
$t$, and has bounded required spatial derivatives on the localized
region. Its finite-variation random-field It\^o formula, applied to
(TC3), makes $X$ a continuous semimartingale. Denote its canonical
drift and bracket measures by $dA,dC$, without assuming their
absolute continuity.

For an analytic deletion the time derivative at the moving driving
point contributes $-3q_t^\pm dt$. Uniqueness of the canonical
decomposition and It\^o's formula therefore give, on the active
interval $[0,\theta]$,
\[
 p_t^\pm dA_t+\tfrac12q_t^\pm(dC_t-6dt)
       =(p_t^\pm)^2\widehat b^\pm_{u_\pm(t)}dt.
 \tag{TC7}
\]
The determinant in (TC2) makes this a uniformly invertible system
of two signed-measure equations. Thus $dA=b\,dt$, $dC=v\,dt$, and
\[
 |b_t|+|v_t-6|\le C\bigl(
       |\widehat b^+_{u_+(t)}|+|\widehat b^-_{u_-(t)}|\bigr).
\]
Changing variables with $m^2dt\le du_\pm\le P^2dt$ proves (TC5).
It also gives
$\mathbb E\langle M\rangle_h\le6h+C_pB^{1/p}h^{1-1/p}$.
The martingale is square integrable by this bracket bound. Doob's
inequality controls its supremum; H\"older's inequality gives
$\mathbb E\sup_{t\le h}|\int_0^{t\wedge\theta}b_sds|^p
\le C_pBh^{p-1}$. Split at $\delta/2$ to obtain (TC6).
\end{proof}

By Proposition~\ref{prop:signed-test-lp-drift}, condition (TC4) is
exactly $\mathcal J_{p,P^2h_0}(\operatorname{Law}(\widehat W^\pm))
\le B$, with the drivers frozen after their matched stops. No signed
energy of the original driver is assumed. Locality is relevant when
comparison laws are identified with the corresponding deleted-domain
members of an exploration family.

\subsection{Nonflat calibration prefixes exclude diverging anisotropy}

For the moment suppose the physical half-plane states
$D_1=\mathbb H,D_2=i\mathbb H$ are admissible, with source zero,
target infinity, and third normalization marks $1,i$. Rotate their
traces back to $\mathbb H$ and let $K^1,K^2$ be their filled
capacity-one hulls. If each is almost surely not contained in a line,
there are $R_0<\infty$, $0<\delta<1/8$, and $\eta>0$ such that
for $j=1,2$,
\[
 \mathbb P\left\{K^j\subset B_{R_0},\quad
   \inf_{|\beta|\le\sqrt2}\sup_{z\in K^j}
          |\operatorname{Re}z+\beta\operatorname{Im}z|
                         \ge4\delta\right\}\ge\eta.
 \tag{TC8}
\]
Indeed the supremum is continuous in $\beta$; its minimum on this
compact interval vanishes only for a hull contained in one of the
corresponding lines. Boundedness of the hulls and monotone choices
of $R_0,\delta$ prove (TC8).

The conditional lower oscillation bound supplies this nonflatness.
A positive-capacity straight-ray driver has form $c\sqrt t$.
On $[1/2,1]$, for $|c|\le C$, its oscillation on each of $n$ equal
intervals is $O(C/n)$, below the lower-tail threshold of order
$n^{-1/2}$ for large $n$. Conditioning successively bounds the
probability of all such small oscillations by $(1-p_0)^n$.
Let $n\to\infty$, then take a countable union over integer $C$.

\begin{theorem}[Model-independent coercivity of baseline slit energies]
\label{thm:baseline-affine-coercivity}
Assume the two calibration laws satisfy (TC8) and continuous affine
Loewner admissibility. Let $\mathcal A$ be any collection of
orientation-preserving determinant-one linear candidates. In each
candidate's transformed flat-state chart, normalized by the third
mark, suppose both remote slit comparisons satisfy (TC4), with the
same $p>1$ and $B<\infty$. Then the condition numbers of all
$A\in\mathcal A$ are uniformly bounded. Their normalized conformal
tensors lie in a compact ellipticity class. The bound depends only on
$p,B$, the fixed slits, and the calibration constants.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose $e_j\in\{1,i\}$ with $|Ae_j|\ge\|A\|/\sqrt2$. Its
normalized transformed chart is the half-plane self-map
\[
 B_A(z)=\frac{A(e_jz)}{Ae_j}=x+\beta y+icy,
 \quad z=x+iy,
 \qquad |\beta|\le\sqrt2,\quad \|B_A\|\le\sqrt2,
 \quad c=|Ae_j|^{-2}\le\frac2{\kappa(A)},
 \tag{TC9}
\]
where $\kappa(A)=\|A\|\|A^{-1}\|=\|A\|^2$ because $\det A=1$.
On the event in (TC8), $B_AK^j$ has horizontal extent at least
$4\delta$, radius at most $\sqrt2R_0$, and height at most $cR_0$.

A half-plane hull of radius at most $R$ and height at most $H\le R$
has $\operatorname{hcap}\le CRH$. The geometric capacity theorem
\cite[Theorem 1]{lln-capacity} bounds capacity by a constant times
the area of its tangent-disk union; that union lies in a rectangle
of width at most $2R+2H$ and height $2H$. Thus, on this event,
\[
 s_A(1)=\tfrac12\operatorname{hcap}(B_AK^j)
                     \le CR_0^2/\kappa(A).
 \tag{TC10}
\]
Choose $h_*\le h_0$ such that $2\sqrt{h_*}\le\delta$ and the
right side of (TC6), at threshold $\delta$, is smaller than $\eta$.
If $\kappa(A)\ge CR_0^2/h_*$, then $s_A(1)\le h_*$ on the
calibration event. The deterministic Loewner hull-radius estimate
\[
 \sup_{z\in B_AK^j}|z|
     \le\sup_{u\le s_A(1)}|W_u^A|+2\sqrt{s_A(1)}
\]
forces the driver to reach magnitude at least $3\delta$ by time
$h_*$. Its version stopped on exiting $[-1,1]$ therefore has
supremum greater than $\delta$. This occurs with probability at
least $\eta$, contradicting (TC6). Hence the stated condition-number
bound holds.
\end{proof}

This half-plane statement uses admissible unbounded states explicitly.
The original bounded-domain formulation does not silently supply them.
The next version uses only bounded physical calibration states.

\begin{corollary}[Coercivity using two bounded physical prefixes]
\label{cor:bounded-prefix-affine-coercivity}
Take two fixed bounded half-disks $D_j\subset e_j\mathbb H$,
$e_1=1,e_2=i$, with flat source zero, remote targets, and radii
larger than $2r>0$. Stop each physical exploration at its first
exit from $B_r(0)$, rotate back by $e_j^{-1}$, and fill relative
to the auxiliary half-plane $\mathbb H$; call the resulting hull
$K^j$. It has finite positive auxiliary capacity time $T_j$ and
a continuous auxiliary driver. The original bounded-state
conditional lower oscillation bound implies that $K^j$ is almost
surely not contained in a line, hence satisfies (TC8).

For each candidate $A$ form the transformed prefix in the auxiliary
chart $B_{A,j}(z)=A(e_jz)/(Ae_j)$, with terminal capacity $T_{A,j}$.
Extend its driver constantly after $T_{A,j}$ and use
\[
 \theta_{A,j}=h_0\wedge\inf\{t:|W_t^{A,j}|\ge1\}
                                     \wedge T_{A,j}.
 \tag{TC11}
\]
If the two corresponding slit-comparison drivers in each orientation,
frozen at $u_\pm(\theta_{A,j})$, satisfy (TC4) uniformly over $A$,
then the same condition-number bound follows. No unbounded ensemble
state or infinite capacity lifetime is required.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The initial actual chordal chart is analytic on the smaller source
half-disk and reflects across its flat boundary. Local analytic
Loewner comparison therefore gives continuous auxiliary half-plane
driving and a strictly increasing continuous clock through the stopped
prefix. Its auxiliary capacity is positive by strict hull growth and
finite by boundedness. Its radius is at most $r$: filling a path in
the convex source half-disk cannot add points outside that half-disk.

A line-supported prefix would be a straight ray segment initially.
Its image under the analytic actual chart is an analytic simple
slit, whose driver is continuously differentiable on every strictly
positive interior capacity interval. The lower oscillation bound
excludes a locally Lipschitz driver on any nontrivial interval:
partition a rational interval into $n$ pieces, apply the same
$(1-p_0)^n$ estimate, and take countable unions over rational intervals
and integer Lipschitz constants. This proves nonflatness and (TC8).

The terminal auxiliary time is observable in its driver filtration:
\[
 T_{A,j}=\inf\{u:\sup_{z\in K_u}|B_{A,j}^{-1}z|\ge r\}.
\]
Before the trace reaches the physical circle, its filled physical
hull stays in the convex source half-disk. After constant extension,
the artificial Loewner hulls retain the terminal hull, so the same
first-contact formula still holds. Deleted terminal stops are
observable by the inverse geometric reconstruction.

The deletion identities and (TC5)--(TC6) thus remain valid with the
additional terminal stop in (TC11), with the time measure active
only up to that stop. The constant extension has no further driver
movement. On the calibration event the thin-hull estimate now gives
$T_{A,j}\le CR_0^2/\kappa(A)$; the radius estimate at that terminal
time gives precisely the preceding contradiction.
\end{proof}

The auxiliary target infinity is a normalization of local hull
observables, not a newly asserted target of the bounded-state family.
These four energies are observables of two fixed stopped physical
laws in candidate-dependent auxiliary charts. They are not the
canonical drivers of four fixed physical deleted states. Locality can
identify suitable comparison laws when its hypotheses apply, but the
stated uniform signed bounds must themselves be proved.

\begin{corollary}[Polynomial properness of the calibration energy]
\label{cor:polynomial-affine-coercivity}
In the bounded-prefix setup put
\[
 \mathcal B_p(A)=\max_{j=1,2,\,\sigma=\pm}
   \mathcal J_{p,P^2h_0}
       (\operatorname{Law}(\widehat W^{A,j,\sigma})).
\]
There is a constant $C$ independent of $A$, with extended values
allowed, such that
\[
 \kappa(A)^{p-1}\le C\bigl(1+\mathcal B_p(A)\bigr).
 \tag{TC12}
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
For $B=\mathcal B_p(A)<\infty$ choose
$h_*=c(1+B)^{-1/(p-1)}$. A sufficiently small fixed $c>0$ ensures
$h_*\le h_0$, $2\sqrt{h_*}\le\delta$, and (TC6) is smaller than
$\eta$, since
\[
 B^{1/p}h_*^{1-1/p}\le c^{(p-1)/p},\qquad
 Bh_*^{p-1}\le c^{p-1}.
\]
The preceding argument gives $\kappa(A)\le CR_0^2/h_*$. Raising
to the power $p-1$ proves (TC12). Infinite $B$ is immediate.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Use in common-tensor selection and the remaining gap.}
An energy-bounded calibration class has compact closure in the
nondegenerate normalized tensor space. Its baseline class need not
itself be closed. One may apply the earlier lower-semicontinuity and
finite-intersection selection argument on its compact closure, provided
every finite collection of target tests has a candidate in this same
baseline class with the same prescribed envelopes. The coercivity
result does not prove that simultaneous signed feasibility. Its four
bounds are full energies, not four scalar constraints, and none is
claimed as a consequence of unsigned RSW alone.
